- 2026-11-23 (Run a JavaScript step whose script throws): What: a Run a JavaScript step whose script runs and throws, is stopped by a limit or is refused now fails its file, like every other step that errors. Before, `run_on` in `crates/engine/src/actions.rs` failed the file only when `run_javascript` returned an error (JavaScript off, document missing or read-only); a script that ran and threw came back as an outcome with `error` set, the step logged `Run a JavaScript: <error>` and the file was still written, so a half-done action was presented as a success (rule 15 of AGENTS.md). Now the outcome's `error` is returned as the step's error, so `run_on` returns `Run a JavaScript: <error>` for that file and writes nothing for it, the desktop run lists it under failed and `action_run` gives the file an `error` entry and no output; a script that runs cleanly is still written and logged as `Run a JavaScript`. Callers needed no change: `crates/ui-egui/src/workflows/actions_ui.rs` and `action_run` in `crates/automation/src/a11y/documents.rs` already treat any `run_on` error as a failed file and show its text. The parity entry `misc.action-wizard-run` loses the sentence added by #186 that excepted a throwing script, now says a script that throws or cannot run fails the file, and cites the two new tests. Why: #186 recorded this exception while auditing the entry; a review of it found that the exception was itself a defect, since the other step kinds fail the file and publish nothing, and nothing else in the action tells the person that the script stopped part way. Cost: none for a clean script; a script that throws now ends that file's run at once instead of going on to the later steps and the save. Tested: `a_javascript_step_that_throws_fails_the_file` in `crates/engine/src/tests.rs` (a script that sets a field runs cleanly and run_on returns a file; the same script followed by a throw makes run_on return an error that starts with `Run a JavaScript:` and holds the thrown text), and `action_run_fails_a_file_whose_script_throws_and_writes_one_whose_script_runs` in `crates/automation/tests/it/action_failures.rs` (through the tool: the throwing file has an error entry holding the thrown text, no `output` and an empty output folder; a clean script gives an `output` path, no error and one file in the folder); `an_action_runs_a_javascript_step_only_when_javascript_is_on` still passes. Mutations: 5 were run on the new code. Killed: the error ignored again and only logged (M1: by the engine test and by the automation test); the step failing even for a clean script (M2: by both engine tests; the automation run of it did not link, see Not mutated); the error message emptied (M3: by the engine test); the failure to run the script ignored (M4: by the existing JavaScript-off test); the error returned only when the outcome's `rc` is false (M5: by the engine test). Not mutated: M2 to M5 were run against the engine tests only: the automation test binary did not link on those runs because the disk was nearly full (`ld` bus error or `ld returned 1`), so their automation half was not run; only M1 was run against both. The wording of the logged lines and the parity text. Not tested: the desktop Action Wizard run of a file whose script throws (no desktop test asserts the failed list; the caller was read, not run); a script that throws after changing a field in a document with several steps after it; that each step kind other than the watermark, removal and script steps fails the file when it errors; a script that never ends or that is stopped by a time limit; Windows and macOS (only Linux was run). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (no -A, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-engine` (all passed: 908 library tests, 2 of them ignored, and 9, 2 and 61 in the other targets, 2 of them ignored) and `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (94 library tests passed; 800 integration tests passed and 1 failed; 2 in another target passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: tests of crates other than `pdfcraft-engine` and `pdfcraft-automation` (`pdfcraft-ui-egui`, which holds the desktop caller, and `pdfcraft-cli` were not built or tested), `cargo xtask wasm`, `cargo xtask i18n-report`, the workspace sweep, a hand check through `pdfcraft-cli` or the desktop app, and the automation half of mutations M2 to M5. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-23 (parity audit, no code): What: three parity entries were checked against the code and the tests that exist, and two changed; no code changed. `organize.page-range-selection` stays partial: it now also cites the range parser's unit test (`selection_ranges_are_sorted_unique_and_bounded`), and its notes say what exists (in the app only, not headless: the Organize panel's Pages selected field takes typed pages and ranges, and its menu picks all, even, odd, landscape, portrait or none, one criterion at a time, so odd and landscape cannot be combined there; the UI control channel's `select` view option sets the same selection from the same range parser; there is no `pdfcraft-cli run` or MCP select tool, the page tools take a `pages` list, and among the page tools only `page_rotate` also takes subset and orientation (`doc_print` takes a subset for printing)) and what is not (a headless select tool, subset or orientation on other page tools, a test of the menu choices themselves). It also cites `organize_page_range_tracks_thumbnails_and_applies_typed_selection`, which types a range in the field, checks the field follows thumbnail clicks, and applies a rotation to exactly the selected pages. `misc.action-wizard-run` stays partial, but its note "Known gap: a failing non-removal step still publishes its output as success" was no longer true and is removed: `run_on` in `crates/engine/src/actions.rs` returns the error of any step that errors, so nothing is written for that file and the desktop run lists it under failed; only a step that finds nothing to do is logged and passed. The exception is a Run a JavaScript step whose script runs and throws: `run_javascript` returns the error inside its outcome, the step logs `Run a JavaScript: <error>` and the file is still written; the step fails the file only when the script cannot run at all (JavaScript off, document missing or read-only). The notes say that, name the twelve step kinds and five built-in actions as the limit, and the entry cites the tests in which a failing step fails the file: `actions_fail_when_hidden_information_could_not_be_removed` and `an_action_runs_a_javascript_step_only_when_javascript_is_on` in the engine tests, and `failing_watermark_step_fails_the_file_and_publishes_nothing` and `action_run_marks_the_failing_file_as_error_without_output` in `crates/automation/tests/it/action_failures.rs` (a watermark step that cannot draw its text fails the file, and through `action_run` the file gets an error entry and no output). `organize.associated-files` is unchanged: it is planned with no tool and no evidence, and its note already says it duplicates `core.pdf20-associated-files` (partial, crediting `af_list`, `af_add`, `af_remove` and `af_set_relationship`) and that merging or deleting it is left to the owner; planned is the weaker status and the note says nothing false, so it was left. ROADMAP.md and docs/gaps.md say nothing about these three entries that contradicts them (the stale action-wizard gap appeared only in the parity file). Why: rule 15 of AGENTS.md: an entry must not say more or less than what ships. The action-wizard note told readers of the parity file that a failed step is reported as success, which the code and its tests show is not so; the page-range note was one line that did not say that the selection exists only in the desktop panel. Cost: none at run time; one parity file, one ROADMAP line and one gaps.md header line and row. Tested: `cargo xtask parity` checks that every evidence path names an existing test, which the two new evidence entries on `organize.page-range-selection` and the two on `misc.action-wizard-run` pass; no behaviour was tested or changed, and the claims in the notes were read from `crates/engine/src/actions.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/workflows/actions_ui.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/organize_panel.rs`, `crates/ui-egui/src/panels/page_selection_ui.rs` and `crates/automation/src/dispatch.rs`, not run. Mutations: not applicable, docs and data only; no code was changed, so none were run. Not mutated: everything, for the same reason. Not tested: the desktop Action Wizard run of a file whose step fails (no desktop test asserts the failed list); that each step kind other than the watermark, removal and script steps fails the file when it errors (the removal and script tests do not reach the apply-error path of the other steps; the watermark tests do); a script that runs and throws in a Run a JavaScript step (its outcome is logged and the file is written, no test pins that); the Organize panel's menu choices through the UI; any claim about what Acrobat's Action Wizard or page selection offers beyond the three entries' own titles. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask layers`. Failed: none. Not run: `cargo clippy`, `cargo test` and the mutation run (no Rust code changed), `cargo xtask wasm`, `cargo xtask i18n-report`. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-23 (M9, signing hand-off package, headless): What: a new pair of headless tools hands a document and its local signing plan to someone else as one zip. `signing_handoff_export` takes `doc` and `path` (a new file ending in .zip, inside the write root) and writes, atomically and without replacing anything (an existing file at `path` is refused and left alone), one zip with exactly three entries: `document.pdf` (the PDF as it is on disk, byte for byte), `plan.json` (the plan beside it, as the plan file would hold it) and `manifest.json` (a format name, format version 1, the PDF's file name, its SHA-256 and size, the SHA-256 of `plan.json`, and the plan's state and recipients with name, email, role, order and status). The output path is checked first, so a path outside the roots is refused before the document is looked at. Refused with nothing written: a document with no file, with unsaved changes, that opening changed (a repair or a form layout), whose file on disk is no longer byte for byte the file it was opened from (the same check `signing_plan_sign` makes), or with no plan beside it; an output that does not end in .zip, exists, or is outside the write root; a PDF whose name is not a plain name, and a zip name that is only an extension (`.zip`, `dir/.zip`; the name is taken with `Path::file_name`). `signing_handoff_import` takes `path` (the zip, inside the read root) and `folder` (inside the write root, made when missing) and, before writing anything, checks that the archive holds exactly manifest.json, document.pdf and plan.json once each (a zip-slip name such as `../x`, an absolute or drive name, a folder entry, a duplicate or a fourth entry is refused), that no entry expands past its cap (manifest 64 KiB, plan 1 MiB, PDF 512 MiB, at most three entries, counted on the bytes that really come out so a small archive that expands hugely is refused), that the manifest is of this format and version 1 (another version is refused) and that its file name is one plain name ending in .pdf (no folder part, drive, `..`, leading dot, control, hidden or direction-changing character, and no Windows device name such as `CON`, `NUL` or `COM1`, with or without an extension, by the same `is_device_name` the roots use, now `pub(crate)`), all three checked right after the manifest is read and before the plan or the PDF is inflated, then that the PDF starts with %PDF- and its size and SHA-256 are the manifest's, that the plan file's SHA-256 is the manifest's (so a plan whose message, history or names were changed is refused even when it is still a valid plan), that the plan is a valid plan (the plan reader's own checks) and that the manifest's description of the plan equals the plan's. Only then are the PDF and the plan written side by side in `folder` under the names the plan expects (`<name>.pdf` and `<name>.pdf.pdfcraft-sign.json`), each published atomically without replacing a file (`bates::publish_new`, so a file another process creates in the moment before is not replaced either); if either name already exists the call is refused before anything is written, and if the second file cannot be written the first, made by this call, is removed. The result is the plan as `signing_plan_get` reads it (so `next` is the next recipient), the written paths, the hash and size, `hash_checked` true and `signatures_checked` false. Every result says `sent` false, `notified` false and `local_record_only` true and that the package is not signed or encrypted (the hashes (of the PDF and of the plan) catch damage or a change to either, not someone who changes a file and the manifest together). The package holds no secret: a plan has none and a digital ID or password is never read. Both tools are marked destructive (not read-only) and described as such. Zip reading and writing is `craft-zip`, already a workspace dependency of `pdfcraft-export` and on the licence allowlist; `pdfcraft-automation` now lists it too (one new edge in Cargo.lock, no new package). `signing_plan.rs` gained `plan_from_bytes` and `plan_bytes`, taken out of `read_plan_sidecar` and `save` with their behaviour unchanged (the sidecar's refusals still end `it was left alone`; the package's plan ends `nothing was written`, as `plan_from_bytes` now takes that ending as a parameter), and the new code is its child module `signing_plan/handoff.rs`. Parity: `sign.handoff-package` is partial (headless, local plan only); the notes of `sign.sequential-signing` no longer say there are no hand-off packages. Import does not yet validate the signatures in the PDF or check that nothing outside the recipient's fields changed, as `docs/esign-design.md` (Local design, item 4) hopes; it says so in every result. Why: `docs/esign-design.md` (Local design, item 4) wants a plan plus PDF to travel as one file by mail or a shared folder, and the pieces are merged (plan, field labels, `signing_plan_sign`); this is the smallest step that moves a half-signed document and its plan together without trusting the receiver to copy two files and name them right, and it is testable with the validator as the oracle. No network, provider or server code is added. Cost: export reads the PDF from disk once and compares it with the open document's saved bytes, and holds the PDF, its deflated copy and the zip in memory (up to the 512 MiB input cap, as every tool that reads a PDF does); import reads the zip (the same cap), inflates each entry within its cap, hashes the PDF once and writes two files; an export refused early costs only the reads that found it; an import reads the whole zip, then inflates only the manifest and checks its format, version and file name before it inflates the plan and the PDF, so a package of another version or with a bad file name costs the manifest alone. Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/signing_handoff.rs`, eleven tests with made-up people at example.org and self-signed P-256 test IDs: `a_package_round_trips_the_pdf_bytes_and_the_plan_and_holds_nothing_else` (the package holds exactly the three entries, the manifest has only its documented keys, the password, the ID file name and the words key and password are in no entry but the PDF, the imported PDF and plan files are byte for byte the originals, the next recipient is Ana, `signatures_checked` and `sent` are false, `signing_plan_get` reads the same plan in both places, and the manifest carries the plan's hash), `a_signed_document_round_trips_still_validates_and_the_next_recipient_can_sign_there` (Ana signs with `signing_plan_sign`, the package is exported and imported, the imported file equals the signed file, `sign_list` reads the same statuses for it and Ana's is not invalid, the plan says Bo is next, Bo signs the imported copy, Ana's status is unchanged and only the imported copy took Bo's turn), `a_pdf_that_does_not_match_the_manifest_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written` (one byte flipped with the length kept, a wrong size in the manifest, and bytes that match their manifest but are not a PDF: each refused, the folder snapshot unchanged, no destination folder), `a_manifest_of_another_version_or_with_a_plan_that_disagrees_is_refused` (version 2 and 0, another format name, an unknown manifest key, a manifest whose plan statuses differ from the plan, and a plan that is not a valid plan whose hash the manifest carries, refused as not a signing plan with the ending `nothing was written` and not `left alone`: each refused and nothing made), `paths_that_climb_out_of_the_folder_are_refused_in_the_archive_and_in_the_manifest` (seven zip-slip, absolute, drive and folder entry names in place of the PDF in an archive of exactly three entries, so that the name list refuses each and the test asserts its wording `is not part of a hand-off package`; an archive that names `manifest.json` twice (a third entry, `manifest.jsoX`, byte-replaced to the same length) refused with `twice`; and fifteen manifest file names such as `../evil.pdf`, `sub/evil.pdf`, `C:evil.pdf`, `CON.pdf`, `nul.PDF`, `com1.pdf`, `con .pdf`, a right-to-left override, a NUL and an empty name: each refused, nothing written inside or outside the folder), `an_archive_that_is_too_big_or_has_too_many_entries_is_refused` (a 4 MiB manifest and a 4 MiB plan that deflate to a few kilobytes, an archive of five entries and a file that is not a zip: each refused with the folder unchanged and with its own reason asserted: `larger than` for the two expansions, `entries, more than 3` for the five entries, `not a ZIP archive` for the junk file, and none of them with the name-list wording), `an_existing_file_in_the_destination_is_never_overwritten_and_nothing_else_is_written` (an existing PDF, and separately an existing plan, in the destination: refused, the existing file and the whole tree unchanged; a second import into one folder refused; a file where the folder should be refused), `an_export_is_refused_without_a_saved_unchanged_document_a_plan_or_a_new_zip_name` (an output not ending in .zip, a bare `.zip`, `sub/.zip`, a path through a file, an existing output left alone, unsaved changes, a document with no file, no plan: each leaves no zip), `a_stale_copy_of_the_document_is_not_exported` (another copy signs, the first copy is refused with the on-disk sentence and nothing is written, the copy that signed exports) `a_plan_changed_inside_the_package_is_refused_even_when_it_is_still_a_valid_plan` (plan.json with a changed message, a changed history or a changed recipient name, each still a valid plan and the manifest unchanged: refused with the plan-hash sentence, the folder unchanged) and `paths_outside_the_roots_are_refused_and_nothing_is_created` (separate read and write roots and a folder outside both: exports to the outside, to the read root and through `..` are refused; imports from a zip in the write root or outside the read root, and into the read root, outside and through `..` are refused; none of the three folders changed; the allowed import works); `file_writing_tools_are_not_read_only` lists both tools; the hostile-input sweep `no_tool_panics_on_hostile_arguments` has a fixture for each (a saved document with a plan, and a package made from it), runs each once unchanged and asserts that the export wrote its zip and the import both files, and `no_tool_escapes_the_root` still passes with no allowlist entry and now reaches both tools' path arguments for real (the export checks its output path before the document). Mutations: 25 were run on the new code, each with the new tests and the hostile and file-writing tests: 24 killed and 1 survived. Killed: the hash comparison disabled, the size comparison disabled, the version check disabled, the manifest format check disabled, the plain-name check disabled, the leading-dot rule removed from it, the path separators removed from its character list, the Windows device-name refusal removed from it, the existing-destination refusal disabled, the manifest cap raised to 64 MiB, the plan cap raised to 64 MiB, the PDF header check disabled, the manifest-describes-the-plan check disabled, the plan-hash check disabled, the entry-name allowlist disabled, the entry limit raised from 3 to 300, the duplicate-name refusal disabled, the unsaved-changes refusal of export disabled, the on-disk equality check of export disabled, the existing-output refusal of export disabled, the .zip-name check of export disabled (it is now taken from the file name), the zip-name-is-only-an-extension check disabled, the export output checked against the read root instead of the write root, and the import folder checked against the read root instead of the write root (9, most of the suite); each by one or more tests (the three run again after the manifest was moved ahead of the plan and the PDF were killed again). Survived: the changed-on-open refusal of export disabled: a document that opening changed also fails the disk-equality check, so the refusal is redundant with it and exists for a clearer message; no test makes opening change a file to show its own sentence. Not mutated: the order of the reads in import (that the manifest is checked before the plan and the PDF are inflated: no test can see which read happened first), the words of the refusals and results, the description and parity text, the `Manifest` field names, the removal of the first file when the second cannot be written, the order of the checks inside import after the manifest is read, the 200-byte name limit, the `plan_summary` content beyond what the manifest comparison reads, and the check that the export's source file is inside the read root. Not tested: the removal of the PDF when the plan cannot be written (nothing makes the second write fail after the first succeeds as root); an archive with a duplicate entry name; a zip that expands to more than the 512 MiB PDF cap (the caps are tested through the manifest and plan entries only, which share the code path); a file that appears in the destination between the check and the write (`persist_noclobber` refuses it, but no test races it); an export whose source file is outside the read root; a PDF whose name has a hidden character (the manifest names are tested, the export side uses the same check); empty destination folders this call created may be left behind when a later write fails (not cleaned up, not tested); Windows and macOS (only Linux was run; the file modes of written files are those of a temporary file, owner only, as `bates::publish_new` makes them); that a person who changes a file and the manifest together is detected (it is not: the package is not signed); that the imported signatures validate or that nothing outside the recipient's fields changed (not done by import; the signed round trip checks the validator reads the same statuses); a hand check of the tools through `pdfcraft-cli` and an MCP client; no screen, no sending and no server exist to test. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p <crate> --all-targets -- -D warnings` for `pdfcraft-engine`, `pdfcraft-automation` and `pdfcraft-cli`, one crate at a time (no `-A`, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (94 library tests passed; 799 integration tests passed and 1 failed; 2 in another target passed), `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-cli` (all passed: 5 and 103 tests in its two targets) and `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-engine` (all passed: 907 library tests, 2 of them ignored, and 9, 2 and 61 in the other targets, 2 of them ignored); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: tests of crates other than `pdfcraft-automation`, `pdfcraft-cli` and `pdfcraft-engine` (`pdfcraft-ui-egui` was built only by `cargo xtask wasm`, its tests were not run), `cargo xtask i18n-report`, the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), and a hand check of the tool through `pdfcraft-cli`. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-23 (M9, sequential signing, headless): What: a new headless tool, `signing_plan_sign`, performs the next signer's turn of the local signing plan beside the document's file in one step. Arguments: `doc`, `email` (the recipient, compared with the plan's addresses without regard to case), `id` and `password` (a digital ID exactly as `sign_document` takes it: a .p12/.pfx path, `keychain:` or `windows:`), optionally `field`, and `reason`, `location`, `contact`, `appearance`, `labels` and `graphic`, which mean what they mean in `sign_document`; `certify`, `page`, `rect`, `out` and `run_form_scripts` are not offered (the schema refuses them). In order: (1) the plan beside the document's own file is read (a document with no file, with unsaved changes, or with no plan is refused; the signed file replaces the saved one, so unsaved changes are refused instead of being swallowed; and the file on disk must still be byte for byte the file the open document was read from, checked before signing and again just before the write after the plan is read again, within one session (a write by another program in the moment before the rename is not detected), otherwise the call is refused with `the file changed on disk since it was opened; reopen it`, so a stale second copy of the document cannot overwrite an earlier recipient's signature; a document that opening changed (a repair or a form layout) cannot be compared and is refused with a sentence saying to save and reopen it; the plan is also read again right before the write and the turn re-checked) and the recipient must be the plan's next in order and a signer: an address not in the plan, a viewer, someone else's turn, a finished plan and an approver are each refused with a sentence (an approver's turn is recorded with `signing_plan_advance` status `approved`; there is no `approve` mode here, by choice). The plan's own turn check is the one `signing_plan_advance` uses (`Plan::turn_of`, and `Plan::record` for writing the step, both taken out of `signing_plan_advance` with its behaviour unchanged). (2) The signature field is the one unsigned signature field whose `/PdfCraftSigner` label (`form_field_signer`) names the recipient, compared without regard to case; none, or several, is refused with a sentence that says what to do (several are listed, at most 10), unless `field` names the field to sign: a field with preSign or postSign scripts is refused with a sentence pointing at `sign_document` and `signing_plan_advance` (this tool never runs form scripts); a named field must be a signature field, with a name no other field has, that holds no signature and is not labelled for another recipient (an unlabelled one is allowed, because the caller chose it). (3) The signing is `sign_document`'s own code: the body of `sign_document` up to the signed bytes is now `Automation::sign_bytes` (returning the signed file and what the writing needs) and `sign_document` calls it and then writes as before, so nothing is copied; the document is signed as an incremental update, so the bytes of earlier signatures stay in front. (4) Before anything is written the validator reads the signed bytes (`Session::check_signing` in the engine, which opens them without touching the open document and validates them against the open document's trust): every signature the document holds must still be there and not worse (its status, changes it now forbids, changes that can still be checked), which is the check `signature_damage` makes for a signer label, now with its words chosen by the change (`signature_damage_by`; the sentences for a label are byte for byte as they were), and the new signature must be present and not invalid; for a signature that already had forbidden changes before, the new signing also must not add a kind of forbidden change it did not have (a label keeps its older, looser rule). A field locked by an earlier signature's lock (`signature_field_lock`) and a certification that allows no changes are refused here, naming the earlier signature; the refusal is `the document was not signed`, and the document, its file and the plan are untouched, so there is nothing to roll back (the signed bytes are never adopted until the check passes). (5) Then the signed file is written over the document's own file atomically, the document adopts it (like `sign_document`: the file is saved, the document shows the signed file and the undo history is dropped, because a signature cannot be undone; a call refused for not being the recipient's turn keeps the history and adds no step), and only then is the plan read again, the recipient checked to still be next, and the step written (`signed`, atomically, through the plan's own writer). If the plan cannot be used or written after the document was signed, the result is not `complete`: `status` is `partial`, `ok` false, `needs_attention` true, `signed` true, `plan_advanced` false, with the reason and a warning that says which cause it was (a write that failed: the plan still shows the recipient as next; a plan that is gone or was changed or finished by someone else: this call did not change it, look at it with `signing_plan_get`) and that the step can be recorded with `signing_plan_advance` if it is still the recipient's turn, and not to sign again. Over MCP a partial result is also marked `isError` (`tool_content` in `mcp.rs`, as for `command_batch`), and `pdfcraft-cli run` fails on it (`apps/pdfcraft-cli/src/run.rs`, as `batch` does for failed files: a single tool exits non-zero, a `--script` stops at that step, a `--report` marks the step `error`), each with the JSON body kept. If the open document cannot adopt the signed file after it was written, the plan is still updated (the file on disk is signed) and the result is `partial` with `plan_advanced` true and a warning to reopen the file. The tool is marked destructive (it replaces the document's file) and the description says that signing uses a person's credential (use only a digital ID and password the person gave you for this signing, ask before each call) and gives `password` its own description; a refusal after a crash between the save and the plan write points at `signing_plan_advance`. A `complete` result has the new signature as `sign_list` reads it, the plan as `signing_plan_get` reads it, and the signature counts before and now. Every result says `identity_checked` false (the plan does not verify who holds the digital ID: the signer certificate is not compared with the recipient's address, so whoever supplies an ID signs as the recipient; the description says so), `sent` false, `notified` false and `local_record_only` true. `sign_as_recipient` takes the plan writer as a parameter (the real one is passed by the tool) so that a test can make the write fail after the document is signed. Parity: `sign.sequential-signing` is partial (headless, local plan only, no screen, no hand-off packages). Why: `docs/esign-design.md` (Local design, items 3 and 7) needs a recipient to sign their turn and the plan to advance without trusting the caller to do both in the right order; with the pieces already merged (plan, field labels, `signature_field_lock`, the PAdES path) this is the smallest step that does it and is testable with the validator as the oracle. Cost: the file on disk is read twice (before signing and before the write) and compared with the saved bytes of the open document; the signed file is read back and its signatures validated once more before it is written (the signature validation an open or an edit does; the save checks of `check_save` are not run on it); the plan is read three times (before signing, before the write and after); an early refusal costs only the reads that found it. Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/signing_plan_sign.rs`, sixteen tests with made-up people at example.org and self-signed P-256 test IDs: `two_recipients_sign_in_order_and_the_first_signature_stays_intact` (the stored label has an upper-case letter and the call spells the address in capitals; Ana then Bo: each file keeps the earlier bytes in front, Ana's status after Bo's signature equals her status alone, Bo's is not invalid, a fresh open of the saved file reads two signatures and says Ana's covers changes_allowed, the plan lists both signed and the approver next), `the_plan_does_not_check_who_holds_the_id_and_the_result_says_so` (Ana's turn signed with Bo's ID succeeds, `identity_checked` false, `sent` false, the signer is Bo), `a_turn_out_of_order_or_not_a_signers_is_refused_and_nothing_changes` (Bo first, the approver, the viewer, an unknown address, an approver's turn after both signed, a repeat: each leaves the file, the plan and every status as they were), `a_refused_call_keeps_the_undo_history_and_a_signing_drops_it`, `no_plan_unsaved_changes_extra_arguments_and_a_document_without_a_file_are_refused` (also that the schema refuses `certify`, `out`, `page`, `rect` and `run_form_scripts`), `a_finished_plan_refuses_a_further_turn` (both signed and the approver done: a further turn is refused), `the_field_is_the_one_labelled_for_the_recipient_or_the_one_the_caller_names` (unlabelled refused, a field labelled for another refused, a text field and an unknown name refused, a named own field signed), `a_named_unlabelled_field_is_signed_and_the_labelled_one_is_left_alone`, `two_unsigned_fields_for_one_recipient_need_the_caller_to_choose`, `a_recipient_whose_only_field_already_holds_a_signature_is_refused`, `a_field_locked_by_an_earlier_signature_is_refused_by_the_validator_and_the_plan_stays_put` (Ana's lock names Bo's field: refused naming her signature, file, plan and statuses unchanged, the plan still has Bo next; a field the lock does not name signs), `a_certification_that_allows_no_changes_refuses_the_next_signer` (file, plan and every status unchanged), `a_plan_that_is_not_a_plan_or_an_id_that_does_not_open_refuses_before_any_write` (a missing ID names the file, a wrong password and a bad plan file are refused, each with the file, plan and statuses snapshot unchanged), `a_stale_copy_of_the_document_cannot_overwrite_an_earlier_signature` (two document ids on one file: Ana signs through one, Bo through the stale other: refused with the on-disk sentence, the file is Ana's signed file byte for byte, the plan unchanged; a copy opened afterwards signs), `a_field_with_signing_scripts_is_refused_and_nothing_changes` (an XFA box with signing scripts: refused, file and plan unchanged) and `a_field_named_with_a_dot_is_found_labelled_and_signed` (a field `Party.Cy`: the form's name and the validator's name agree); three unit tests in `crates/automation/src/signing_plan.rs`, `a_plan_that_cannot_be_written_after_signing_says_the_document_was_signed` (a plan writer that fails: status partial, `ok` false and `needs_attention` true, the warning says the plan still shows Ana as next, the plan writer was called once and only when the saved file already held the signature, the document is signed, the plan file is as it was, Ana is still next, and the result names the recipient), `the_plan_is_not_written_when_the_signature_is_refused` (the writer is never called for a turn refused or a field not found) and `the_plan_is_not_written_for_a_signature_the_validator_refuses` (Ana's lock names Bo's field: Bo's turn is refused and the writer was called once, for Ana only); in `crates/automation/src/mcp.rs` `a_partial_signing_is_reported_as_an_error_and_a_complete_one_is_not`; in the engine `a_signing_may_not_add_a_kind_of_forbidden_change_to_a_signature_that_already_had_one`; in the CLI crate `a_result_that_needs_attention_fails_the_run_and_an_ordinary_one_does_not` (`print_output` fails for `needs_attention` true or `ok` false with status `partial`, after printing the body, and not for an ordinary result); `file_writing_tools_are_not_read_only` lists the tool; the hostile-input panic sweep `no_tool_panics_on_hostile_arguments` has a fixture for the tool (a saved document with a labelled signature field, a plan and an ID that opens) and asserts that some call signed; `no_tool_escapes_the_root` still passes with no allowlist entry, but it is not claimed to cover this tool's `id` or `graphic` (that sweep gives the tool an unsaved blank document, so it is refused before it reaches them). Mutations: 17 were run on the new code, each with the new tests: 14 killed and 3 survived. Killed: the turn check replaced by a plain lookup (2 tests), several labelled fields resolved by taking the first (1), the validator check after signing removed (2: the lock and the certification), the check moved after the write so a refusal leaves a written file (1: the lock test, which compares the file), the plan written before the document is saved (2 unit tests and 7 integration tests), the partial status reported as `complete` (1 unit test), the approver refusal removed (1), the unsaved-changes refusal removed (1), the label compared without lower-casing (11), the on-disk equality check made always true (1: the stale-copy test), the signing-scripts refusal disabled (1), the partial result not marked `isError` over MCP (1 unit test), the CLI's needs-attention check disabled (1 CLI unit test), and the engine's forbidden-kinds comparison for a new signing disabled (1 engine unit test). Survived: the check that the new signature is not invalid, disabled (no test can make the validator read a freshly made signature as invalid); the second turn check when the step is written after signing, replaced by a plain lookup, and the turn re-check right before the write, removed (nothing can change the plan between the reads of one call, so neither can be reached by a test). Not mutated: the words of the refusals and results, the description and parity text, the checks that a named field is a signature field with a unique name, the `signed_names` term of the unsigned-field test, the 10-name cap of the several-fields sentence, the engine's `Signing` wording of `signature_damage_by`, the changed-on-open refusal, and the sentences by cause of a partial result. Not tested: a plan write that really fails on the disk (the partial status is tested through the plan writer parameter; as root a read-only folder does not stop a write, and a folder in place of the sidecar is refused when the plan is read first, before signing); the open document failing to adopt the signed file after it was written (a partial result with the plan updated, not exercised); a plan that is gone or changed between the checks and the write (the plan-gone and plan-changed warnings are not exercised); an interrupted call between the save and the plan write (only the refusal sentence that points at `signing_plan_advance` is asserted by what a signed field says, not a real crash); a file changed on disk between the second check and the rename (the window is small and not tested); the changed-on-open refusal; a name two fields share, through this tool; `graphic`, `appearance` and `labels` through this tool (they are passed to `sign_document`'s code unchanged); Keychain and Windows store IDs; an encrypted document, a dynamic form, a file name ending in `.PDF`; the roots refusing the sidecar for write through this tool (the same check as the other plan tools, not asserted here); a plan file that goes bad between the two reads of one call; a new signature the validator reads as invalid; Windows and macOS (only Linux was run); no screen and no hand-off package exist to test. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p <crate> --all-targets -- -D warnings` for `pdfcraft-engine`, `pdfcraft-automation` and `pdfcraft-cli`, one crate at a time (no `-A`, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (94 library tests passed; 788 integration tests passed and 1 failed; 2 in another target passed) `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-cli` (all passed: 5 and 103 tests in its two targets) and `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-engine` (all passed: 907 library tests, and 9, 2 and 61 in the other targets); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: tests of crates other than `pdfcraft-automation`, `pdfcraft-cli` and `pdfcraft-engine` (`pdfcraft-sign` and `pdfcraft-organize` were not changed; `pdfcraft-ui-egui` was built only by `cargo xtask wasm`, its tests were not run), `cargo xtask i18n-report`, the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), and a hand check of the tool through `pdfcraft-cli`. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-22 (M8, sanitize, signer label): What: removing private data without also removing form fields (the Remove Hidden Information panel, `doc_remove_hidden` with a `categories` list that lacks `form-fields`, and the optimizer's discard of private data, `doc_optimize` `discard`) no longer keeps the form field signer label `/PdfCraftSigner` (a recipient's email address, written by `form_field_signer`): it is removed from every field, widget kid and nested field dictionary the owner sweep reaches. Whole-document Sanitize Document already dropped the label, because removing form fields flattens the fields, drops their widgets and removes `/AcroForm`, and the full rewrite writes only reachable objects; it now also counts the label under private data. `OWNED` in `crates/redact/src/sanitize.rs` gained one entry, `(Hidden::PrivateData, SIGNER_LABEL_KEY)`; the key is repeated in `pdfcraft-redact` as `SIGNER_LABEL_KEY` (re-exported by the engine as `SANITIZED_SIGNER_LABEL_KEY`) because redact and organize are siblings and neither may depend on the other, and a test in the automation crate asserts it equals `FIELD_SIGNER_KEY`. Each dictionary that held a label adds 1 to the existing private-data count (`doc_hidden_info`, `removed` of `doc_remove_hidden`); there is no separate count. A full Sanitize counts a labelled field twice in the summed `removed` total (once under form-fields, once under private-data), because that total is the sum before minus the sum after. The sweep strips the key from any dictionary it reaches outside name maps, not only from dictionaries that are form fields, and no other category removes it (removing comments alone leaves it). The English choice text now says what the category holds: `Hidden::label` is `Private data (other applications' data and PdfCraft signer labels)` and the optimizer's checkbox `Discard private data (other applications' data and PdfCraft signer labels)` (the old English text is a translation key, so other languages show English for them until craft-i18n catches up); the `doc_hidden_info`, `doc_optimize` and `form_field_signer` descriptions, the parity notes and the redact README say it too. A signed document is refused exactly as for every sanitize and for the optimizer (both need a full rewrite), so its label stays; this is tested for `doc_remove_hidden`. Why: PR #181 added the label, but a person who removed private data without removing form fields, or who discarded it in the optimizer, still sent every recipient's address in the file. Cost: one more key in the owner sweep's existing single walk; nothing extra for a file with no label. Tested: `private_data_removes_signer_labels_from_fields_widgets_and_nested_fields` (redact: a plain field that is its own widget, a parent field with a widget kid, a nested field with its own widget kid: five labels counted and removed, fields, names and other keys kept), `a_form_without_signer_labels_is_not_touched_by_private_data` (count 0, refused as nothing to remove, field dictionary unchanged), `sanitize_leaves_no_signer_label_in_the_saved_file`, and in the automation crate `private_data_removed_with_other_categories_but_not_form_fields_leaves_no_label_in_the_file` (`doc_remove_hidden` with `private-data` and `metadata`, saved: no label bytes; fails on the code before this change), `the_optimizers_private_data_discard_leaves_no_label_in_the_copy` (`doc_optimize` with and without `discard` `private-data`; the open document is unchanged), `removing_private_data_takes_the_label_out_counts_it_and_can_be_undone` (count, another category leaves it, saved file has none, fields stay, undo restores both labels, Sanitize document removes them), `a_signed_document_is_not_sanitized_and_keeps_its_label` and `the_sanitizers_copy_of_the_key_is_the_one_the_label_is_written_under`. Mutations: 4 were run on the redact change and checked with the automation and redact tests: the entry removed (killed, by 4 automation tests including both new ones), the entry filed under comments (killed, by the same 4), filed under metadata (killed by 3 automation tests; the `private-data` plus `metadata` test passes by design because metadata is in its list), the key text changed (killed, by 2 redact tests). Not mutated: the descriptions and docs text and the English choice text. Not tested: the signed-document refusal through `doc_optimize` (the same `is_signed` check, read, not run); a label on a dictionary that is not a field (it is removed too, by design, and not asserted); the GUI dialog and the optimizer's checkbox; a file whose label sits deeper than the sweep follows (refused as for any private data); Windows and macOS (only Linux was run). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p <crate> --all-targets -- -D warnings` for `pdfcraft-redact`, `pdfcraft-engine`, `pdfcraft-automation` and `pdfcraft-ui-egui`, one crate at a time (no `-A`, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on `pdfcraft-redact`, `pdfcraft-engine` and `pdfcraft-automation` (all passed except the first failure below) and `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-ui-egui --lib` (1114 passed, 1 failed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root), and `canvas::tests::a_moved_picture_is_drawn_from_the_fresh_raster_while_a_tile_is_still_stale` in `pdfcraft-ui-egui` (no GPU adapter in this environment: `No adapter found`). Not run: the user interface integration tests (`pdfcraft-ui-egui` `it`, which need a GPU), tests of other crates, `cargo xtask i18n-report`, and the workspace sweep. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-22 (automation, Windows path display): What: error messages that name a path no longer show Windows' extended-length form. A new display helper `shown` in `crates/automation/src/paths.rs` drops a `\\?\` prefix from a drive path (`\\?\C:\x` shows as `C:\x`) and turns `\\?\UNC\server\share\x` into `\\server\share\x`; any other verbatim name and every ordinary path is left as it is. It is used for display only: the path used to open or write a file is never changed. It is applied where the failing tests showed the wart: the `is a folder, not a file` refusal of `write_atomic` (so `doc_save` and the other writers), the same refusal in `image_save`, and the `<folder>: <system error>` message when a folder cannot be made (`Written::within`, so `action_run` and the other tools that make a folder, and `write_atomic`'s own folder). Other messages that print a path with `.display()` are unchanged and may still show the prefix on Windows. Why: four automation tests failed on the maintainer's Windows PC: the three in `save_flatten_failure.rs` and `an_action_run_whose_folder_cannot_be_made_names_it` in `partial_output.rs`. The root is canonicalised (on Windows that gives `\\?\C:\...`) and the tools built their messages from that path, so the message read `\\?\C:\Users\...\taken.pdf: is a folder, not a file` where the tests expected `C:\Users\...\taken.pdf: ...`. For `partial_output.rs` the wording after the path (`Cannot create a file when that file already exists. (os error 183)`) is not what the test checks, only that the message starts with the folder path; the same prefix was the cause there. The product was at fault (a message a person reads should not show `\\?\`), not the tests, so no test was changed, weakened or skipped. Cost: one string prefix check on an error path; nothing on the success path. Tested: `paths::tests::the_extended_length_prefix_is_dropped_for_display_only_where_it_adds_nothing` checks the helper on strings, so it runs on Linux: a drive path, a UNC path, an ordinary Windows path, a Unix path, a non-verbatim UNC path, a verbatim name that is not a drive and the `Path` form. Mutations: 4 were run on the helper: UNC prefix dropped to the bare rest (killed), no drive-colon check (killed), alphanumeric instead of alphabetic drive letter (killed), `shown` not calling the string rule (survived at first: the test only called the string rule; the `Path` assertion was added and the mutation is now killed). Not mutated: the call sites of `shown`. Not tested: the Windows behaviour was NOT run here (this is Linux) and the four failing tests have not been seen to pass; it will be checked on the maintainer's PC. If a temp folder there is given as an 8.3 short name, the root's canonical form would still differ from it and the tests would still fail for that reason; this is not known. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (no `-A`, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (90 library tests passed; 767 integration tests passed and 1 failed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: tests of crates other than `pdfcraft-automation`, `cargo xtask i18n-report`, the four failing tests on Windows (only Linux was run), and the workspace sweep. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-22 (M9, field signer, label, headless): What: two new headless tools, `form_field_signer` and `form_signer_report`, assign a form field to a signing-plan recipient. `form_field_signer` takes `doc`, `field` (the field's name, as `form_fields` gives it) and optionally `signer`: leaving `signer` out only reads; an email address sets the label; an empty string or an explicit null clears it (a null is not the same as leaving the argument out). The label is a private key of the field's own dictionary, `/PdfCraftSigner`, an email address written as a text string (PdfCraft had no private key of its own before; the name is unlikely to collide with another reader's key, and a reader that does not know it ignores it); it is read from the field's own dictionary only, not inherited from a parent or taken from a widget kid. It is a label stored in the file: it does NOT restrict who can fill or sign the field, nothing is sent and nobody is notified, and other readers ignore it; every result says so (`label_only` true and a `note`), and so do both tool descriptions and the parity notes (rule 15). The address is stored in the PDF and travels with every copy of the file; the note says so. Departures from `docs/esign-design.md` (Local design, item 2): the label lives on the field's own dictionary and not on the widget kid, and the assignment lives in the PDF while the plan lives in its sidecar, so there are two places to keep in step by hand (`form_signer_report` lists where they differ); both are said in the tool description. The address is checked by the signing plan's own checker (`check_email` in `signing_plan.rs`, now `pub(crate)` and not copied): one @, no spaces, a dot in the domain, at most 254 bytes and a local part of at most 64 bytes. The plan is looked up beside the document's own file (`<file name>.pdfcraft-sign.json`, from the document's path, which goes through `resolve_for` and the read root; the file itself may be gone, and after a Save As the plan beside the new file is the one looked at). With a plan beside the file, a set must name a recipient of it whose role is signer or approver: an address that is not in the plan and a viewer are refused with a sentence that says so, and the label is written with the plan's own spelling of the address (addresses are compared without regard to case). With no plan, or a document with no path, or a path not ending in .pdf, a well-formed address is accepted and the result says plainly that it was not checked against a plan (`plan.state` is `none` or `not_checked`). A sidecar that is not a valid plan, or one the roots refuse, refuses a set (nothing is written, the file is left alone), and a read, and `form_signer_report`, still answer with `plan.state` `error` and the plan parts empty. A set or clear is one undo step (`Edit::SetFieldSigner`, label "Set field signer", checked by the same permission rule as other field property edits: it needs permission to modify the document; the `settable` flag in a result does not look at permissions, and the description says so), a refused call writes nothing and adds no undo step, and setting the label a field already has or clearing a field with none changes nothing (`changed` false, no undo step). A signature field that holds a signature (`/V` not null, `/FT` found on the field or a parent) is refused for a write and still read. Labelling ANY field, and clearing a label, is a change to the form after the document was signed, so it can break a signature already in the file: a certification that allows no changes (`P` 1) treats it as a change it forbids, and a signature whose `/Lock` names the field, or all fields, locks it. This is decided by the validator and not by a copy of its rules (`pdfcraft-organize` cannot use `pdfcraft-sign`, same layer): in `Session::apply` (`crates/engine/src/session/editing.rs`), once the edit is in and the document is validated again as it already is after every edit, `signature_damage` compares every signed signature's verdict before and after; if any one's status fell, it now has changes it forbids, or its changes could no longer be checked, the edit is rolled back (the redo steps are kept as they were, and so is the oldest undo step that the push made room for when the history was full) and refused with a sentence that names the signature field and the cause: a certification that allows no changes (any change to the form, a label on any field included), a lock that covers this field, a status that fell, or changes that could no longer be checked; a signature that allows form filling (an approval signature, or a certification at level 2 or 3) does not stop the label, and a signature that already had changes it forbids before the edit passes, because the label does not make its verdict worse. The check also applies to a label edit inside a batch (`contains_field_signer`, recursive). It does not cover signatures made after the label is written: sign_list after labelling a document that will be signed. A field name that two fields share is refused, for a read too, as `signature_field_lock` does. A label read from a file is echoed with control, zero-width and direction-changing characters taken out (the check of `signing_plan.rs`, `is_hidden`, now `pub(crate)`), `signer_cleaned` says whether any were, and `well_formed` says whether the stored text is a well-formed address as it stands (an address with spaces round it is not called well formed, since the plan comparisons do not trim); an empty string (or white space only) is no label in both tools (`signer` null, `empty_label` true; the report counts the field as unassigned; a clear takes the key out). `form_signer_report` lists every field with its label (`assigned`, each with its kind, `signature_field`, the signer and the plan recipient it matches), the fields with none (`unassigned`, with `unassigned_signature_fields` counting the signature fields among them), labels that are not a string (`unreadable`), labels naming an address that is not a recipient of the plan (`signers_not_in_plan`, with `signers_not_in_plan_count`) and plan recipients with no field (`plan_recipients_without_fields`, with its count, each with whether the recipient has a turn); each of the five lists has at most 500 rows (the counts cover every field; `truncated` says any list was cut), and a name two fields share is marked. The label is in `pdfcraft_organize::field_signer` (read, change check and set, with a read allowance of 1000 reads), the edit in the engine, the tools in `crates/automation/src/field_signer.rs`. No Field Properties combo and no recipient-limited signing view are built; the parity entry `sign.field-assignment` is partial (headless only). Follow-up, not done here: the Sanitize document code (`crates/redact/src/sanitize.rs`, around its `OWNED` list) does not strip `/PdfCraftSigner`, so a sanitized file keeps the address; the sanitizer was not edited in this slice, and an issue should be opened for it. Why: `docs/esign-design.md` (Local design, item 2) needs a field to say whose it is before a signing view can show a recipient only their fields; this is that label and its report, small and testable on its own, with no service, account or network. Cost: one small read of the field's dictionary per field in a report (at most 1000 reads each); a set is one dictionary write but, like every edit, is followed by a refresh of the whole document (the edit's scope is the full one), including validating every signature again; a set refused for a signature costs a second full re-inspection for the roll back; the undo step that rolls back is a copy of the working document kept only until the edit is accepted; a text string of at most 254 bytes in the file; no network, no setting, no asset, no dependency and no translated text (the tool results are English; the undo label is English). Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/field_signer.rs`, nineteen tests, and seven unit tests in `crates/engine/src/session/editing.rs`: `a_label_is_set_read_saved_reopened_undone_redone_and_cleared`, `a_call_that_changes_nothing_adds_no_undo_step`, `the_label_survives_an_incremental_save_over_a_signed_file` (a signed signature field is read but refused for a write; an unsigned one in the same file is labelled and saved incrementally with the signed bytes in front, and the audit report does not say the changes are forbidden), `a_label_that_the_signature_forbids_is_refused_and_leaves_it_whole` (a certification with P=1 made by `sign_document`: the label is refused with the validator's sentence, no undo step, the file and the audit report's `covers` whole_file, status and empty `changes_after_signing` are as they were), `a_label_on_a_field_a_signatures_lock_names_is_refused_and_one_it_does_not_name_is_not` (a signed Sig2 whose `/Lock` includes `name`: refused; `city` is written, the status is as before and the audit report says the changes are allowed; a lock of all fields refuses any field; a refused label leaves an undone step redoable), `a_label_is_written_where_the_signature_allows_form_filling` (an approval signature and a P=2 certification: written, status not worse than before, `covers` changes_allowed, saved incrementally, and a clear is allowed and one undo step), `with_a_plan_only_a_signer_or_an_approver_of_it_can_be_named`, `without_a_plan_a_well_formed_address_is_accepted_and_says_it_is_unchecked`, `a_plan_that_cannot_be_read_refuses_a_set_and_still_allows_a_read` (now also the report: `plan.state` error and the plan parts empty), `an_unknown_or_shared_field_name_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written`, `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_write_and_still_reads_and_reports`, `a_read_only_field_and_a_field_a_lock_names_can_be_labelled_when_the_document_allows`, `the_report_lists_fields_signers_unassigned_signature_fields_and_the_plans_gaps`, `a_label_that_is_not_a_string_is_reported_as_unreadable_and_can_be_replaced`, `an_empty_label_is_no_label_in_both_tools_and_a_clear_takes_it_out`, `hidden_characters_in_a_stored_label_are_not_echoed_and_the_label_is_flagged` (an override and a zero-width character in a stored label, in both tools, with `well_formed`) and `the_labels_not_in_the_plan_are_capped_and_the_report_says_it_was_cut` (505 labels not in a plan: count 505, 500 rows), `a_refused_label_keeps_the_oldest_undo_step_of_a_full_history` (101 steps on a signed document with a lock, then a refused label: 100 undo steps are still there and the oldest one kept is unchanged) and `the_refusal_names_its_cause` (the certification and the lock sentences); `hidden_characters_in_a_stored_label_are_not_echoed_and_the_label_is_flagged` also checks that an address with spaces round it is not well formed. The unit tests of `signature_damage` use hand-built signatures: forbidden changes with the status already invalid, a status that falls, changes that could no longer be checked with the status staying unknown, the sentence for each cause (a certification, a lock, other), a signature that already forbade the changes passing, an unmatched signature, and a batch holding a label edit counting as one. The tests use fictional people only. The hostile-input panic sweep (`no_tool_panics_on_hostile_arguments`) and the damaged-file sweep now cover both tools, and its fixture gives them a saved document with a field `name` and a plan beside it so that hostile values reach the `signer` argument and the plan check; `no_tool_escapes_the_root` is not claimed to cover them (they take no path argument: the document's path and the plan beside it go through `resolve_for` and `check_roots`), and `tools_do_not_touch_the_file_system_without_resolving_the_path` passes with no allowlist entry. Mutations: 25 distinct ones were run over two rounds, 22 killed and 3 survived on the final code. Killed: the viewer refusal removed; the address comparison made case-sensitive; an empty or white-space string treated as a read; the same-value no-change check removed; the signed-field refusal removed; the `signers_not_in_plan` condition inverted; the unreadable-plan refusal on a set replaced by treating it as no plan; the whole signature-damage check turned off; the redo steps not restored after a refusal; echoed labels not cleaned; `well_formed` always true; the report failing on an unreadable plan; the not-in-plan list uncapped; an empty string treated as unreadable; and, in the second round on the final code (eight runs, eight killed): the forbidden-changes term removed, the status-fell term removed and the changes-no-longer-checked term removed (these three survived the first round and are now killed by the unit tests, so they are not redundant), the dropped undo step not put back, a batch not counted as a label edit, the certification sentence off, the lock sentence off, and `well_formed` ignoring spaces. Survived: the check that the object is a field with a `/T` removed from the engine's change check, in two forms (the `settable` refusal removed, and `settable` always true), because every field the tools offer has a name and no test passes the engine another kind of object; and the `signers_not_in_plan` term removed from `truncated` (it cannot be the only cut list: more than 500 rows there means more than 500 assigned fields, which are cut as well). Not mutated: the 500-row cap of the other lists, the 254-character read cut and `exact`, the 1000-read allowance, the `/Parent` walk that finds a field's type, the root checks, and the plan-state wording. Not tested: a signature whose changes the validator cannot compare, through a real file (only the hand-built unit test covers that term); an unmatched signature through a real file (only the unit test); a non-field object given to `Edit::SetFieldSigner` through the engine (not reachable through the tools); a label on a field whose type is inherited from a parent and which holds a signature; a field with widget kids; more than 500 fields in the other lists; a label longer than 254 characters in a file; an exhausted read allowance; the roots refusing the sidecar for a document outside the read root; Save As to another folder than the plan's; an undo or redo through the user interface (no UI is built); the label read by another PDF reader; the sanitizer follow-up; Windows and macOS (only Linux was run); and that the label restricts nobody, which is true because no code reads it for that and is not asserted by a test. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (no `-A`, exit 0 each); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (89 library tests passed, 767 integration tests passed and 1 failed, 2 in another target passed); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine` (all passed, 906 engine library tests among them); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: tests of the other crates (`pdfcraft-sign` was not changed and its tests were not run; `pdfcraft-ui-egui` was built only by `cargo xtask wasm`, its tests were not run), `cargo xtask i18n-report` (not run), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), the mutations listed under Not mutated, and a hand check of the tools through `pdfcraft-cli`. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-22 (M9, signing plan, local record, headless): What: four new headless tools keep a local signing plan beside a PDF: `signing_plan_set`, `signing_plan_get`, `signing_plan_advance` and `signing_plan_clear`. A plan is recipients (name, email, role `signer`, `approver` or `viewer`, order number), a message and each recipient's status with its history (`pending`, `sent`, `signed`, `approved`, `declined`, each with a time in seconds from the host's clock, 0 when the host has none; a step recorded by a host whose clock reads earlier than the last event takes that last time, so a history never runs backwards), kept as plain serde data in a sidecar file named from the PDF's whole file name, `<file name>.pdfcraft-sign.json` (`a.pdf` has `a.pdf.pdfcraft-sign.json`, so `a.pdf` and `a.PDF` never share a plan); the PDF's bytes are never touched. The plan assumes the sidecar: keeping it inside the PDF as an attachment is not built. It is a local record only: nothing is sent anywhere, nobody is notified, no signature is collected, and `signing_plan_advance` writes down what the caller says happened without looking for a signature in the PDF; every result says so (`local_record_only` true and a `note`), and so do the tool descriptions and the parity notes (rule 15). `sent` is in the file format for a later sending route and no tool sets it. Arguments: `path` (the PDF, which must exist and end in .pdf, read through the roots like every path); `signing_plan_set` takes `recipients` (an array of `{name, email, role?, order?}`), `message?` and `replace?`; `signing_plan_advance` takes `email` and `status`: a signer's step is `signed`, an approver's is `approved`, and either may be `declined`; the other combination is refused. Limits and rules, all checked before any write: at most 50 recipients, a name of 1 to 200 characters, an email of at most 254 bytes with a local part of at most 64 bytes (octets, as the SMTP limits are; a refusal for length says so in its own sentence naming both limits), a message of at most 2000 characters; names and emails refuse every control character, the line and paragraph separators (U+2028, U+2029), and the characters that hide, reorder or disguise text, which is the list of `hides_text` in `crates/engine/src/links.rs` (copied, since that function is private to the engine crate) without its other whitespace: U+200B to U+200F, U+202A to U+202E, U+2060 to U+2064, U+2066 to U+2069, U+FEFF, U+061C, soft hyphen, U+034F, the Hangul fillers U+115F, U+1160, U+3164 and U+FFA0, U+180E and the tag characters U+E0000 to U+E007F; the message refuses the same except that it may hold line feeds, tabs and carriage returns; the email check is shallow on purpose (one @, something either side, no spaces, a dot inside the domain; it is not checked that the domain exists or that the address can receive mail); a duplicate address is refused, compared without regard to case; at least one recipient must be a signer or approver; the order is a strict list, 1, 2, 3 ... with none missing or repeated, given to every recipient or to none (none means the list order), one recipient at a time, a viewer has no turn, and parallel groups are not supported (not implemented, so not offered). `signing_plan_advance` refuses a recipient whose turn it is not, a viewer, an address not in the plan, a status that does not fit the role, a missing plan and a finished plan (everyone who signs or approves has done so, or someone declined); `signing_plan_set` over a plan in which any recipient has a status other than pending (signed, approved, declined or sent) needs `replace: true`, and its result says whether a plan was replaced (`replaced`) and whether that plan had history that is now gone (`discarded_history`); the tool is not marked destructive, because that flag is for the whole tool and most calls only make a plan. A sidecar that cannot be read as a valid plan (not JSON, wrong shape or version, unknown fields, repeated addresses, order numbers off, a name or address that is not valid or not already trimmed, a history that does not start pending, run forward in time and end at the recipient's status, a turn taken out of order or in the wrong role, a message that breaks the rules, over 1 MiB, or a link or folder instead of a file) is refused by all four tools with "left alone" and is never overwritten or deleted. A sidecar that is a link is not followed: the name is looked at without following it, the file is opened once, what was opened must be a plain file within the size limit and (on unix) the same file the name showed, and the read stops one byte past the limit; `O_NOFOLLOW` is not used because it needs `libc` or `rustix` and `rustix` is only a macOS test dependency of this crate, so a swap between the look and the open is caught by the same-file comparison on unix and not on other systems. A refused call writes nothing; the one write is an atomic file write (`write_atomic`). The sidecar is derived from the resolved PDF path and checked against the read root and, for a write, the write root as well (it does not itself pass through `resolve`; a root refusal names the sidecar), so with separate roots the plan works only where the PDF's own folder is in both (a refusal, not a silent success). Where the code lives: `crates/automation/src/signing_plan.rs`, a module of the shared tool layer, because the model is small, plain data with its validation and nothing below the tool layer needs it yet; no new crate, and when the app needs the same plan it moves into a crate under `crates/` in its own PR. `serde` (already a workspace dependency, derive enabled) is added to `crates/automation/Cargo.toml`, and `Cargo.lock` changes by that one line; nothing else in the manifests. `crates/automation/tests/it/main.rs` gained `mod signing_plan`, and `crates/automation/tests/it/hostile_args.rs` a fixture arm for the four tools, which puts them in the hostile-input sweep with a real PDF and a real plan so hostile values reach their validators; no other existing test code changed (the lint test in `file_access.rs` that wants a `resolve` before a file-system call is satisfied by `resolve_plan_paths` and by the parameter name `resolved_sidecar`, with no allowlist change: the sidecar is derived from the resolved PDF path and root-checked, so the call itself satisfies the scan, which is why the helper was renamed instead of being added to `RESOLVED_BY_CALLER`). The design is the first item of "Local design" in `docs/esign-design.md`; its attachment storage, field assignment, sign-as-next-recipient, hand-off package, audit page and screen are not built, and the feature is called a signing plan, not after any product. Why: asking other people to sign a document, and tracking who has, needs a record of who, in what order, with what status, before any route that sends it; this is that record and its order rules, small and testable on its own, with no service, account or network. The parity entry `sign.request-plan` is partial (headless only, no screen yet). Cost: one small file read (at most 1 MiB, normally under 20 KB) and one atomic write per call, parsing at most 50 recipients; no rendering, no network, no setting, no asset and no translated text (the tool results are English). Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/signing_plan.rs`, fourteen tests: `a_plan_round_trips_through_the_sidecar_and_leaves_the_pdf_alone` (a set then a get give the same plan with the recipients in order, the message kept, everyone pending with one history entry, the state in progress with the first recipient next, the result saying it is a local record that does not send or collect signatures, the PDF's bytes unchanged, clear removes the sidecar and not the PDF, clearing nothing is not an error), `listed_order_numbers_decide_the_turns`, `turns_are_taken_in_order_and_the_plan_finishes` (out of turn, a viewer, an unknown address and a status other than signed, approved or declined are refused with the folder byte for byte unchanged; a signer cannot approve and an approver cannot sign; the address is matched without regard to case; the turn passes over the viewer; a recipient who signed cannot sign again; the history grows by one event that is not earlier than the first; the plan completes with no next recipient and then refuses a further step with nothing changed), `a_declined_plan_is_finished_and_needs_replace_to_be_remade`, `an_unprogressed_plan_is_replaced_without_replace`, `a_refused_set_writes_nothing_new_and_keeps_the_old_plan` (35 bad recipient lists including hidden characters (U+061C, soft hyphen, U+2028, U+2029, U+2060, U+2066, U+FEFF, U+034F, U+115F, U+180E, U+3164, a tag character, a zero-width space in an address, a line feed and a right-to-left override; not every character of the list) and the byte limits, eight bad messages and four bad paths, each refused with the folder unchanged, first with no plan and then with a plan in place; the duplicate address is named as such; the limits of 50 recipients, 200 characters and 2000 characters are accepted, and a message with line feeds, tabs and carriage returns is accepted), `a_sidecar_that_is_not_a_plan_is_refused_and_never_overwritten_or_deleted` (eighteen files, each a valid plan with one thing changed, or garbage: another version with a valid recipient, an unknown field, an order number off, an address twice, signed out of turn, approved in a signer's role, a name or address not trimmed, a hidden character in a name, a bad message, a history not starting pending, running backwards in time or ending at another status, and a valid plan padded with spaces past 1 MiB; each refused by all four tools with the file unchanged and with the sentence of that rule; the unaltered plan is then read; the order-numbers and repeated-address refusals have different sentences, as have signed out of turn and approved in the wrong role, and a history running backwards has its own), `a_sidecar_that_is_a_link_to_a_valid_plan_is_not_followed` (unix only: the target is a valid plan, all four tools refuse with "not a plain file", the target's bytes are unchanged and the link is still there), `a_plan_with_a_recipient_marked_sent_is_not_overwritten_without_replace`, `a_first_plan_replaces_nothing_and_discards_nothing`, `a_clock_set_back_does_not_make_the_history_run_backwards` (a plan made at a real time and then advanced by a host whose clock reads 0: the new event is not earlier than the first and the file is still read back), `the_limits_count_characters_for_names_and_messages_and_bytes_for_addresses` (200 two-byte characters in a name and 2000 three-byte characters in a message are accepted and 201 and 2001 refused; an address of exactly 254 bytes and a local part of exactly 64 bytes are accepted and one more refused with the sentence naming the limits), `files_that_differ_only_in_the_case_of_the_extension_keep_separate_plans` (returns early, saying so, in a case-insensitive folder) and `the_plan_stays_inside_the_roots` (with a read root and a write root that differ the write is refused and nothing is made; the refusal names the sidecar; a PDF outside both is refused; with one folder for both it works). The two hostile-input tests (`no_tool_panics_on_hostile_arguments`, `no_tool_escapes_the_root`) now include the four tools: the escape test loops over every tool's path properties, so it covers `path` of these tools, but for the tools that also need `recipients` or `email` the placeholder values may be what refuses the call, so only the get and clear refusals are certain to come from the path. Mutations: on the final code, twelve were run: the turn-order check removed from `signing_plan_advance` (killed by `turns_are_taken_in_order_and_the_plan_finishes`); the validation of a sidecar when read removed (killed by the not-a-plan test); the `replace` check removed (killed by the declined-plan test); the duplicate-address check in `signing_plan_set` removed (killed by `a_refused_set_writes_nothing_new_and_keeps_the_old_plan`, after its first version survived because the plan's own validation also refuses a duplicate with another sentence, so the test asserts the sentence); the link refusal removed, both the look at the name and the same-file comparison together (killed by the link test); the size limit removed, both the check of the file's size and the check of the bytes read together (killed by the padded-plan case); the version check removed (killed by the another-version case, whose file holds a valid recipient); `started` not counting `sent` (killed by the marked-sent test); the time of an advance taken as the clock reads, without the last event as a floor (killed by the clock test); `replaced` and `discarded_history` always false (killed by the declined-plan and unprogressed-plan tests); and the address length and the local-part length counted in characters instead of bytes (each killed by the byte-limit test and the bad-lists test). Survived: none of the mutations run survives. Not mutated: the link look alone and the same-file comparison alone, and the size check alone and the read limit alone (each pair covers the other, so one removed alone is not seen by any test, by design), the email shape rules other than their use in the tests above, the hidden-character list entry by entry, the 50, 200 and 2000 limits, the history-length limit, the root checks, the role-and-status match in `signing_plan_advance`, the trimmed-name check, the history-order and history-start checks read back, and the validity rules that read a sidecar back other than as one group.
