Not tested: parallel signing groups (not implemented); a recipient's `sent` status other than in a hand-made file (no tool sets it); a sidecar that is a folder or a FIFO (a named pipe would be opened and could block; the look at the name sees it is not a plain file first, but no test makes one); an unreadable sidecar (permissions) or a full disk; a link swapped in between the look and the open (the same-file comparison is not exercised by a race); two callers writing the same plan at once (the last write wins, the write itself is atomic), and a file swapped in between the checks and the delete in `signing_plan_clear` (the delete is of the name, not of the opened file); a PDF path through a link; the time 0 of a host without a clock; Windows and macOS (only Linux was run, and the link test is unix only); the claim that an email address belongs to a person (not checked); and that nothing is sent, which is true because no code here opens a connection and is not asserted by a test. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (no `-A`, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (89 library tests passed, 748 integration tests passed and 1 failed, 2 in another target passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; the mutations above. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: tests of the other crates (only `pdfcraft-automation` and its manifest and the lock changed; the `cargo xtask` checks above built what they need), `cargo xtask i18n-report` (not run), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), the mutations listed under Not mutated, and a hand check of the tools through `pdfcraft-cli`. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-22 (M9, signature audit report, headless): What: `signature_audit_report` (new, headless) summarises the signatures of the open document as PdfCraft's validator read them (the same validation `sign_list` gives; nothing is validated again). Argument: `doc`, and optionally `output`, a path inside the write root (an existing file there is replaced). It returns the document's name, page count, size and the SHA-256 of the document as PdfCraft holds it now (`unsaved_changes` says there are changes not saved, and `changed_on_open` that opening the file made PdfCraft add changes of its own, such as a dynamic XFA form's layout or data sync or a repaired page tree: the signatures were checked against those bytes, so changes after signing may include PdfCraft's own, the fingerprint may differ from the file on disk, and saving does not clear the flag; `document_cut` says the name was cut at 240 characters, and `stand_in_counted` that the counts include a stand-in row for fields a too-large form kept unread), the counts of signatures (`found`, `signed`, `valid`, `unknown`, `invalid`), the number of certificates on the trust list, and for each signature field, document timestamp, empty signature field or stand-in row for fields a too-large form kept the validator from reading (`kind`: `signature`, `document_timestamp`, `unsigned_field`, `unread_fields`): the field name, the status (`valid`, `unknown`, `invalid` or `unsigned`) with the validator's own explanatory sentences (`reasons`; `reasons_cut` when more than 12 were left out), the signer and the certificate's subject and issuer, whether the certificate is self-signed, the signing time written in the file (`/M`) and the signing time used for checks (a signed attribute, else `/M`; both claimed by the signer), any timestamp (for a signature its time is called validated when the token's authority chains to the trusted list; for a document timestamp only when the status is `valid`), the hash algorithm, the signature format, the revision covered, whether the signature covers the whole file (`covers_whole_file`: true, false, or null when not determined) or changes follow it (`covers`: `whole_file`, `changes_allowed`, `changes_disallowed`, `not_determined` when validation stopped before the changes were classified or could not compare the fields the signature locks, or `not_applicable`; `changes_after_signing` names them, from the existing change classification), the certification level (1 to 3) of a certification signature, the page, and the reason and location the signer gave. `status_meaning` says that unknown is not shown invalid but not shown valid either (an untrusted signer, or something that could not be checked, including signatures PdfCraft cannot check yet), and invalid includes unreadable signature data and a missing or revoked signer certificate. `checked` and `not_checked` list what was and was not done: revocation is read only for a signature's signer certificate, only from information stored in the file's /DSS and when the signing time is known, nothing online is contacted, revocation of intermediate certificates and of timestamp authorities' certificates is not checked, revocation data archived inside the signature (adbe-revocationInfoArchival) is not read, who the signer really is in the world is not checked, and nothing says whether a signature has legal effect. The text is English (`language`). With `output` the same facts are also written as a new plain-text PDF titled `Signature audit report` (made with the existing text-to-PDF code, `Session::create_report`) that says it is a summary generated locally from the signatures in the file and is not itself signed. A document with no signed signature gives an empty report on a read (`signed` 0) and is refused for `output`, with nothing written; a document whose structure could not be read is refused, since no signature was looked for; an `output` outside the write root, or the open document's own file, is refused before anything is written. The report changes nothing in the document and signs nothing. At most 50 entries are listed (`truncated` says there were more; the counts cover all of them), text taken from the file is made one line, stripped of characters that hide, reorder or disguise text and cut at 240 characters (`text_cut`), and at most 12 of a signature's sentences are repeated. The code is `crates/engine/src/signature_audit.rs` (the report and its text) and `Session::signature_audit` and `signature_audit_pdf`, and the tool in `crates/automation/src/signing.rs`; the signature crate gained `SignatureInfo::changes_checked` (whether the change check ran and could be finished) and the document `Document::changed_on_open`; the parity entry `sign.audit-report` is partial. Why: PdfCraft signs and validates digitally, but a person who has to hand a signed file to someone else had no one-page record of who signed it, when, with what, whether the file changed afterwards and what was not checked. This is the first, local slice of that: a report read from the file, with no service, account or network. The feature is called a signature audit report and not a certificate of completion: `certificate` already means an X.509 certificate here, and a certificate of completion reads as an assurance this report does not give (the name also avoids another company's product wording, AGENTS.md rule 22). It follows rule 15: it says what the validator did and did not check, that the report is not signed, and says nothing about legal effect. There is no screen yet (rule 14 is met by the tool, the screen comes later), so the entry is partial. Cost: One pass of SHA-256 over the document's bytes, one comparison of the bytes at open with the input (once per open), and a copy of at most 50 entries holding about 21 texts each (nine of at most 240 characters and at most 12 sentences), so at most about 250,000 characters, about 1 MB as UTF-8; in the report PDF a character the font cannot draw is written as a code point of up to 8 characters, so the worst case is about 2 million characters and a PDF of several hundred pages. Reading the signatures is free: they were read when the document was opened under the validator's own limits (20,000 fields, 100,000 entries) and not read again. No dependency, asset, setting or translated text (the report is in English, and `language` says so); the tool's description and schema are new; the output is one atomic file write. Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/signature_audit_report.rs`, nine tests on files signed with the signing tools (`sign_id_create`, `sign_document`): `a_signed_file_is_reported_with_the_signer_hash_time_coverage_and_the_files_fingerprint` (one signature: the SHA-256 and size equal the file's, `changed_on_open` false, signer, subject, self-signed, SHA-256 hash algorithm, status unknown and no timestamp, the revision, the claimed and read time, whole-file coverage, no certification level, reason, location and page, the validator's sentences passed on, `not_checked` saying no online revocation check and not signed, then trusting the ID makes the status valid exactly as `sign_list` says and the trusted count 1), `the_first_of_two_signatures_permits_the_second_and_only_the_last_covers_the_whole_file` (two signers, the first covered with changes allowed and the second whole-file, revisions in order), `changes_a_certification_forbids_are_reported_as_changes_after_signing` (a no-changes certification covers the whole file, a comment added afterwards turns it into changes disallowed and invalid with the changes named and the same revision, `unsaved_changes` true, the fingerprint no longer the file's, and the written PDF says the changes are not saved and are not permitted), `an_unsigned_document_gives_an_empty_report_and_no_report_file` (an empty report with the page count and the file's SHA-256, `output` refused with nothing written, an empty signature field listed as unsigned with no revision and `not_applicable` coverage), `the_report_file_is_a_pdf_that_says_what_it_is_and_what_it_did_not_check` (the written PDF opens, has no signature of its own, and its text holds the title, the sentence that it is a summary generated locally and not itself signed, the SHA-256, the signer, the page count, the coverage sentence, the no-online-revocation, intermediate-certificate and no-legal-effect statements, the meaning of unknown and the trust sentence, and does not say certificate of; an `output` outside the write root and over the document's own file are refused, the signed file untouched), `hostile_signature_data_is_cut_cleaned_counted_and_never_a_panic` (a signer name with a line break and 300 characters is one line of at most 240, `text_cut` is true and `reasons_cut` false and the PDF says so; a signer name and a file name holding bidirectional controls, zero-width characters, a byte order mark and a line separator come out without them; 60 signature fields are counted, 50 listed and `truncated` true), `a_signature_with_no_usable_byte_range_is_invalid_with_reasons_and_no_coverage_claim` (a signature value with a byte range of [0 0 0 0], which is refused before its /Contents is read, is invalid with sentences, its reason is one clean line, and its coverage is `not_determined`, its revision and `covers_whole_file` null), `a_document_timestamp_that_does_not_validate_is_not_called_validated` (a document timestamp with an empty byte range is listed as a document timestamp, is not valid, has coverage not determined, and the PDF neither says `Timestamp: validated` nor gives it a certification level or calls it an approval signature) and `the_report_file_goes_to_the_write_root_and_never_to_the_read_root` (separate read and write roots: the report is written inside the write root and refused inside the read root, with nothing written there). The engine's unit tests (`crates/engine/src/signature_audit.rs`) hold five hand-built cases: `a_document_timestamp_time_is_validated_only_when_the_status_is_valid_but_a_signatures_when_its_authority_is_trusted`, `a_report_of_a_document_without_its_structure_is_refused`, `changes_the_signature_cannot_be_compared_with_are_not_reported_as_permitted`, `only_the_listings_own_stand_in_row_is_called_unread_fields` (a real field named UnreadFields stays a signature) and `a_document_name_cut_at_the_limit_says_so`. - 2026-11-22 (M4, split name template, headless): What: `doc_split` takes an optional `name_template` that names each part (without `.pdf`) from the placeholders `{name}` (the document's file name without its extension, as `file_stem` gives it), `{bookmark}` (the top-level bookmark on the part's first page, empty when there is none; it works with `every`, `before` and `max_mb` as well as `bookmarks: true`), `{n}` (part number from 1), `{first}` and `{last}` (its page numbers from 1), with `{{` and `}}` for literal braces; an unknown placeholder, a stray brace or a template over 1000 bytes is refused before anything is written, and an expansion is cut at 4096 bytes so a huge bookmark title costs nothing. The expanded name is made a safe file name (`crates/automation/src/split_name.rs`): characters other than letters, digits, space and `-_.,()` become `_` (every path separator, `:` and control character); every `..` becomes `_`, repeated until none is left, so `...` becomes `_.`; dots and spaces at either end are dropped; a Windows device name (CON, PRN, AUX, NUL, COM0-9, LPT0-9, COM and LPT with a superscript 1, 2 or 3; with or without an extension) gets a `_` in front, checked again after the cut; and the name is cut to 200 bytes between characters. A closing `.pdf` of the template's own text (`x.pdf`, `x.pdf.`, `x.pdf .`, any case) is not doubled, and a `.pdf` inside a `{bookmark}` or `{name}` value is left alone. A name that is empty after that falls back to the part's default name. The default names (`<name>-partK`, `<name>-<bookmark>`) get the same device-name guard, so a document called `nul.x.pdf` gives `_nul.x-part1.pdf`. Parts that end up with the same name get -2, -3 as before, compared as `to_uppercase().to_lowercase()` so case and sigma forms count as equal. Without `name_template` the names are unchanged except for that device-name guard. What the code does about an existing file, and the tool text now says so in its own sentence: a file already in `out_dir` with a part's name is replaced (written atomically), not skipped or renamed around; only parts of the same call are de-duplicated; on a later failure a replaced file stays with the new content. The parity entry `organize.split-naming` (title: never overwrite) is now `partial`, with `doc_split` and four tests, and its notes say there is no never-overwrite mode and that the split dialog in the app has no name template and is unchanged. Why: the entry was `planned` with no tool although `doc_split` already named parts after bookmarks, and its tool text mentioned replacing an existing file only inside the failure sentence. Cost: one new source file and one optional string argument; no dependency, asset, setting or translated text; the argument is in the tool's schema and description. Tested: in `crates/automation/tests/it/split_name_template.rs`: `a_template_names_every_part_from_its_placeholders` (`every`), `a_template_can_use_the_bookmark_a_part_starts_at` (`bookmarks: true`), `bookmark_placeholder_works_with_before_and_without_bookmarks_true` (`before`, no `bookmarks: true`), `without_a_template_the_default_names_are_unchanged`, `a_template_with_nothing_usable_falls_back_to_the_default_name`, `parts_that_get_the_same_name_stay_distinct_files`, `bookmark_titles_that_differ_only_in_case_get_distinct_files` (Intro and INTRO give `Intro.pdf` and `INTRO-2.pdf`), `a_template_cannot_put_a_part_outside_out_dir` (nine hostile templates, parts listed and the parent folder compared, nothing new beside `out_dir`), `windows_device_names_and_trailing_dots_are_made_safe`, `a_device_name_stem_is_guarded_in_the_default_names_too`, `a_long_template_is_cut_to_a_name_the_system_accepts`, `a_template_over_the_length_cap_is_refused`, `a_bad_template_is_refused_and_writes_nothing`, `a_file_with_a_parts_name_is_replaced`; and nine unit tests in `split_name.rs` (sanitizer cases, `.pdf` strip, device name after a cut, huge value, sigma fold). `hostile_args.rs` needed no named entry: `no_tool_panics_on_hostile_arguments` walks every property of every tool, so `name_template` gets the whole value zoo, and the template is parsed first so those values reach the parser. Mutations: each reverted by the tests, run one at a time. On the final code: `{bookmark}` filled only with `bookmarks: true` (the `before` test fails); no device-name guard in the default names (the nul.x test fails); fold by `to_lowercase()` only (the sigma unit test fails); superscript and 0 digits dropped from the device check (the unit test and a tool test fail). On the first version of the code, before the review fixes, whose changed parts are not all re-mutated: no device-name prefix, separators allowed by the sanitizer, no de-duplication (at least three tool tests fail), an empty name returned instead of the fallback, the template ignored, no length cap; each failed at least one test. Not mutated: on the final code, the de-duplication, the length cap, the empty-name fallback, the ignored template and the `.pdf` strip (the last has unit tests but was not mutated); the `..` collapse and the dot trim were not mutated one by one. With `/` and `\` allowed by the sanitizer only the unit test fails: the tool tests still pass because `child` in `paths.rs` also turns separators into `_`, so the tool-level confinement does not prove the sanitizer alone. Not tested: Windows itself (the device names and trailing dots are checked as strings on Linux, and no file with such a name was created); a template on a split by file size (`max_mb`) was not run, only `every`, `before` and `bookmarks`; no test of `--automation-write-root` for this argument (the output folder resolves as before, the template adds no path); the split dialog in the app, which has no template and is unchanged. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings`; `cargo test -p pdfcraft-automation --no-fail-fast` (89 library tests, 2 and 0 in the other targets, 725 of 726 integration tests passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`, all on the final tree. Failed: `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing`, the known failure that is unrelated to this change (this change touches no batch code). Not run: `cargo xtask i18n-report`, `cargo xtask ci`, tests and clippy of any crate other than `pdfcraft-automation` (no other crate changed), the desktop app. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-22 (M7, Bates removal page choice, headless): What: `doc_bates_remove` takes an optional `pages` (1-based page numbers, the same argument `doc_header_footer` and `doc_backdrops` take) and removes the Bates numbers from only those pages; without `pages` it removes them from every page as before. `Edit::RemoveMarks` gained `pages: Option<Vec<usize>>` (0-based; `None` is every page), which `edit.bates_remove` in the app and `doc_remove_marks` still set to `None`. A page's shrink comes off only on the pages chosen, and only where just Bates numbers were drawn; a page whose header or footer still needs the shrink keeps it. An empty or out-of-range `pages` is refused with an invalid-arguments error, and a `pages` choice with no Bates numbers on any of its pages is refused with `there is nothing to remove on the chosen pages`; the whole-document refusal keeps its old text. Undoable in one step. The Remove Bates numbering menu item in the app is unchanged and still removes from every page. The parity entry `edit.bates-remove` stays partial: its note now says the page choice is headless only. Why: the parity note said `No page-range choice yet`. Cost: none beyond a field on one `Edit` variant; no dependency, asset, setting or translated text; the tool's description and schema gained `pages`. Tested: `bates_remove_with_pages_takes_numbers_off_only_the_chosen_pages` (page 2 of 3 loses its number, pages 1 and 3 and every footer keep theirs, one undo brings it back, no `pages` still removes all), `bates_remove_with_pages_refuses_an_empty_or_out_of_range_choice_and_pages_without_numbers` and, in the edit crate, `removing_bates_from_chosen_pages_restores_only_their_shrink` (page 1 back at its original content, page 2 keeps number and shrink; this guards existing per-page behaviour the new path relies on and passes without this change); the older Bates removal tests in the automation and edit crates still pass. Mutations: removing `pages` from the tool's schema made both new automation tests fail (the refusal test now matches the real `at least one page` and `out of range` texts); in `doc_bates_remove`, passing `pages: None` always, and in the engine, removing from every page whatever `pages` says, each made both new automation tests fail; in the edit crate, `remove_tagged` looping over every page instead of `pages` made `removing_bates_from_chosen_pages_restores_only_their_shrink` fail; each was reverted. Not mutated: the `nothing to remove on the chosen pages` wording (the test checks only `nothing to remove`), the schema text, the parity note. Not tested: a header-and-Bates page inside a chosen range (covered in the edit crate for one page; through the tool only for every page), a locked or encrypted document, the MCP listing of the new argument by a client, the app. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-edit -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo check -p pdfcraft-ui-egui`, `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-edit`, `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-engine`, `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (as `--lib`, `--test it` and `--test pattern_redaction`, one after the other, to save disk), `cargo xtask layers`, `cargo xtask assets`, `cargo xtask parity` and `cargo xtask wasm`, on the final tree; the xtask commands exited 0, and `layers`, `assets` and `parity` were run again after ROADMAP.md and docs/gaps.md were written. Failed: `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` in `pdfcraft-automation --test it` (711 passed, 1 failed; the known failure, unrelated to this change). Not run: `cargo clippy` and `cargo test` on `pdfcraft-ui-egui` (it was only checked to compile, for the two lines in `crates/ui-egui/src/commands.rs` that gained `pages: None`), the desktop menu item (the app was not started), `cargo xtask i18n-report` (no user-facing text changed). - 2026-11-22 (M7, Actions tab menu item list): What: the desktop Actions tab (Field Properties) no longer takes a free-text menu name for the Execute a menu item action. It offers a choice of five items, Next page, Previous page, First page, Last page and Print, which write `NextPage`, `PrevPage`, `FirstPage`, `LastPage` and `Print`, the list `form_set_actions` and `link_named_action` use (re-exported from the engine as `SAFE_MENU_NAMES`). The choice is kept in its own `ActionDraft` field (`menu`), so text typed for another kind is not touched; `ActionDraft::action` refuses any other name with `Choose one of the listed menu items`. A refusal is now kept in the dialog (`FieldDraft::action_error`) and drawn every frame, as a live region, until the next edit or a successful Add or Update, instead of for one frame; this widens to the existing refusals of the same tab (`Enter a URL`, `Enter a page number`, `Enter the field names`), which are announced the same way. The Select Trigger, Select Action and Menu item combo boxes are tied to their labels for assistive technology. A menu action already in a file with another name is still loaded and listed, and left as it is (the tab only writes it back unchanged) until the user deletes it or adds a new action on the same trigger, with the note `Not on the safe menu list; kept as it is` after the action's text, with or without a chain. Why: #170 left the desktop tab writing any typed string (`Quit`, `SaveAs`, a misspelt name) as a menu action, which the tool refused. Cost: no new dependency; a few lines in `crates/ui-egui/src/tools/prepare/tabs.rs` and `draft.rs`, one `pub use` in the engine, new English strings `Menu item:`, `First page`, `Last page`, `Choose one of the listed menu items` and `Not on the safe menu list; kept as it is` (English only; `Next page`, `Previous page` and `Print` are already used elsewhere). Tested: three new non-GPU tests in `tools::prepare::draft::tests` (each of the five names becomes the action with its own distinct label, whatever the text; `SaveAs`, `print`, `Quit`, a trailing-space name and an empty name are refused; the menu choice leaves the text of a web link alone) and one in `crates/forms/src/tests.rs`, `an_off_list_menu_action_left_as_it_is_survives_a_set_that_replaces_another_trigger` (a `Quit` action on Mouse Exit is the same object after a set that replaces Mouse Up). Mutations: none. Not mutated: everything. Not tested: the drawn tab, the combo boxes by keyboard, the accessibility tree, the announced message and the note on an off-list action, loading a file with an off-list name in the app, and the `edit.action-named` parity entry (note updated, no evidence added). The refusal message cannot be reached from the combo box; it guards `ActionDraft::action`. Ran: `cargo fmt --check` (exit 0); `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-ui-egui --all-targets -- -D warnings` (exit 0, on the final tree, no `-A` allowance); `cargo check -p pdfcraft-ui-egui --tests` (exit 0); `cargo test -p pdfcraft-forms --lib an_off_list_menu_action` (1 passed); `cargo test -p pdfcraft-ui-egui --lib menu_` (12 passed, including the 3 new `tools::prepare::draft::tests` tests; the first run of it failed 2 of them, which found that `ActionDraft::action` still read the typed text for a menu item, and that was fixed before the final run). Failed: nothing, on the final tree. - 2026-11-21 (M9, signature seed values, headless): What: `signature_seed_values` (new, headless) reads, sets and removes the seed values of a signature field: its `/SV` dictionary (ISO 32000-2, signature fields), which tells whoever signs what the field's author wants the signature to be. This tool models four of them: `/Reasons` (text strings to pick from), `/SubFilter` (signature formats allowed, names), `/DigestMethod` (digest algorithms allowed, names) and `/AddRevInfo` (a boolean: revocation information must be embedded), and the `/Ff` flags that make an entry required and not only suggested (a set bit for each: bit 2 for `/SubFilter`, bit 4 for `/Reasons`, bit 6 for `/AddRevInfo`, bit 7 for `/DigestMethod`, counted from 1, so the values 2, 8, 32 and 64). Arguments: `doc`, `field` (the field's name, as `form_fields` lists it), and one of nothing (read), any of `reasons`, `sub_filters`, `digest_methods`, `add_rev_info` and `required` (set), or `remove` true. A set writes a new direct dictionary (`/Type /SV`, `/Ff` only when something is required, and the entries given) in the field, replacing `/SV`; a removal takes `/SV` out of the field's dictionary; an indirect `/SV` dictionary that is replaced or removed is left in the file unreferenced. A set is deliberately narrow: `reasons` is 1 to 16 text strings of 1 to 127 characters, none twice; `sub_filters` is 1 to 4 names of `adbe.pkcs7.detached`, `adbe.pkcs7.sha1`, `adbe.x509.rsa_sha1` and `ETSI.CAdES.detached`, `digest_methods` is 1 to 3 names of `SHA256`, `SHA384` and `SHA512` (both compared byte for byte, none twice, so there are at most 4 and 3); `add_rev_info` true is refused when `sub_filters` is given and holds neither `adbe.pkcs7.detached` nor `adbe.pkcs7.sha1` (revocation information is not assumed to be embeddable by `adbe.x509.rsa_sha1` or `ETSI.CAdES.detached`; with no sub-filter named it is accepted); an explicitly empty array for any argument is refused by the tool (leave it out); `required` names entries (`sub_filter`, `reasons`, `add_rev_info`, `digest_method`), each of which must be given in the same call and none twice; a set with no entry is refused (use `remove`). A set replaces the whole dictionary, so it is refused while the field's `/SV` holds a key this does not write (`/Filter`, `/V`, `/Cert`, `/MDP`, `/TimeStamp`, `/LegalAttestation`, `/LockDocument`, `/AppearanceFilter` or any other key besides `/Type`, `/Ff` and the four; counted in the read as `other_entries`, null values included); a removal is not refused for them and drops them too. The tool refuses, before anything is written: a `field` that names no field, names a field that is not a signature field, or names more than one field (a read is refused too); `remove` combined with a seed value; a signed field (its `/V` is not null), and an object that is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page (found by object number). A removal of seed values the field does not have is no change (`changed` false, no write, no undo step). A write is one undo step and needs permission to modify the document. A read returns `seed_values`: `has_entry` (`/SV` is there and not null), `present` (a dictionary after references), `flags` (`/Ff` when an integer after references), `required` (the four entries whose bit is set), `reasons`, `sub_filters` and `digest_methods` (each `items`, the strings or names among the first 16 entries after references in file order, strings cut at 127 characters and names at 127 bytes, any name listed and not only the ones a set accepts; `count`, the entries the array has; `truncated`, it has more than 16 so the rest were not looked at; `skipped`, entries among the first 16 that are not of the right kind), `add_rev_info` (when a boolean after references), `other_entries`, `exact` (false when a listed reason was cut at 127 characters or a name was cut at 127 bytes or is not valid UTF-8; a reason that is not valid text is shown as it decodes and not detected; true when none is listed), `signed` (`/V` is read from the field's own dictionary only; one inherited from a parent is not counted), `settable` and `capped` (the allowance of reads was spent and what follows was not read). The tool does not check whether the document already has signatures that a change to this field would invalidate (a certification, or a signature that locks this field, can make the validator flag a change after signing); check sign_list afterwards. It does not apply them: `crates/sign` does not read `/SV`, so sign_document does not offer a reason or hold a signature to a field's values, and the validator does not check a signature against them; PDF/A is not checked either (a set does not look for the PDF/A identification). Why: `sign.seed-values` was planned with nothing behind it, and the spec-parity row for seed values said none. A headless agent preparing a form for signature could not say what a field asks of its signer, or set it, and a file made elsewhere with seed values could not be read. A read, set and remove of the four common seed values is the data the seed-value tab of Field Properties edits, it has a clear definition in the standard, and the module follows `signature_field_lock` (the sibling `/Lock` of the same field). It is read, set and remove only, of four values: no UI, no application of the values when signing and no check when validating, so the entry is partial. Cost: A read has one allowance of 10,000 reads of the field's own, shared by everything the read makes and charged before each `Document::get` is made, and when it cannot be paid for the read stops with `capped`: one for the field's object, up to 33 for each reference followed (as `Document::resolve` follows them, the first object and up to 32 more, each hop charged) for the `/SV` entry, `/Ff`, `/AddRevInfo` and each of the three lists, and one more for each list entry looked at, charged before the entry is looked at. At most 16 entries of each list are looked at, so the real limits cost at most 1 + 33 + 33 + 3 * (33 + 16 * 34) + 33 = 1,831 reads and cannot spend the allowance, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`). Counting the keys of `/SV` for `other_entries` is an iteration over the dictionary that makes no read and is not charged. Separate from that, whether the field is a structural object is decided by the page-tree walk of `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance of 2,000,000 reads (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`) charged before each read, and whose first read, of the catalog, is not charged; it runs once for a read of a field that is a dictionary, holds no signature and was read within the allowance, so such a read makes one walk (a read that was capped, of a signed field or of an object that is not a dictionary makes none). A call that sets or removes seed values that are there makes three (the tool's check, the edit's check and the answer), a removal of none that are there makes two (the check and the answer; the edit is not applied), and a redo applies the edit again, which makes one more; each walk is preceded by a read of the field's dictionary, and an applied edit also copies the field's dictionary once in `Document::update_dict`. The list of the form's fields comes from the form reader under its own limits, which this tool does not charge; the tool's name check is a comparison in memory. Strings are cut at 127 characters before they are kept and entries past 16 are counted and not looked at; no stream is decoded. A set allocates its at most 23 strings or names (16 reasons, 4 sub-filters and 3 digest methods), checked first, and one dictionary with the arrays; that is not charged against the allowance. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/signature_seed.rs`, 394 lines), one new edit and one new tool (`crates/automation/src/forms.rs`: 94 lines added and 1 removed), 310 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (eight tests and their helpers) and 325 in `crates/automation/tests/it/signature_seed_values.rs` (six tests). Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `signature_seed_values_are_read_with_their_lists_flags_and_other_entries` (no seed values; a null is no entry; a number is an entry and no dictionary; a dictionary with `/Ff`, `/Reasons`, `/AddRevInfo` behind references, a reason behind a reference, a number among the reasons and a string among the sub-filter names skipped, a name a set would refuse listed, `/Cert` and a null `/Filter` counted as other entries and `/Type` and `/Ff` not; `/Ff` 74 read as `/SubFilter`, `/Reasons` and `/DigestMethod` required; an `/Ff` that is a string and an `/AddRevInfo` that is a number read as none; a field with a `/V` that is a reference is signed and not settable and one whose `/V` is null is not signed), `signature_seed_values_are_set_replaced_removed_and_survive_a_save` (a set writes a direct `/SV` dictionary in the field with `/Type` and an `/Ff` of 72, leaves the old indirect one as it was, and after a full save and reopen reads back every list, the flag, `/AddRevInfo` false and a reason with a non-ASCII letter; a second set with nothing required writes no `/Ff` and drops `/Reasons`, `/DigestMethod` and `/AddRevInfo`; a removal takes only `/SV` out and keeps the field's other entries; a second removal is `NoChange` and the saved bytes are equal), `a_set_is_refused_over_seed_values_it_would_lose_and_a_removal_is_not` (a set over an `/SV` that holds a `/Cert` is refused with its own reason and writes nothing, a removal of it is allowed, and a set afterwards works), `signature_seed_values_are_refused_on_a_signed_field_and_on_structural_objects` (the signed field is reachable from the catalog, so a save keeps it and the byte comparison guards it; the catalog, the `/Pages` node, an inner node, a page, a signed field, a number and a missing object are each not settable and refused for a set and for a removal, with the saved bytes equal after the refused writes, and a signed field says why), `every_read_signature_seed_values_make_is_paid_for_before_it_is_made` (a field with its `/SV` and a `/SubFilter` list behind references and two entries: with exactly the 5 reads it takes it is whole, with 4 the second entry is missing, with 3 the first is missing and the count is known, with 2 the list read was not paid for, with 1 the dictionary read was not, with 0 the field was not; `change_with` refuses with the allowance as the reason with 0, 1 and 4 reads; an `/SV` behind a chain of 32 references is followed with 34 reads and one of 33 is not), `signature_seed_lists_cut_long_reasons_and_check_what_they_write` (the default read is exact; 16 reasons are listed whole and 19 are listed as 16 with a count of 19 and `truncated`, the last listed being the 16th; a reason of 127 characters is exact and one of 128 is cut and not exact; a sub-filter name of 200 bytes is cut at 127 and not exact and a digest name that is not valid UTF-8 is not exact), `signature_seed_values_are_checked_before_they_are_written` (16 reasons accepted and 17 refused; no entry at all, an empty reason, a repeated reason and a reason of 128 characters refused and one of 127 accepted) and `signature_seed_names_and_required_entries_are_checked_before_they_are_written` (every name of the two allowlists accepted, and an upper-case sub-filter, `sha256`, `SHA1`, an unknown name and a repeated name refused; `add_rev_info` true refused with only `adbe.x509.rsa_sha1` or only `ETSI.CAdES.detached`, accepted with `adbe.pkcs7.detached`, with `adbe.pkcs7.sha1` beside the CAdES name, with no sub-filter, and `add_rev_info` false accepted with `adbe.x509.rsa_sha1`; a required entry that is not given refused for each of the four, or required twice; `Entry::parse` and the four bit values). In `crates/automation/tests/it/signature_seed_values.rs` (a one-page file with two signature fields saved, reopened and read back from the saved bytes): `seed_values_are_read_set_saved_removed_undone_and_redone` (the full empty read, a set of all four values with two required and the saved file read back, the other signature field untouched, a second set that replaces the first and writes no `/Ff`, a removal, a second removal that changes nothing and leaves the saved bytes equal, three labelled undo steps and a fourth that finds none, and a redo that restores the first set; the saves pin the clock), `a_call_that_cannot_be_applied_is_named_and_changes_nothing` (24 bad arguments and refusals each named, among them an empty array for each of the four list arguments and revocation information with a sub-filter that does not allow it, and a list of 17 reasons, with no write and no undo step), `a_signed_field_is_read_but_not_edited_and_an_unsigned_one_still_is` (a field given a reason, signed with `sign_document`, reopened: its seed values are read, a set and a removal are refused as a signed field, no undo step, and the other field can still be set and the file saved with both; the signature's status afterwards is not asserted), `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_write_and_still_reads`, `a_file_made_elsewhere_is_listed_by_what_it_holds_and_a_set_over_unmodelled_entries_is_refused` (a hand-made file with 20 reasons, one of them a number, an unmodelled sub-filter name, a `/Cert`, a `/Filter` and `/Ff` 66: the first 16 entries looked at give 15 reasons, a count of 20, `truncated`, 1 skipped, the unmodelled name listed, `required` read from the flags and 2 other entries; a set is refused with no undo step and a removal works) and `a_name_two_fields_share_is_refused_rather_than_guessed` (a read, a set and a removal are each refused). The fixture of the last two is a file whose cross-reference table is written with its real offsets. Mutations: 32 run on the final code (no code line was edited after them; after them only the parity notes, the docs, ROADMAP and this text changed), and all 32 failed a test and none survived. An earlier run of 24 of them on the tests as they were then let two survive (a required `/AddRevInfo` that did not have to be given, and a reason limit of 128 characters, which the test compared through the constant); two assertions were added, then clippy asked for a collapsed `if let` and a smaller test function, and the set was run again; a review then added a rule for `add_rev_info`, a refusal of empty arrays in the tool and two more tests, clippy asked again for a smaller test function, and the whole set, with three mutations for those, was run once more on the code after that, which is the run counted here. 26 in the organize module, against the eight organize tests whose names contain `seed` (all new): the bit of `/SubFilter` moved, no charge for a list entry, a null `/V` counted as signed, `/Ff` counted among the other entries, `truncated` at 16 entries, a set allowed over one other entry, `/Ff` written when nothing is required, the allowlists compared without case, a required `/AddRevInfo` that need not be given, a reason limit of 128 characters, a removal of nothing counted as a change, a removal refused over other entries, skipped entries not counted, a repeated reason accepted, the exactness of a name ignored, `capped` never set, `/Type` not written, the required entries not folded into `/Ff`, the structural objects settable, the exactness of a string ignored, an entry required twice accepted, an empty reason accepted, a repeated digest method accepted, the `add_rev_info` rule dropped, `adbe.pkcs7.sha1` not counted as a PKCS#7 name, and `required` read without the `/DigestMethod` bit; and 6 in the tool and the edit, against the six tests of `signature_seed_values.rs`: a field that is not a signature field accepted, `remove` accepted with seed values, empty arrays accepted, `changed` always true, `flags` not reported and the undo label changed. The mutations were run by two scripts that apply each one, run the tests that name the module or the tool, and put the file back; the final gates were run after the last one, on the restored files. Survived: none. Not mutated: the allowance of 10,000 reads (tested only through `read_with` and `change_with` with small ones, and the real limits cost at most 1,831, see Cost), the 33 reads of a reference chain (`Gets::follow` in `crates/organize/src/reference_xobjects.rs`, shared and covered by the chain test here), the walk of the page tree cut short, the bit values of `/Reasons`, `/AddRevInfo` and `/DigestMethod` (asserted by the last organize test but mutated only for `/SubFilter`), the contents of the two allowlists (the organize test loops over the constants, so a name taken out of one would not fail it; the tool test uses `adbe.pkcs7.detached`, `SHA256`, `SHA384` and `SHA512` as literals), the read of `/AddRevInfo` and of `/Reasons` taken one at a time, the tool's own check of the names in `required` (its message is asserted but the check was not mutated), the schema's `maxItems`, `Document::signature_seed` and `Document::signature_seed_change` when a document has no editor, the permission check of the edit in `check_permission` (the modify-restriction test covers it but it was not mutated), the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (the undo label the test reads comes from the match, which was mutated), the re-exports in `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`, and the tool, parity and spec-parity texts. Not tested: the bit numbers of `/Ff` against a file written by another product (they are taken from the specification as read, and no fixture here was made elsewhere with `/Ff` set), a removal on a PDF/A file (nothing checks it), an `/SV` or a list that is a stream, a negative or very large `/Ff`, a field whose widget is a kid of the field (every field here has its widget merged into its dictionary, so a seed value on the field object with a separate widget is not covered), an `/V` inherited from a parent field (`signed` reads the field's own `/V` only, so a signed field whose `/V` is inherited reads as unsigned), whether the validator flags a signature after a seed-value change on a file that already has signatures (`sign_list` is not read in the tests), a reason that is not valid text (shown as it decodes and not detected, so `exact` stays true), a seed value read by Acrobat or another viewer when signing (the tool does not apply them, and nothing here signs against them), a page-tree walk over its limits, a read that spends its allowance of 10,000 reads (not reachable), and the user interface (none). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (without any `-A` allowance, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 389 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 894 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 72 in other targets passed (2 ignored); automation: 80 library tests passed, 708 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests in each); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; `cargo xtask i18n-report` (it skipped: no craft-i18n checkout); the 32 mutations above (all 32 failed a test). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests and the applications' tests (not touched: the app does not use the new engine functions; `cargo xtask wasm` compiled the ui-egui library with the new edit), the optimize and preflight crates' tests (not touched), the sign crate's tests (not touched; `sign_document` is only called by a test here), the translations of the new undo label (i18n-report had no craft-i18n checkout to read), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading a file this tool changed, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-21 (M12, screen annotation media action, headless): What: `screen_actions` (new, headless) reads and removes the sound, movie, rendition and rich-media actions of screen annotations, and never sets one: a screen annotation is a dictionary whose `/Subtype` is `Screen` (the area of a page where a rendition plays), and its `/A` is the action that starts a clip; the four kinds are `/S` `Sound`, `Movie`, `Rendition` or `RichMediaExecute` (compared byte for byte; a rendition action can carry a `/JS` script). Arguments: `doc`, and `remove` (a position from 0 in the listing; leave it out to read). The file is scanned by object number (`Document::scan_objects`), not through the pages, so a screen annotation that no page lists is listed too and one written as a direct dictionary inside another object is not; only a dictionary (not a stream) whose `/Subtype` is a direct name `Screen` counts. A read returns `screen_actions`: `count`, `truncated` (more than 256 were found; the first 256, in object-number order, are listed), `capped` and `items` with `index`, `object`, `has_entry` (`/A` is not null), `present` (`/A` is a dictionary after references), `action` (the `/S` name, cut at 127 bytes; any kind of action is named), `exact` (false when it was cut or is not valid UTF-8), `kind` (`sound`, `movie`, `rendition` or `rich_media_execute`, null for any other action), `has_script` (a rendition's `/JS` is stored and not null; the script is not read), `has_next` (a `/Next` chain), `has_additional_actions` (the annotation has an `/AA`; it is only reported) and `removable`. An `/S` that is an indirect reference is not followed and reads as no kind. There is no set and no argument that could write one, because a document written by this tool must not start playback or carry a script, and a rendition needs media clips and players this crate does not build. A removal takes `/A` out of the annotation, with any `/Next` chain it has (the chain goes with the action), only when `/S` is one of the four names; an annotation with another kind of action (a JavaScript or web action included, which are kept), a null `/A`, no `/A`, an `/S` that is not a direct name, or a position with nothing listed (also 256 and more) is `changed` false: nothing is written and no undo step is added. The annotation's `/AA`, appearance, `/MK`, the rest of its dictionary, the page's `/Annots`, the rendition or clip an action named and an indirect old `/A` dictionary are left as they are (an indirect `/A` stays in the file unreferenced); the result's `removed` says what went (`action`, `kind`, `next`). A write is one undo step (`Edit::RemoveScreenAction`, labelled Remove screen annotation media action, now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document. A removal is refused, before anything is written, for an object that is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page (compared by object number) and when the page-tree walk was cut short, and when the scan could not find the position within its allowance. It does not play anything, does not read a rendition (`/R`, media clips, player requirements), and does not check for PDF/A, which does not allow sound, movie or rendition actions (this tool can take them out, and says nothing about whether a file then conforms). Sibling tools that exist already: `link_media_action` reads and removes the same four kinds of action on a link annotation (it reads and removes, and sets none), `link_launch_action`, `link_named_action`, `link_embedded_action` and `link_thread_action` read, set and remove other kinds of action on a link, and `reference_xobjects` has the same scan-by-object-number shape. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::screen_action` (`read_screen_actions`, `screen_action_change`, `remove_screen_action`, `MAX_LISTED`, `MAX_GETS`), run by the new edit, with `Document::screen_actions` and `Document::screen_action_change`. `misc.legacy-multimedia` (Legacy multimedia (screen annotations, renditions)) moves from planned to partial and its notes say what is missing; the Screen, Movie, RichMedia, 3D row and the Sound, Movie, Rendition, RichMediaExecute row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` now say what is there. Why: `misc.legacy-multimedia` was planned with nothing behind it. A headless agent could not see that a screen annotation would start a rendition, a sound or a movie, or run a rendition's script, or take that out of a copy, and `link_media_action` only covers links. The screen annotation is where a rendition action normally sits, its `/A` has a small definition in the standard, and a removal can be tested in a saved file by what the annotation's `/A` holds. Only reading and removing the action are done, so the entry is partial and its title is only partly met: renditions are not read, nothing creates or deletes a screen annotation, no viewer here plays anything, and there is no UI. Cost: each scan (`scan`, used by `read_screen_actions`, `screen_action_change` and `remove_screen_action`) has one allowance of 2,000,000 reads (`MAX_GETS`), charged before each read is made: one for each object scanned (the last is paid for only when it is there to read) and, for each screen annotation, up to 33 for the reference chain behind `/A` (the first object and up to 32 more, as `Document::resolve` follows them, each hop charged); when it cannot pay the scan stops with `capped`, and an annotation whose chain was not fully paid for is not listed. A rendition's `/JS`, `/Next` and `/AA` are looked at where they are written and make no read. Not charged by this module: the first read of the catalog in a page-tree walk, and the read `Document::update_dict` makes of the annotation when a removal writes. The page-tree walk that decides whether an object is a structural one is `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`), charged before each read; it runs once for a read when something is listed and once for a change check or a removal that found its position. Through the tool, with `remove`, a call makes a scan before the edit (to say what goes), one for the change check and one for the final read, so a read makes 1 scan and, only when something is listed, 1 walk, and a removal through the tool makes: with a position of 256 or more, 2 scans (the check and the edit make none, the position is refused as no entry before scanning) and 2 walks when something is listed (0 when nothing is); with a position below 256 where nothing is listed, 3 scans and 2 walks when something is listed (0 when nothing is); at an annotation with no media action, 3 scans and 3 walks; and at one whose action goes, 4 scans and 4 walks (the same three, and the applied edit's own). Each scan reads the whole file's objects, so a call on a very large file does up to four passes over it. A name is kept cut at 127 bytes, and at most 256 annotations are listed. No content, media or script stream is decoded (`Document::scan_objects` does decode object streams, to reach the objects in them). No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/screen_action.rs`, 245 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 226 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (four tests) and a new `crates/automation/tests/it/screen_actions.rs` (247 lines, four tests). Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `screen_annotations_are_listed_with_what_their_action_holds` (the four kinds; `/Next`, a rendition's `/JS` and an `/AA` noticed, `/JS` on a movie not counting, null `/JS`, `/Next` and `/AA` reading as none; six other actions (`URI`, `JavaScript`, `ImportData`, `sound`, `Sounds`, `GoTo3DView`) listed and not media; the action behind a reference; an `/S` behind a reference reading as no action; no `/A`, a null `/A` and an `/A` that is a number; a 300-byte `/S` cut at 127 and not exact, and an `/S` that is not valid UTF-8 not exact; a link, a widget, a stream, a `/Subtype` behind a reference and a `/Subtype` that is a string not listed; positions counted from 0 in object-number order), `a_screen_annotations_media_action_is_removed_with_its_chain_and_survives_a_save` (a removal over an action behind a reference takes `/A` out, keeps `/AA`, `/T`, `/Rect` and the `/Subtype`, leaves the old object, keeps the other annotations at their positions with their actions, and reads back after a full save; a second removal is `NoChange` and the saved bytes are equal; a `/Next` chain goes with the action; a JavaScript action, a web action whose `/Next` is a sound action, an indirect `/S`, a null `/A`, no `/A`, and positions 8, 255, 256, 1000 and `usize::MAX` are `NoChange` or `NoEntry` and leave the saved bytes equal, each annotation reachable from the catalog so a change would show), `a_screen_annotation_that_is_a_structural_object_is_refused` (the catalog, the `/Pages` node, an inner node and a page, each marked as a screen annotation with a media action, are listed, not removable and refused, the reason containing the words page tree, with the saved bytes equal and `/A` kept; an ordinary one is removable) and `the_screen_annotation_listing_stops_at_256_and_every_read_is_paid_for_first` (257 annotations: 257 counted, 256 listed, `truncated`, and position 256 `NoEntry`; a scan with exactly as many reads as there are objects lists the last annotation and with one fewer is `capped` with nothing listed; a change check is allowed with the first and refused naming the allowance with the second; an action behind a reference needs one more read and is not listed without it; chains of 32 references are followed with 33 reads and one of 33 is not, and with 32 reads the scan is `capped`); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/screen_actions.rs`, through `screen_actions`: `the_screen_annotations_are_read_removed_saved_undone_and_redone` (a file whose page lists five screen annotations and a link with a sound action: the link is not listed; the listed kinds, a rendition's whole item including `has_script`, `has_next` and `has_additional_actions`, a URI action as `action` with no kind, an annotation with no `/A`; a read leaves no `changed`; three removals found in the saved file as no `/A` while the `/AA` and the URI action stay, `removed` naming the action, kind and `/Next`; removals of a URI action, no action and positions 5, 6, 256 and 1,000,000 `changed` false with saved bytes equal and an action already out `changed` false; three undo steps labelled Remove screen annotation media action, the saved bytes after the last equal to the file before the first removal, and three redos equal to the file saved after the removals; the clock is pinned), `there_is_no_way_to_set_a_media_action` (calls with extra arguments `kind`, `action`, `script` and `set` write nothing and add no undo step, the schema's only properties are `doc` and `remove`, and the description says there is no set), `a_bad_argument_is_named_and_changes_nothing` (a negative, a string and a fractional `remove` named; `i64::MAX` and a null `remove` leave the file as it was) and `a_screen_annotation_that_is_a_page_keeps_its_action_and_a_modify_restriction_holds` (a copy protected against changes refuses a removal that would change it, still reads, and a removal that finds no media action is `changed` false; a page object marked as a screen annotation with a sound action is listed first, not removable, refused with the words page tree, with the saved bytes equal and no undo step). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. The hostile-argument sweep in `crates/automation/tests/it/hostile_args.rs` runs over every tool in the table, including this one. Mutations: 19 run on the final code (no code line was edited after them; after them only the parity notes, the docs, ROADMAP and this text changed), and all 19 failed a test, 18 of them counted: 15 in the organize module, against the four organize tests whose names contain `screen_annotation` (all new), the `Movie` kind taken out, the `/Subtype` not checked, a stream accepted as a screen annotation, `has_script` set for any kind of action, a null `/Next` counted as present, a removal that also takes `/AA` out, an annotation with no media action counted as a change, the structural check dropped from the change check, the structural check dropped from the read, the read of each object not paid for, `exact` never cleared, `truncated` never set, a scan stopped by its allowance reported as no entry, the listing not cut at 256 and an `/A` behind a reference not followed; and 3 in the tool, against the four tests of `screen_actions.rs`, `removed` never reported, a call that changes nothing reporting `changed` true and a refusal swallowed. The mutations were run by two scripts that apply each one, run the tests of that crate that name the module or the tool, and put the file back; the final gates were run after the last one, on the restored files. The nineteenth, which lower-cased the `/S` before matching, failed but is not counted: it stopped every kind from matching and so did not test the byte-for-byte comparison. Survived: none. Not mutated: the allowance of 2,000,000 reads (tested only through `read_with` and `change_with` with small ones), the 33 reads of a reference chain (`Gets::follow` in `crates/organize/src/reference_xobjects.rs`, shared and covered by the chain test here), the walk of the page tree cut short (`cut` in `target` and `read_with`), a byte-for-byte comparison of the kinds against a case-insensitive one (the other-name cases `sound` and `Sounds` are tested but a true case-insensitive mutation was not run), the guard that sends a position of 256 or more to no entry (a position of 256 would also find nothing in the listing, so it is not told apart), the tool's result fields other than `removed`, `changed` and the ones the rendition item asserts, the check that `remove` is 0 or more (its message is asserted but the check was not mutated), `Document::screen_actions` and `Document::screen_action_change` when a document has no editor, the permission check of the edit in `check_permission` (the modify-restriction test covers it but it was not mutated), the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (its text is asserted by the undo test but was not mutated), and the re-export in `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`. Not tested: a removal on a PDF/A file (nothing checks it), a screen annotation written as a direct dictionary inside another object (not listed, and no test says so), an `/S` that is an indirect reference through the tool (tested only in the organize module, where it is listed with no kind and a removal changes nothing), an `/A` that is a stream, a screen annotation shared by two pages, a page-tree walk over its limits, a file with more objects than the allowance of 2,000,000 reads, a rendition's `/R` or a sound action's `/Sound` stream (no stream is read), an action followed or removed by another viewer, and the optimizer's handling of these actions. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (without any `-A` allowance, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 381 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 894 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 72 in other targets passed (2 ignored); automation: 80 library tests passed, 702 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests in each); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; `cargo xtask i18n-report` (it skipped: no craft-i18n checkout); the 19 mutations above (18 counted). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests and the applications' tests (not touched: the app does not use the new engine functions; `cargo xtask wasm` compiled the ui-egui library with the new edit), the optimize and preflight crates' tests (not touched), the translations of the new undo label (i18n-report had no craft-i18n checkout to read), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading a file this tool changed, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-21 (M12, link media action, headless): What: `link_media_action` (new, headless) reads and removes the sound, movie, rendition and rich-media actions of a link, and never sets one: an action dictionary in the link annotation's `/A` whose `/S` is `Sound`, `Movie`, `Rendition` or `RichMediaExecute` (compared byte for byte; the first two are deprecated in PDF 2.0, and a rendition action can carry a `/JS` script). Arguments: `doc`, `page` and `index` (the link, as `link_list` gives them, 1-based), and `remove` true or nothing (read). A read reports what is stored: `is_media` and `kind` (`sound`, `movie`, `rendition` or `rich_media_execute`), the `/S` name as `action` (any kind of action is named; `exact` false when it or a movie's operation is cut at 127 bytes or is not valid UTF-8), a movie's `/Operation` name (`operation`, when it is a name after references), a rendition's `/OP` (`rendition_op`, when it is an integer after references; its range is not checked), `has_script` (a rendition's `/JS` is stored and not null; the script is not read), `has_target` (a movie's `/Annotation` or `/T`, a rendition's `/AN` or a rich-media action's `/TA` is stored and not null; the value is not followed, so a reference counts as present), `has_next` (a `/Next` chain), `destination` (the link also has a `/Dest`) and `settable`. An `/S` that is an indirect reference is not followed and reads as no action. There is no set and no argument that could write one, because a document written by this tool must not start playback or carry a script, and a rendition needs media clips and players this crate does not build. A removal takes `/A` out of the link, with any `/Next` chain it has (the chain goes with the action: a media action reached only through another action's `/Next` is not touched), only when `/S` is one of the four names; a link with no action, with another kind of action (a JavaScript, a launch, a web or a menu action included, which are kept), with a null `/A` or with an `/S` that is not a direct name is `changed` false: nothing is written and no undo step is added. The link's `/Dest` and the rest of the link are kept, the screen, movie or rich-media annotation an action points at is not touched, and an indirect old `/A` dictionary is left in the file unreferenced; the result's `removed` says what went (`action`, `kind`, `next`). A write is one undo step (`Edit::RemoveLinkMediaAction`, labelled Remove link media action, now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document. The tool refuses, before anything is written: a link that does not exist or is written in the page and has no object, and an object that is not a link annotation or is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page (compared by object number). It does not play anything, and does not check for PDF/A, which does not allow sound, movie or rendition actions (this tool can take them out, and says nothing about whether a file then conforms; the preflight crate already lists the sound, movie and rendition actions as forbidden). Sibling tools that exist already: `link_launch_action`, `link_named_action`, `link_embedded_action` and `link_thread_action` read, set and remove other kinds of action, `link_run_javascript` runs a link's script, `link_add` and `link_edit` write web, page and remote-document links, and `link_list` reports a link with one of these actions as `other` (the `/S` name) unless the link also has a `/Dest`, which it shows instead. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::media_action` (`read_media_action`, `media_action_change`, `remove_media_action`, `MAX_GETS`), run by the new edit, with `Document::link_media_action` and `Document::link_media_action_change`. `edit.action-media` (Play sound / media / rich-media actions) moves from planned to partial and its notes say that only reading and removing exist; the Sound, Movie, Rendition and RichMediaExecute row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` now says what is there (GoTo3DView stays none). Why: `edit.action-media` was planned with nothing behind it, and the spec table said none. A headless agent could not see that a link would play a sound or a movie, start a rendition or run a rich-media command, or take that out of a copy. The four actions have a small definition in the standard, and a removal can be tested in a saved file by what the link's `/A` holds. Only reading and removing are done, so the entry is partial and its title (playing) is not met: no viewer here plays anything, nothing creates a media action or a clip, and there is no UI. Cost: each call of `read_media_action` and `media_action_change` has one allowance of 10,000 reads (`MAX_GETS`), charged before each `Document::get` is made, and stops with `capped` (a read) or a refusal that names the allowance (a change) when it cannot pay: one for the link and up to 33 for each reference chain behind `/A` and behind the one entry read of the action (a movie's `/Operation` or a rendition's `/OP`; the other kinds read none), as `Document::resolve` follows them (the first object and up to 32 more, each hop charged), so at most 1 + 2 * 33 = 67 reads, far below the allowance, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`, `change_with`); a change check needs nothing more than that read. `remove_media_action` runs a change check and then writes. Separate from that and not charged by this module: the page-tree walk that decides whether the object is a structural one is `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`), charged before each read, and whose first read, of the catalog, is not charged; it runs once for each read of an object that is a link: once for a read, 3 times for a removal through the tool that changes nothing (the read before the edit, the check and the answer) and 4 times for one that changes the link (those three and the applied edit), counted by a temporary counter in `structural` that was taken out again. The checks of `/Next`, `/Dest`, `/JS`, `/AN`, `/TA`, `/Annotation` and `/T` look at the stored value and make no read. The tool finds the link with `Document::links`, which reads the annotations of every page under the limits of `crates/annot/src/links.rs`. A name is kept cut at 127 bytes. No stream is decoded. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/media_action.rs`, 234 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 237 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (four tests) and 228 in `crates/automation/tests/it/link_media_action.rs` (four tests). Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_media_action_is_read_with_its_kind_and_entries` (the four kinds; a movie's operation, `/T` and `/Annotation` as target and a `/Next`; a rendition's `/OP`, `/JS` and `/AN`, an `/OP` that is a real, a name or a string reading as none; a movie operation that is a string reading as none and a 300-byte one cut at 127 and not exact; `/JS` on a movie not counting; null `/JS`, `/AN`, `/OP` and `/Next` reading as none; a rich-media `/TA`; nine other names (`URI`, `JavaScript`, `Launch`, `Named`, `GoToE`, `sound`, `Sounds`, `RichMedia`, `SetOCGState`) reported as `action` and not media; the action behind a reference; an `/S` behind a reference reading as no action; a link with only a `/Dest`, a null `/A` and an `/A` that is a number; a widget, a number and a missing object reading as not a link), `a_media_action_is_removed_with_its_chain_and_survives_a_save` (a removal over an action behind a reference takes `/A` out, keeps `/Dest`, `/Border` and the `/Subtype`, leaves the old object, and reads back after a full save; a second removal is `NoChange` and the saved bytes are equal; all four kinds go with a `/Next` chain; a JavaScript, a launch, a web action whose `/Next` is a sound action, an indirect `/S`, a null `/A` and a link with no action are `NoChange` and leave the saved bytes equal, with each link reachable from the catalog so a change would show), `a_media_action_is_refused_on_what_is_not_a_link` (the catalog, the `/Pages` node, an inner node, a page, a widget, a number and a missing object refused for a removal with the saved bytes equal; a page marked as a link and carrying a media action still refused, the reason containing the word page, and its `/A` kept) and `every_read_a_media_action_makes_is_paid_for_before_it_is_made` (a direct movie action is read with exactly 1 read and with 0 not even the link; an action and an operation behind references need 3 reads in all and with each one fewer the next field in that order is not read; a rendition's `/OP` the same; a sound action needs only the link's read; chains of 32 references are followed with 1 + 33 reads and one of 33 is not; the same for an operation; a change check is allowed with 1 read and refused naming the allowance with 0, and with a reference to the action it needs 2); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/link_media_action.rs`, through `link_media_action`: `a_media_action_is_read_removed_saved_undone_and_redone` (reads of a sound, a movie with a `/Next`, a rendition with a script and a rich-media action with a `/Dest`, and of a JavaScript action as not media; `link_list` reporting `other` Sound, Movie and Rendition and a page destination for the link that also has a `/Dest`; a read leaving the saved bytes equal; four removals found in the saved file as no `/A`, no `/Next` and the `/Dest` kept where there was one; a removal of a JavaScript action, of a web action and of a link with no action `changed` false, with a sound action behind a web action's `/Next` kept in the saved file; four undo steps labelled Remove link media action, the saved bytes after the last equal to the file before the first removal, and four redos equal to the file saved after the removals; the clock is pinned), `there_is_no_way_to_set_a_media_action` (calls with extra arguments `kind`, `operation` and `script` write nothing and add no undo step, and the schema's only properties are `doc`, `index`, `page` and `remove`), `a_call_that_cannot_be_applied_is_named_and_changes_nothing` (a `remove` that is not a boolean, a link and a page that do not exist, and an inline link for a removal and for a read; saved bytes equal and no undo step) and `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_removal_and_still_reads` (a copy protected against changes refuses a removal, still reads, and a removal of nothing is `changed` false). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. The hostile-argument sweep in `crates/automation/tests/it/hostile_args.rs` runs over every tool in the table, including this one. Mutations: 15 run on the final code (no code line was edited after them; after them only the parity notes, the docs, ROADMAP and this text changed), and all 15 failed a test: 13 in the organize module, against the four organize tests whose names contain `media_action` (all new), the `Sound` kind taken out, the kind matched case-insensitively, a removal that also takes `/Dest` out, a link with no media action counted as a change, a null `/Next` counted as present, a rendition's `/OP` read without following a reference, a movie's `/Operation` read without following a reference, a rendition's `/JS` not noticed, the structural check dropped from `settable`, `exact` not cleared for a cut operation, the `/Subtype` of the link not checked, a movie's `/T` ignored as a target and the read of the link not paid for; and 2 in the tool, against the four tests of `link_media_action.rs`, `removed` never reported and a removal that reports `changed` false after writing. The mutations were run by a script that applies each one, runs the tests of that crate that name the tool or the module, and puts the file back; the four organize tests passed again after the last one. Survived: none. Not mutated: the allowance of 10,000 reads (tested only through `read_with` and `change_with` with small ones), the 33 reads of a reference chain (`Gets::follow` in `crates/organize/src/reference_xobjects.rs`, shared and covered by the chain tests here), the walk of the page tree cut short (`!cut_short` in `settable`), the tool's result fields other than `removed` and `changed`, `Document::link_media_action` and `Document::link_media_action_change` when a document has no editor, the permission check of the edit in `check_permission` (the modify-restriction test covers it but it was not mutated), the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (its text is asserted by the undo test but was not mutated), and the re-exports in `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`. Not tested: a removal on a PDF/A file (nothing checks it), a rendition `/OP` outside 0 to 4 (read as the integer it is), an `/S` that is an indirect reference through the tool (tested only in the organize module, where it reads as no action and a removal changes nothing), a rich-media action in `link_list` without a `/Dest` (the one tested has one), a link object that is itself a reference to another object (it reads as not a link), an `/A` or `/Annots` array that is a stream or behind a reference chain on a page, a link shared by two pages, a page-tree walk over its limits, an action followed or removed by another viewer, the optimizer's handling of these actions, and a sound action whose `/Sound` stream is present (the test files have none; no stream is read). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (without any `-A` allowance, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 377 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 894 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 72 in other targets passed (2 ignored); automation: 80 library tests passed, 698 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests in each); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; `cargo xtask i18n-report` (it skipped: no craft-i18n checkout); the 15 mutations above. After an amendment that changed only text (the tool's description in `crates/automation/src/tools.rs`, the parity notes and the docs), `cargo fmt --check`, clippy on the three crates and the automation tests were run again; the organize and engine tests, the mutations, layers, assets, wasm and i18n-report were not run again (no code line changed; the organize and engine crates do not depend on the description).
