- 2026-11-21 (M7, link menu action, headless): What: `link_named_action` (new, headless) reads, sets and removes the menu-item action of a link: an action dictionary `/S /Named` in the link annotation's `/A` (ISO 32000-2 12.6.4.12), whose `/N` is a name that tells a viewer to run one of its menu items. The standard defines four, `NextPage`, `PrevPage`, `FirstPage` and `LastPage`; PdfCraft's form buttons run those four and `Print` and `SaveAs` (`crates/ui-egui/src/canvas/area.rs`); the list a set accepts is the first five of those six names. `SaveAs` is left out because it opens a dialog and is not one of the standard names, and `Print` is on the list although PDF/A allows only the four page names (a set does not check for PDF/A). The app ran the safe list for a form button but nothing could put such an action on a link, and no tool read one: `link_list` reports a link with this action only as `other: "Named"`, and clicking it says the action is unsupported. Arguments: `doc`, `page` and `index` (the link, as `link_list` gives them, 1-based), and one of nothing (read), `name` (exactly one of `NextPage`, `PrevPage`, `FirstPage`, `LastPage`, `Print`; nothing else is written) or `remove` true. A set replaces the link's whole `/A` with a new direct dictionary (`/Type /Action`, `/S /Named`, `/N`) and takes `/Dest` out, which a link with an action must not have; a `/Next` chain or any other entry of the old action is not kept, and an indirect old `/A` dictionary is left in the file unreferenced; the result's `replaced` says what was there (`action`, `has_entry`, `destination`, and `next`, whether a `/Next` chain went with it). A removal takes `/A` out of the link only when it is a named action, whatever its `/N`; a link with no action, or with another kind of action, is `changed` false: nothing is written and no undo step is added. A set that repeats what the link already has is written again, as one more undo step. The tool refuses, before anything is written: `remove` combined with `name`, a `name` that is not on the list, a link that does not exist or is written in the page and has no object, and an object that is not a link annotation or is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page (compared by object number; a link object that is itself a reference to another object reads as not a link). A write is one undo step (`Edit::SetLinkNamedAction`, labelled Set link menu action, now in `EDIT_LABELS`; a removal has the same label) that needs permission to modify the document, so a copy protected against changes refuses a write and still reads. The result is `page`, `index` and `named_action`: `has_entry` (an `/A` that is not null), `present` (it is a dictionary after references), `action` (the name of `/S`, cut at 127 bytes and shown with replacement characters when not valid UTF-8, or null), `exact` (neither `/S` nor `name` was cut or invalid UTF-8), `is_named`, `name` (`/N` when it is a name after references, cut like `action`, whatever name it is; a `/N` that is a string or anything else reads as null), `safe` (the name is whole and on the list a set accepts), `has_next` (the action has a non-null `/Next`), `destination` (the link has a `/Dest` that is not null), `settable` (it is a link and not one of the structural objects above, that walk was not cut short, and the read was not `capped`) and `capped` (the read's allowance, below, was spent); a call that writes also has `changed`, and a set that wrote has `replaced`. It does not run the action: nothing in the viewer follows a link's named action, the names of Acrobat's other menu items are not offered, no other kind of action is read, and there is no Link Properties page for it. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::named_action` (`read_named_action`, `named_action_change`, `set_named_action`, `NamedTarget`, `SAFE_NAMES`), run by the new edit, with `Document::link_named_action` and `Document::link_named_action_change`. Parity: the `form_set_actions` tool already writes `/S /Named` for a form field with any `menu` name it is given, with no safe list; that is not changed here. `edit.action-named` (Execute-a-menu-item action, safe list) moves from planned to partial with the tool and the tests, because the data of the action can now be made, read and removed on a link; the action is not run for a link and there is no UI, so it is not shipped. The Named row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` now says what is there for a link. Why: `edit.action-named` was planned with nothing behind it, although the entry's safe list already ran for form buttons. A headless agent could not give a link a Next page, Previous page, First page, Last page or Print action, or even see that a link had one. The action has a small, fixed definition in the standard, needs nothing else in the document, and can be tested in a saved file by what the link's `/A` holds. It is read, set and remove only: no viewer behaviour for links, no UI, no menu item outside the five, so the entry is partial. Cost: each call of `read_named_action` and `named_action_change` has one allowance of 10,000 reads (`MAX_GETS`), charged before each `Document::get` is made, and stops with `capped` (a read) or a refusal that names the allowance (a change) when it cannot pay: one for the link, up to 33 for the reference chain behind `/A` and up to 33 for the one behind `/N` (as `Document::resolve` follows them, the first object and up to 32 more, each hop charged), so at most 1 + 33 + 33 = 67 reads, far below the allowance, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`, `change_with`); a change check needs nothing more than that read. `set_named_action` runs a change check and then writes. Separate from that, whether the object is a structural one is decided by the page-tree walk of `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance of 2,000,000 reads (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`) charged before each read, and whose first read, of the catalog, is not charged; it runs once for each read of an object that is a link. A read call makes one walk; a call that sets a link makes four (the read of the link's old action for `replaced`, the tool's check, the edit's check and the answer), a removal of a named action makes three, a removal of nothing makes two (the check and the answer; the edit is not applied), and a redo applies the edit again, which makes one more; each walk is preceded by a read of the link's dictionary, and an applied edit also copies the link's dictionary once in `Document::update_dict`. Not charged by this module: the tool finds the link with `Document::links`, the link list the other link tools use, which reads the annotations of every page under the limits of `crates/annot/src/links.rs` and is kept until the document changes; and nothing else reads. An action name and a menu name are kept, each cut at 127 input bytes (a name that is not valid UTF-8 is shown with replacement characters, so what is shown can be longer than that). No stream is decoded. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/named_action.rs`, 224 lines), one new edit and one new tool (59 added lines and 2 removed, the import, in `crates/automation/src/links.rs` and 9 added in `crates/automation/src/tools.rs`), 211 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (four tests) and 199 in `crates/automation/tests/it/link_named_action.rs` (three tests). Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_named_action_is_read_with_its_menu_item_and_whether_a_set_accepts_it` (each of the five names of the list read as safe and `SaveAs` and `nextpage` as not safe; a name behind a reference, with a `/Next` chain and a `/Dest` beside the action; an action with no `/N` and a null `/Next`; an `/N` that is a string reading as no name; a name of 128 bytes cut at 127, not exact and not safe; the action behind a reference; a web action whose `/N` is not read; a link with a `/Dest` and no action; a null `/A` and a null `/Dest`; the names `Name`, `Namedx` and `N` read as other actions; a widget, a number and a missing object reading as not a link, not settable and not a named action), `a_named_action_is_set_replaced_removed_and_survives_a_save` (a set over an indirect web action and a `/Dest` writes a direct `/Action` dictionary with `/S /Named` and `/N`, takes `/Dest` out, leaves the old action object as it was and keeps the rest of the link, and after a full save and reopen reads back `PrevPage`; each of the five names is written and read back; a set over an action with a `/Next` chain drops it; a removal takes only `/A` out; a second removal is `NoChange` and the saved bytes are equal; a named action with the name `Quit` is removed; a link with a web action is `NoChange` for a removal and keeps it; `NamedTarget::new` refuses the empty string, `Quit`, `nextpage`, `NextPage ` with a space, `SaveAs` and `Full Screen`), `a_named_action_is_refused_on_what_is_not_a_link` (the catalog, the `/Pages` node, an inner node, a page, a widget, a number and a missing object are each not settable and refused for a set and for a removal, with the saved bytes equal after the refused writes; a page that is marked as a link is still refused and the reason contains the word page) and `every_read_a_named_action_makes_is_paid_for_before_it_is_made` (a direct action and name are whole with exactly 1 read and with 0 not even the link is read; an action behind a reference needs 2 and a name behind a reference 3, and with one fewer the name, or the action, is not read and the read is not settable; an action behind a chain of 32 reference objects is followed with 1 + 33 reads and one behind 33 is not followed, and with 1 + 32 reads both are capped; the same for a name behind a chain of 32; a change check for a set and for a removal is allowed with 1 read, refused naming the allowance with 0, and a removal of an action behind a reference is refused with 2); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/link_named_action.rs`, through `link_named_action`: `a_named_action_is_read_set_saved_removed_undone_and_redone` (a read of a link with a web action and of one with a named action and a `/Next` chain; a set of `NextPage` whose `replaced` says the web action and no `/Next`, found in the saved file as `/S /Named /N /NextPage`, and listed by `link_list` as `other` Named; a link with a `/Dest` losing it, with `replaced` saying a destination; a link with nothing getting `FirstPage`; a link with a `/Next` chain getting `PrevPage` with `replaced` saying the chain went and the saved file having none; a removal that takes `/A` out, and a second removal `changed` false with the saved bytes equal; five undo steps all labelled Set link menu action, the unchanged call adding none, the saved bytes after the last undo equal to the file before the first set, and five redos equal to the file before them), `a_call_that_cannot_be_applied_is_named_and_changes_nothing` (nine refusals by message: `remove` with `name`, a `name` of `Quit`, of `nextpage` and of the empty string, a `name` that is a number, a `remove` that is a string, a link and a page that do not exist, and a link written in the page; the saved bytes equal and no undo step) and `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_write_and_still_reads` (a copy protected against changes refuses a set and still reads; a removal where there is nothing to remove is `changed` false rather than a refusal). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. The hostile-argument sweep of `crates/automation/tests/it/hostile_args.rs` runs over every tool in the table, including this one. Mutations: 23, restoring after each, all run on the final code (the module, the tool and the tests were not edited after; only the docs and the commit text changed after the gates): 22 failed a test and 1 survived. In the organize module, 19 (each run against the four organize tests whose names contain `named_action`): `/S` always read as a named action, `/Dest` not taken out by a set, a removal of a link with no named action counted as a change, a removal that takes out `/Dest` instead of `/A`, the cut of a name not making the read inexact, every name safe, any name accepted as a target, `/Type` not written, the structural objects settable, a null `/Next` counted, a null `/Dest` counted, a null `/A` counted, `/N` not written (`Print` written for every target), a read cut short still settable, `/S` matched by its first letter, an object that is not a link settable, `/Next` never reported, an allowance of one more read than given, and the walk of the page tree cut short ignored: 18 failed a test and the last one survived. In the tool, 4 (each run against the 3 automation tests): a removal of nothing reported as a change, `replaced` never reported, the name dropped from the target (`Print` always), and `remove` accepted with `name`; all 4 failed a test. History, for the record: on an earlier run, before the read test was given a null `/Dest` and the names `Name`, `Namedx` and `N` (fixtures, not only assertions), two of the organize mutations (a null `/Dest` counted and `/S` matched by its first letter) survived, which is why those were added; every mutation was then run again on the final code. Survived: the walk of the page tree cut short ignored (`!cut_short` dropped from `settable`): it needs a page tree over its limits, which no test here builds. Not mutated: the allowance of 10,000 reads (tested only through `read_with` and `change_with` with small ones; the real limits cannot reach it, see Cost), the 33 reads of a reference chain (`Gets::follow` in `crates/organize/src/reference_xobjects.rs`, shared with that module and covered by the chain tests here), the tool's result fields other than `changed` and `replaced`, `Document::link_named_action` and `Document::link_named_action_change` when a document has no editor, the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (no test in the crates run here reads the list), the re-exports in `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`, the schema, and the tool and parity texts. Not tested: an action dictionary whose `/S` is behind a reference (it reads as no action), a set on a link when the document is a PDF/A file and the name is `Print` (nothing checks it), a set that repeats what the link already has (it writes again and adds an undo step; no test does it), a name that is not valid UTF-8, a link object that is itself a reference to another object (it reads as not a link, with no reason given), an action or `/Annots` array that is a stream or behind a reference chain on a page, a link whose object is shared by two pages, an action written by this tool read by Acrobat or another viewer, running the action (the viewer does not follow it on a link), the user interface (none), and a read that spends its allowance of 10,000 reads (not reachable). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (without any `-A` allowance, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 361 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 894 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 72 in other targets passed (2 ignored); automation: 80 library tests passed, 687 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests in each); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; `cargo xtask i18n-report` (each of these with exit status 0, the tests with the one failure named below; i18n-report printed "Translation gaps: skipped, built without craft-i18n", so it checked nothing about the new label "Set link menu action", which is English only). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests and the applications' tests (not touched: the app does not use the new engine functions; `cargo xtask wasm` compiled the ui-egui library with the new edit; the existing `buttons_reset_navigate_and_print`, which the parity note cites for form buttons, was not run), the translations of the new undo label (i18n-report had no craft-i18n checkout to read), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading an action this tool wrote, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-21 (M12, link article action, headless): What: `link_thread_action` (new, headless) reads, sets and removes the article action of a link: an action dictionary `/S /Thread` in the link annotation's `/A` (ISO 32000-2 12.6.4.7), which tells a viewer to go to an article thread of the document and, optionally, to one of its beads. `/D` is the thread (the thread dictionary as an indirect reference, its position in the catalog's `/Threads`, or the title of the thread as a text string) and `/B` the bead (the bead dictionary, or its position along the thread); an `/F` (a file specification) puts the thread in another file. PdfCraft listed and removed article threads (`article_threads`) but nothing could point a link at one, and no tool read such an action: `link_list` reports a link with this action only as `other: "Thread"`. Arguments: `doc`, `page` and `index` (the link, as `link_list` gives them, 1-based), and one of nothing (read), `thread` (1 to 256: the `index` that `article_threads` lists plus 1, the position in `/Threads`) with an optional `bead` (1 to 256, the position along the thread from its `/F` along `/N`), or `remove` true. A set replaces the link's whole `/A` with a new direct dictionary (`/Type /Action`, `/S /Thread`, `/D` as the reference of the thread object and, for a bead, `/B` as the reference of the bead object) and takes `/Dest` out, which a link with an action must not have; a `/Next` chain or any other entry of the old action is not kept, and an indirect old `/A` dictionary is left in the file unreferenced; the result's `replaced` says what was there (`action`, `has_entry`, `destination`, and `next`, whether a `/Next` chain went with it). A removal takes `/A` out of the link only when it is a thread action; a link with no action, or with another kind of action, is `changed` false: nothing is written and no undo step is added. A set that repeats what the link already has is written again, as one more undo step. The tool refuses, before anything is written: `remove` combined with `thread` or `bead`, `bead` without `thread`, a `thread` or `bead` outside 1 to 256, a link that does not exist or is written in the page and has no object, an object that is not a link annotation or is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page (compared by object number; a link object that is itself a reference to another object reads as not a link), a `thread` that is not an entry of `/Threads` that is an indirect object whose object is a dictionary, and a `bead` that the thread does not have among its first 256 (a thread whose `/F` is not a reference to a dictionary has none). A write is one undo step (`Edit::SetLinkThreadAction`, labelled Set link article action, now in `EDIT_LABELS`; a removal has the same label) that needs permission to modify the document, so a copy protected against changes refuses a write and still reads. The result is `page`, `index` and `thread_action`: `has_entry` (an `/A` that is not null), `present` (it is a dictionary after references), `action` (the name of `/S`, cut at 127 bytes and shown with replacement characters when not valid UTF-8, or null), `exact`, `is_thread`, `remote` (the action has a non-null `/F`, so its thread is in another file and `thread` and `bead` are not read), `title` (when `/D` is a text string, the string cut at 127 characters; it is not matched against the titles of the threads, so `thread` stays null), `has_next` (the action has a non-null `/Next`), `thread` and `bead` (1-based positions that `/D` and `/B` name, among the first 256 entries of `/Threads` and the first 256 beads, as a reference or as an integer position in range; null when `/D` or `/B` is missing, names none of them, is a title or a direct dictionary, or the action is remote), `destination` (the link has a `/Dest` that is not null), `settable` (it is a link and not one of the structural objects above, that walk was not cut short, and the read was not `capped`) and `capped` (the read's allowance, below, was spent); a call that writes also has `changed`, and a set that wrote has `replaced`. It does not run the action: nothing in the viewer goes to the thread or bead, no thread or bead is added, no other kind of action is read, a title-named or remote `/D` is reported and not resolved, and there is no Link Properties page for it. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::thread_action` (`read_thread_action`, `thread_action_change`, `set_thread_action`, `ThreadTarget`), run by the new edit, with `Document::link_thread_action` and `Document::link_thread_action_change`. Parity: `edit.action-thread` (Read-an-article action) moves from planned to partial with the tool and the tests, because the data of the action can now be made, read and removed; the action itself is not run and there is no UI, so it is not shipped. The Thread row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` (which said none) now says what is there. Why: `edit.action-thread` was planned with nothing behind it. The article threads of a document could be listed and removed, and the one object that makes a thread useful to a reader, a link that goes to it, could not be made or even read by a headless agent. The action has a small, fixed definition in the standard, it is the only place where `/Threads` is referred to by position or by reference, and it can be tested in a saved file by what the link's `/A` names. It is read, set and remove only: no viewer behaviour, no UI, no threads or beads made, so the entry is partial. Cost: each call of `read_thread_action` and `thread_action_change` has one allowance of 10,000 reads (`MAX_GETS`), charged before each `Document::get` is made, and stops with `capped` (a read) or a refusal that names the allowance (a change) when it cannot pay: one for the link, up to 33 for each reference chain followed (as `Document::resolve` follows them, the first object and up to 32 more, each hop charged), one for the catalog, one for the thread's dictionary and one for each bead walked, at most 256 beads, each once. A read of a link that is a thread action naming a thread and a bead therefore costs at most 1 + 33 + 1 + 33 + 1 + 256 = 325 reads, and a change check that sets one 325 + (1 + 33 + 1 + 256) = 616, so the real limits cannot spend the allowance, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`, `change_with`). `set_thread_action` runs a change check and then reads the target again with an allowance of its own (at most 291 reads). At most 256 entries of `/Threads` are looked at, compared as references with no read of their own, and at most 256 bead references and one action name of at most 127 bytes are kept. Separate from that, whether the object is a structural one is decided by the page-tree walk of `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance of 2,000,000 reads (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`) charged before each read, and whose first read, of the catalog, is not charged; it runs once for a read of an object that is a link. A read call makes one walk; a call that sets a link makes four (the read of the link's old action for `replaced`, the tool's check, the edit's check and the answer), a removal of a thread action makes three, a removal of nothing makes two (the check and the answer; the edit is not applied), and a redo applies the edit again, which makes one more; each walk is preceded by a read of the link's dictionary, and an applied edit also copies the link's dictionary once in `Document::update_dict`. Not charged by this module: the tool finds the link with `Document::links`, the link list the other link tools use, which reads the annotations of every page under the limits of `crates/annot/src/links.rs` and is kept until the document changes; and nothing else reads. Argument checks of the tool allocate nothing of note. No stream is decoded. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/thread_action.rs`, 355 lines), one new edit and one new tool (69 added lines in `crates/automation/src/links.rs` and 10 in `crates/automation/src/tools.rs`), 319 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (seven tests and their helpers) and 226 in `crates/automation/tests/it/link_thread_action.rs` (three tests). Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_thread_action_is_read_with_its_thread_and_bead` (a thread named by reference and a bead by reference; positions as integers; an integer thread out of range or negative, a thread named by the reference of a bead and a name each reading no thread and so no bead; a bead of another thread not found; a thread action with no `/D`; a web action whose `/D` is ignored; a link with a `/Dest` and no action; a null `/A` and `/Dest`; the action behind a reference; an entry of `/Threads` that is not a reference not blocking the next one; a widget, a number and a missing object reading as not a link, not settable and not a thread action), `a_thread_action_is_set_replaced_removed_and_survives_a_save` (a set writes a direct `/Action` dictionary with `/S /Thread` and the bead's reference, takes `/Dest` out, leaves the old indirect action as it was and keeps the rest of the link, and after a full save and reopen reads back thread 0 and bead 1; a target with no bead drops the old `/B`; a removal takes only `/A` out; a second removal is `NoChange` and the saved bytes are equal; a link with a web action is `NoChange` for a removal and keeps it), `a_thread_action_is_refused_on_what_is_not_a_link_and_for_a_target_that_is_not_there` (the catalog, the `/Pages` node, an inner node, a page, a widget, a number and a missing object are each not settable and refused for a set and for a removal, with the saved bytes equal after the refused writes; a page that is marked as a link is still refused and the reason names the page; a thread position with no entry, an entry that is not a reference, an entry that is a number, a bead the thread does not have, and a thread with no beads are each refused with the bytes equal; a catalog with no `/Threads` refuses), `what_a_thread_target_accepts` (255 and 256 for a thread, 255 and 256 for a bead), `every_read_a_thread_action_makes_is_paid_for_before_it_is_made` (with exactly the 5 reads it takes the link, thread and bead are whole; with 4 the second bead is not paid for, with 3 the first bead is not, with 2 the thread's dictionary is not, with 1 the catalog is not, with 0 not even the link; an action behind a chain of 32 reference objects is followed with 35 reads (the link, the 33 reads of the chain and the catalog) and one behind 33 is not followed, and with 33 reads, one short of the link and the chain, both are capped; a change check that sets a bead is allowed with exactly 5 reads, refused naming the thread with 4 and with 1, and naming the link with 0; a removal needs only the read of the link) and `a_remote_or_title_named_thread_action_is_reported_and_not_resolved` (an action with an `/F` reads as remote with no thread, bead or title even with `/D` 0 and `/B` 0; a null `/F` is no file; a `/D` string is reported as `title`, not matched against the thread of that title, and with no bead; a title of 128 characters is cut at 127 and not `exact`; a `/D` that is a direct dictionary names no thread; `has_next` is true for a thread action and a web action with a `/Next` and false for a null one; a read cut short after the link is not settable) and `a_ring_of_beads_with_a_loop_and_a_long_one_are_walked_once_and_at_most_256_beads` (a loop of beads that does not lead back to the first is walked once for each bead, so a fourth bead is refused; the 256th bead of a ring of 300 is reached and written, the 257th is not read; the 256th entry of `/Threads` is looked at and the 257th is not, by reference or by position); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/link_thread_action.rs`, through `link_thread_action`: `a_thread_action_is_read_set_saved_removed_undone_and_redone` (a read of a link with a web action; a set of the second thread whose `replaced` says the web action and no `/Next`, found in the saved file as `/S /Thread` naming the thread titled Second, and listed by `link_list` as `other` Thread; a set of the first thread and its second bead replacing that, the saved file naming the bead of width 20; a link with a `/Dest` losing it, with `replaced` saying a destination; a link with nothing getting an action; a removal that takes `/A` out, a second removal and a removal on a link with another kind of action both `changed` false with the other action kept and the saved bytes equal; five undo steps all labelled Set link article action, none added by the unchanged calls, the saved bytes after the last undo equal to the file before the first set, and five redos equal to the file before them), `a_call_that_cannot_be_applied_is_named_and_changes_nothing` (sixteen refusals by message: `remove` with `thread` or with `bead`, `bead` without `thread`, a `thread` of 0, 257 and the smallest integer, a `thread` that is a string, a `remove` that is a string, a `bead` of 0 and 257, a thread the document does not have, a bead the thread does not have (two cases), a link and a page that do not exist, and a link written in the page; the saved bytes equal and no undo step) and `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_write_and_still_reads` (a copy protected against changes refuses a set and still reads; a removal where there is nothing to remove is `changed` false rather than a refusal). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. The hostile-argument sweep of `crates/automation/tests/it/hostile_args.rs` runs over every tool in the table, including this one. Mutations: 31, restoring after each, on the final code (the module and the tool were edited after earlier runs, and every mutation was run again after the last edit of the file it changes; only the docs and the commit text changed after): 31 failed a test and none survived. In the organize module, 27 (each run against the organize tests whose names contain `thread` and the ring test): `/S` always read as a thread action, 257 beads walked, no visited set for beads, 257 entries of `/Threads` looked at, an integer position equal to the length accepted, `/Dest` not taken out by a set, a removal of a link with no thread action counted as a change, a removal that takes out `/Dest` instead of `/A`, a bead read made without being charged, `/B` not written, the structural objects settable, an object that is not a link read as one, a thread of 256 accepted, a bead of 256 accepted, `/Type` not written, a null `/Dest` counted as a destination, the catalog read without being charged, an entry that is not a reference matching any reference, an entry of `/Threads` that is not a dictionary accepted, a bead named in a thread with no `/F` accepted, an `/F` ignored, a null `/F` counted as a file, a title not reported, the cut of a title not making the read inexact, `/Next` never reported, a null `/Next` counted, and a read that was cut short still settable. In the tool, 4 (each run against the 3 automation tests): a removal of nothing reported as a change, the bead dropped from the target, `replaced` never reported, and `next` left out of `replaced`. Earlier, on a previous version of the code, one mutation survived and was fixed: 257 entries of `/Threads` looked at (a test now names the 257th entry by reference and by position), and one mutation was written so that it did not compile and was rewritten. Not mutated: the allowance of 10,000 reads (tested only through `read_with` and `change_with` with small ones; the real limits cannot reach it, see Cost), the 33 reads of a reference chain (`Gets::follow` in `crates/organize/src/reference_xobjects.rs`, shared with that module and covered by the chain test here), the cut-short walk of the page tree (which needs a tree over its limits), the argument ranges of the tool (1 to 256) other than the dropped bead, the value of `next` in `replaced` when it is true (the tests only see false) and the tool's `remote`, `title` and `has_next` fields (the organize tests read them), `Document::link_thread_action` and `Document::link_thread_action_change` when a document has no editor, the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (no test here reads the list; the undo label the tests read comes from the edit's own match), the re-exports in `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`, the schema, and the tool and parity texts. Not tested: a `/Next` chain on an old action (a set drops it, as said above, and no test puts one there), a set that repeats what the link has (it writes again and adds an undo step), a link object that is itself a reference to another object (it reads as not a link, with no reason given), an action or `/Annots` array that is a stream or behind a reference chain on a page, a link whose object is shared by two pages, an action written by this tool read by Acrobat or another viewer, the user interface (none), and a read that spends its allowance of 10,000 reads (not reachable). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (without any `-A` allowance, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 357 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 894 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 72 in other targets passed (2 ignored); automation: 80 library tests passed, 684 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests in each); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; `cargo xtask i18n-report` (each of these with exit status 0, the tests with the one failure named below; i18n-report printed "Translation gaps: skipped, built without craft-i18n", so it checked nothing about the new label "Set link article action", which is English only). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests and the applications' tests (not touched: the app does not use the new engine functions; `cargo xtask wasm` compiled the ui-egui library with the new edit), the translations of the new undo label (i18n-report had no craft-i18n checkout to read), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading an action this tool wrote, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-21 (M9, signature field lock, headless): What: `signature_field_lock` (new, headless) reads, sets and removes the lock of a signature field: its `/Lock` dictionary (ISO 32000-2 12.7.5.5, Table 236), which names the form fields that become read-only once the field is signed. `/Action` is `All` (every field of the form), `Include` (only the fields in `/Fields`) or `Exclude` (every field but those), and `/Fields` is an array of fully qualified field names as text strings, required for `Include` and `Exclude`. The signature validator already read these locks to judge changes made after signing (`sign.field-mdp`, `crates/sign/src/pdf/locks.rs`), but nothing could write one: a lock could only come from a file made elsewhere. Arguments: `doc`, `field` (the field's name, as `form_fields` lists it), and one of nothing (read), `action` (`all`, `include` or `exclude`) with `fields` (set), or `remove` true. A set replaces the field's `/Lock` with a new direct dictionary (`/Type /SigFieldLock`, `/Action`, and `/Fields` for `include` and `exclude`; the PDF 2.0 `/P` entry of an old lock is not kept, and a read does not report `/P`); a removal takes `/Lock` out of the field's dictionary; an indirect `/Lock` dictionary that is replaced or removed is left in the file unreferenced. The tool refuses, before anything is written: a `field` that names no field, names a field that is not a signature field, or names more than one field (two fields sharing a fully qualified name; a read is refused too); `remove` combined with `action` or `fields`, `fields` without `action`, an unknown `action`, `fields` with `all`, an `include` or `exclude` with no names or more than 256, a name that is empty, over 127 characters or listed twice, and a name that is neither the fully qualified name of a field of the form nor the name of a field above others (`address` for `address.city`), which covers the ones below it, as the validator reads it; a field that holds a signature (its `/V` is not null, so a lock read from the signed revision cannot be edited afterwards); and an object that is the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page, compared by object number. A removal of a lock the field does not have is `changed` false: nothing is written and no undo step is added. A write is one undo step (`Edit::SetSignatureLock`, labelled Set signature field lock, now in `EDIT_LABELS`; a removal has the same label) that needs permission to modify the document, so a copy protected against changes refuses a write and still reads. The result is `field` and `lock`: `has_entry` (a `/Lock` that is not null), `present` (it is a dictionary after references), `action` (the name written in the dictionary, cut at 127 bytes and shown with replacement characters when not valid UTF-8, or null), `fields` (the strings among the first 256 entries of `/Fields`, after references, each cut at 127 characters, in file order), `count` (the entries of `/Fields`), `truncated` (more than 256, so the rest were not looked at), `not_names` (entries among the first 256 that are not strings), `exact` (the action and every listed name are whole; true when none is listed, and also for the read of a document that cannot be edited), `signed`, `settable` (the field is a dictionary, holds no signature and is not one of the structural objects above, and that walk was not cut short) and `capped` (the read's allowance, below, was spent; a set or removal on such a read is refused with its own message that names the allowance, not the structural objects); a call that writes also has `changed`. It does not check whether the document already holds signatures that the new lock or the change to this field would invalidate (a signature that locks this field, or a certification, can turn invalid, as the test with a signed file shows): no warning is given, and the tool description says to check `sign_list` afterwards. It does not apply the lock: PdfCraft does not make the locked fields read-only after a signature, it reads no FieldMDP transform in a signature's `/Reference`, `sign_document` writes no lock, and there is no Signed tab in Field Properties. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::signature_lock` (`read_signature_lock`, `signature_lock_change`, `set_signature_lock`, `LockSpec`, `LockAction`), run by the new edit, with `Document::signature_lock` and `Document::signature_lock_change`; `Gets`, `Spent` and `structural` of `crates/organize/src/reference_xobjects.rs` are now `pub(crate)` and `Gets` has a `charge`. Parity: `form.props-signed` (Field properties: signed, lock fields when signed) moves from planned to partial with the tool and the tests, because the tool is the lock a signature field's Signed tab sets (all fields, all but some, only some); `sign.lock-after-signing` (Lock document or fields after signing) stays planned, since it covers the lock applied as part of signing and the document-level lock, which nothing here does, and `sign.field-mdp` is unchanged. The FieldMDP row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` (which said none, though validation reads locks) now says what is there.  Why: `form.props-signed` was planned with nothing behind it. A headless agent preparing a form for signature could not say which fields a signature locks, and a lock could be seen only after the signature was made. A read and a set of the lock is the data the Signed tab of Field Properties edits, it has a clear definition in the standard, and the validator that enforces it is already here, so it can be tested against a real signature: the tests sign a file and see the signature stay intact or become invalid depending on the lock the tool wrote. It is read, set and remove only: no UI, no `/P`, no FieldMDP transform and no change to how a locked field behaves in the viewer, so the entry is partial.  Cost: A read has one allowance of 10,000 reads of the field's own, shared by everything the read makes and charged before each `Document::get` is made, and when it cannot be paid for the read stops with `capped`: one for the field's object, up to 33 for each reference followed (as `Document::resolve` follows them, the first object and up to 32 more, each hop charged), and one more for each entry of `/Fields` looked at, charged before the entry is looked at. At most 256 entries are looked at, so the real limits cost at most 1 + 33 + 33 + 256 * 34 = 8,771 reads and cannot spend the allowance, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`). Separate from that, whether the field is a structural object is decided by the page-tree walk of `reference_xobjects::structural`, which has its own allowance of 2,000,000 reads (`reference_xobjects::MAX_GETS`) charged before each read, and whose first read, of the catalog, is not charged; it runs once for a read of a field that is a dictionary and holds no signature, so a read makes one walk. A call that sets or removes a lock that is there makes three (the tool's check, the edit's check and the answer), a removal of a lock that is not there makes two (the check and the answer; the edit is not applied), and a redo applies the edit again, which makes one more; each walk is preceded by a read of the field's dictionary, and an applied edit also copies the field's dictionary once in `Document::update_dict`. The list of the form's fields comes from the form reader under its own limits, which this tool does not charge; checking each name of a set against that list is a comparison in memory of up to 256 names against the fields, with no read. Strings are cut at 127 characters before they are kept and entries past 256 are counted and not looked at; no stream is decoded. A set allocates its at most 256 names, checked first, one dictionary and array of them, and in the tool a `format!` of one name and a dot for each name checked (at most 128 characters each) and the field's dictionary copy named above; none of these is charged against the allowance. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/signature_lock.rs`, 277 lines), one new edit and one new tool (82 lines in `crates/automation/src/forms.rs`), 247 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (seven tests and their helpers) and 287 in `crates/automation/tests/it/signature_field_lock.rs` (six tests).  Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_signature_lock_is_read_with_its_action_and_names` (no lock; a null is no entry; a number is an entry and no lock; a lock and its `/Fields` behind references listing a string, a string behind a reference and a number, read as two names, a count of 3 and `not_names` 1; an `All` lock listing nothing; an action that is not a name not reported; a field with a `/V` that is a reference is signed and not settable and one whose `/V` is null is not signed), `a_signature_lock_is_set_replaced_removed_and_survives_a_save` (a set writes a direct `/SigFieldLock` dictionary in the field, leaves the old indirect one as it was, and after a full save and reopen reads back the action and three names, one with a non-ASCII letter; an `All` set replaces it and drops `/Fields`; a removal takes only `/Lock` out and keeps the field's other entries; a second removal is `NoChange` and the saved bytes are equal), `a_signature_lock_is_refused_on_a_signed_field_and_on_structural_objects` (the catalog, the `/Pages` node, an inner node, a page, a signed field, a number and a missing object are each not settable and refused for a set and for a removal, with the saved bytes equal after the refused writes, and a signed field says why), `every_read_a_signature_lock_makes_is_paid_for_before_it_is_made` (a field with its lock and list behind references and two entries, the second behind a reference: with exactly the 6 reads it takes it is whole, with 5 the second entry's reference is not paid for, with 4 the second entry is not looked at, with 3 the first entry is not, with 2 the list is not read, with 1 the lock is not read, with 0 not even the field; a lock behind a chain of 32 reference objects is followed with 34 reads and a chain of 33 is not, one read short being capped) and `a_read_that_spent_its_allowance_is_refused_with_its_own_reason` (a set and a removal with 0 or 1 reads are refused naming the allowance and with 2 the set is allowed), `a_signature_lock_action_is_cut_and_the_default_read_is_exact` (the default read is exact; an action name of 200 characters is cut at 127 bytes and the read is not exact) and `a_signature_lock_lists_256_names_cuts_long_ones_and_checks_what_it_writes` (exactly 256 entries not truncated, 259 listed as 256 with a count of 259 and `truncated`, the last listed being the 256th in file order; a name of 127 characters whole and one of 128 cut and not `exact`; what `LockSpec::new` accepts and refuses: 256 names but not 257, an empty `include` or `exclude`, names with `all`, a repeated name, an empty name, 127 characters but not 128); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/signature_field_lock.rs`, through `signature_field_lock`: `a_lock_is_read_set_saved_removed_undone_and_redone` (a field with none reads exactly as empty; an `include` set of two names read back and found in the saved file with `/Type /SigFieldLock`, the other signature field untouched; an `all` set replaces it; a removal takes it out, a second removal is `changed` false with the saved bytes equal; three labelled undo steps and a fourth with nothing to undo, the saved bytes equal to before the first, and a redo equal to the first set), `a_call_that_cannot_be_applied_is_named_and_changes_nothing` (fifteen refusals by message: `remove` with `action`, `remove` with `fields`, `fields` without `action`, an unknown action, `all` with names, `include` with no names and `exclude` with an empty list, a repeated name, a name that is no field and one that is only the start of a field's name, `fields` that is a string, `remove` that is a string, a missing field, a text field, and a `field` that is only the parent of a field's name; 257 names; the saved bytes equal and no undo step), `a_signed_field_is_read_but_not_edited_and_an_unsigned_one_still_is` (a file is made with `form_add_field`, a lock set that excludes `address.city`, and the field signed with a new digital ID: the signed field's lock is still read with `signed` true and `settable` false, a set and a removal on it are refused naming the signature and add no undo step; the unsigned field of the signed file is locked and saved incrementally, with both locks in the file; when the signature's lock also excludes that field the signature is still intact (status unknown, the signer being untrusted), and when it does not, locking that field makes the signature invalid, so the lock the tool writes is the one the validator enforces), `a_lock_of_many_names_lists_the_first_256_and_counts_the_rest` (a hand-made file whose lock has 300 entries, a number, a name of 128 characters and 298 short ones: 255 names listed, the long one cut at 127, the last listed being the 256th entry in file order, a count of 300, `truncated`, `not_names` 1, `exact` false), `a_name_two_fields_share_is_refused_rather_than_guessed` (a hand-made file with two signature fields named Dup: a read, a set and a removal are refused naming the name, nothing is written) and `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_write_and_still_reads` (a copy protected against changes refuses a removal and still reads). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist.  Mutations: 43, restoring after each, on the final code (the run was repeated after the last code edits; only the description text of the tool, the docs and the commit text changed after it): 43 failed a test and none survived. In the organize module, 29 (each run against the organize tests of the module): no charge for an entry, a null `/V` counted as signed, `signed` never set, the structural objects settable, `truncated` at 256, 257 entries looked at, `not_names` not counted, the exactness of a name ignored, a cut that is always whole, the cut at 128 characters, `all` with names accepted, an empty list accepted, 257 names accepted, an empty name accepted, a 128-character name accepted, a repeated name accepted, `/Type` not written, `/Fields` written for `All`, `Include` written as `Exclude`, a signed field not refused, an object that is not settable not refused, a removal of no lock counted as a change, `has_entry` always true, the action not read, `capped` never set, the removal doing nothing, a read that spent its allowance not refused with its own message, the exactness of the action ignored, and the default read not exact. In the tool and the edit, 14 (each run against the 6 automation tests): the permission check, redone as the edit moved to an arm that allows it (dropping its arm does not compile, the match being exhaustive), a field that is not a signature field accepted, a name shared by two fields accepted, a name that matches no field accepted, a name that is only an ancestor refused, `include` mapped to `exclude`, the text of the `remove` with `action` refusal changed, `fields` without `action` accepted, `changed` not reported, `settable` reported as `signed`, `truncated` reported as `capped`, `count` reported as the number listed, `not_names` always 0, and a removal of no lock reported as a change. Earlier, on a previous version of the code, some mutations survived and were fixed: a signed field not refused (a test now reads the reason), a name shared by two fields accepted, `truncated` reported as `capped`, `count` reported as the number listed and `not_names` always 0 (tests added: the shared name and the 300-entry lock), and the refusal of a read that spent its allowance, which was unreachable then and is now reached through `change_with` with a small allowance. One run of the last mutation reported a survivor because its test filter did not match the new test's name; the test was run by hand against the mutation and failed it, which is the result counted.  Not mutated: the values of the limits (256 is tested through the constant in the module tests and as a literal in the tool test, and 127 through the constants and in the tests; the allowance of 10,000 reads is tested only through `read_with` with small ones, and the real limits cannot reach it, see Cost), the 33 reads of a reference chain (`Gets::follow` in `crates/organize/src/reference_xobjects.rs`, shared with that module and covered by the chain test here and by that module's tests, not mutated for this tool), the cut-short walk of the page tree (a field is not settable when the walk was cut, which needs a tree over its limits), a field that is a stream (its dictionary is edited like a dictionary), `Document::signature_lock` and `Document::signature_lock_change` when a document has no editor, the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (no test here reads the list; the undo label the tests read comes from the edit's own match), the re-exports in `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`, the schema, and the tool, parity and spec-parity texts.  Not tested: a lock's `/P`, a lock or `/Fields` that is a stream, a field whose widget is a kid of the field (every field here has its widget merged into its dictionary, so a lock on the field object with a separate widget is not covered), FieldMDP transform references in a signature's `/Reference` (not read by this tool), a lock written by this tool read by Acrobat or another viewer, the user interface (none), and a read that spends its allowance of 10,000 reads (not reachable).  Ran: `cargo fmt --check` (after `cargo fmt` had reformatted the new code); `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings` (without the `-A clippy::collapsible_match` allowance, exit 0); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 350 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 894 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 72 in other targets passed (2 ignored); automation: 80 library tests passed, 681 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests in each); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; `cargo xtask i18n-report` (each of these with exit status 0, the tests with the one failure named below; i18n-report printed "Translation gaps: skipped, built without craft-i18n", so it checked nothing about the new label "Set signature field lock", which is English only).  Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root).  Not run: ui-egui tests and the applications' tests (not touched: the app does not use the new engine functions; `cargo xtask wasm` compiled the ui-egui library with the new edit), the translations of the new undo label (i18n-report had no craft-i18n checkout to read), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading a lock this tool wrote, and the mutations listed under Not mutated.
