- 2026-11-21 (M12, test fix): What: the `setup` of six automation integration test files (`article_threads`, `class_map`, `named_destinations`, `portfolio_collection`, `reference_xobjects`, `role_map`) builds its `Automation` with a fixed clock, `with_clock(|| 1_700_000_000)`, as `form_bulk` already did. No product code changed. Why: a save stamps `/ModDate` (and the matching XMP date) from the wall clock to the second, and these tests compare the bytes of two saves of the same document ('a call that changes nothing writes nothing', 'undone equals before'). When the two saves straddled a second boundary the bytes differed by one digit in the compressed object stream (length 227 against 226) and the test failed, once in a full run and again in one of 40 runs of the two binaries alone, in `portfolio_collection::the_collection_is_read_removed_saved_undone_and_redone`, `reference_xobjects::a_call_that_changes_nothing_writes_nothing_and_a_bad_argument_is_named` and one more; the temp directories are per test, so no shared path or global state was involved. Cost: none at run time; the saved files of these tests carry one fixed date.  Tested: reproduced before the fix (run 10 of 40 of `it portfolio_collection reference_xobjects --test-threads=8` failed on the byte comparison at `reference_xobjects.rs:183`); after the fix 60 runs of the seven byte-comparing files (those six and `form_bulk`) at 8 threads, none failed. No new test: the existing tests are the regression tests and cannot fail on a pinned clock.  Mutations, 1, restoring after: a clock that adds one second on every read in `reference_xobjects` and `portfolio_collection` made 5 of their 8 tests fail, so the byte comparisons do depend on the clock and the fixed one is what keeps them equal.  Not mutated: the other tests of the six files, which do not compare saves or are covered by the same one-line `setup`.  Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test -p pdfcraft-automation --no-fail-fast`; `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`.  Failed, not related: `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` fails in this sandbox, which runs as root (a read-only folder does not stop root), and fails the same on an unchanged tree.  Not run: `cargo xtask i18n-report`, `cargo xtask wasm`, the other crates' tests, the application (no user-facing text, no product code). Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-21 (M12, portfolio collection, headless): What: `portfolio_collection` (new, headless) reads the document's portfolio collection, the catalog's `/Collection` dictionary that makes a PDF open as a portfolio of its embedded files (ISO 32000-2 7.11.6 and 12.3.5), and, given `remove` true, takes that entry out of the catalog. The application already used `/Collection` for the view (`doc_info` reports a portfolio's initial document and view, `organize.portfolio-view`), but nothing read its `/Schema`, the columns of the file list, or could end the portfolio. It is read and remove only: it adds no collection, changes no column, reads no `/Sort`, `/Folders` or file specification `/CI`, and extracts no file, so `organize.portfolio-extract` stays planned and the tool is evidence for the partial `organize.portfolio-view`. The result is `collection`: `present` (the entry is a dictionary after references), `has_entry` (the catalog has a `/Collection` that is not null, which is what `remove` takes out, so a `/Collection` that is a number or a dangling reference is an entry and no portfolio), `view` (`/View` when it is a name written in the dictionary and not through a reference, cut at 127 bytes; the specification has D, T, H and C, details, tile, hidden and custom), `initial_document` (`/D` when it is a string written in the dictionary, cut at 127 characters), `initial_document_cut` (`/D` was cut), `count` (the entries of `/Schema` other than `/Type`), `truncated` (more than 256, so the first 256 are listed), `capped` (the read's allowance, below, was spent) and `fields`, in file order: `key` (cut at 127 bytes), `is_field` (the entry is a dictionary after references; when false the rest is null or default), `name` (`/N`, a string after references, cut at 127 characters), `subtype` (a name written in the field), `order` (an integer written in the field), `visible` (`/V` when a boolean is written in the field, true when there is none), `editable` (`/E` likewise, false when there is none) and `exact` (false when `key` or `name` was cut, or when the key is not valid UTF-8 and is shown with replacement characters; a cut `subtype` or `view` is not flagged). With `remove` true the result also has `removed`: true when the catalog had the entry and it is gone, false when it had none, which is no change: nothing is written and no undo step is added. `remove` is a boolean; `false`, a null and no value only read, and a string or a number is refused (`remove must be true or false`). A removal changes only the catalog: an indirect `/Collection` dictionary is left in the file unreferenced, and the embedded files, `/Names` and the pages are not touched, so the file becomes an ordinary PDF whose embedded files are its attachments. Because only the catalog is written, a `/Collection` that is the same object as the `/Pages` node or a page is read as written and removed from the catalog without the object being changed, so there is no `removable` flag and no refused removal. One removal is one undo step (`Edit::RemoveCollection`, labelled Remove portfolio collection, which is now in `EDIT_LABELS`; with no catalog the edit fails with `OrganizeError::NoPageTree`, the error the other catalog edits use) that needs permission to modify the document; on a copy protected against changes a removal of a present entry is refused and the read still works. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::collection` (`read_collection`, `collection_removes`, `remove_collection`), run by the new edit, with `Document::collection` and `Document::collection_removes`. Parity: `organize.portfolio-extract` stays planned (its note says what is missing); `organize.portfolio-view`, already partial, gains the tool and the tests as tools and evidence and a note that `/Schema` is now read headless with no view built from it, so no entry changes status. The §7.11 row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` (which also wrongly said attachments cannot be added, deleted or described; `organize.attachments-add`, `-delete` and `-description` are shipped) and the portfolio row of `docs/file-format-parity.md` (which wrongly said there is no portfolio view; its header date is updated) now cite `organize.portfolio-view`. Why: `organize.portfolio-extract` is a planned P2 feature that extracts the files of a portfolio, and this slice does not do that, so it stays planned. What was missing next to it was a headless read of a portfolio's columns and a way to end the portfolio: an agent could not see which columns a portfolio defines or how its list is set up, and could not turn a portfolio into an ordinary file that shows its attachments. The Attachments panel of the application already lists and saves attached files (`view.attachments-panel`, tested by `crates/render/src/inspect/tests.rs::attachments_of_both_kinds_are_listed_and_extracted`); only the headless `attachment_*` tools do not save an attached file (they add, delete and describe). Extracting a portfolio's files headless would need a tool that saves an embedded file, a naming and overwrite policy and folders (`/Folders`), which is not small, so this slice is the read and the removal, which need no such model. `/Sort`, `/Folders` and `/CI` are not read because each needs its own model of a tree or of typed values. Cost: A read has one allowance of 100,000 reads, shared by everything the call reads (`/Collection`, `/Schema` and every column and name), charged before each `Document::get` is made, and when it cannot be paid for the read stops with `capped`; a column is listed only when all its reads were paid for, so the columns before the one that could not be read stay and that one is dropped whole. References are followed as `Document::resolve` follows them (the first object and up to 32 more, so at most 33 reads for one value, each charged), and each `/Schema` entry looked at costs one more read, charged before it is looked at. A column costs at most 1 + 33 (the entry behind a reference) + 33 (`/N` behind a reference) = 67, so with at most 256 columns listed a read is at most 33 + 33 + 256 * 67 = 17,218 reads: the real limits cannot spend the allowance of 100,000, which is a second guard and is tested only with small allowances (`read_with`). The catalog's own read is not charged (one in `read_collection`, one in `collection_removes`). Counting the entries of `/Schema` walks a dictionary that is already in memory (twice: once to count, once to list) and makes no read, so it is not charged. `/View`, `/D`, `/Subtype`, `/O`, `/V` and `/E` are read as written and cost no read. At most 256 columns are kept, each with a key of at most 127 bytes, a name of at most 127 characters and a subtype of at most 127 bytes, so the strings are cut before they are copied; every column past 256 is counted and not kept, and no stream is decoded. A call without `remove` reads once; a call with `remove` reads the catalog in the tool, again in the edit, copies the catalog dictionary once in `Document::update_dict`, and reads once more for the answer. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/collection.rs`, 194 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 216 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (five tests and their helpers) and 237 in `crates/automation/tests/it/portfolio_collection.rs`. Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `a_collection_is_read_with_its_view_first_file_and_columns` (a document with no `/Collection` reads as empty; a saved and reopened indirect collection with `/View` T and a `/D` string lists four `/Schema` entries with `/Type` not counted: a column written in the schema, one behind a reference whose `/N` is behind a reference and whose `/O` is 3, `/V` false and `/E` true, an entry that is a number (`is_field` false), and a dictionary whose `/N` is a number, `/V` and `/E` are integers and `/O` is a string, each with its key, name, subtype, order, `visible`, `editable` and `exact`; a `/View` and a `/D` behind references are not read; a `/Collection` that is a number or a dangling reference has an entry and is no portfolio, a null has no entry, and `collection_removes` follows `has_entry`), `removing_the_collection_takes_only_the_catalog_entry` (no entry: the saved bytes are equal; an indirect collection removed: the entry is gone, the page count is the same and a saved copy reads as no portfolio; a direct one removed and saved incrementally, reopened with none; a `/Collection` that is the `/Pages` node: the entry goes, the node keeps its `/Kids` and the pages stay), `the_collection_listing_is_bounded_and_counts_what_it_does_not_list` (exactly 256 columns not truncated; 300 listed as 256 with `truncated`, a count of 300 and the last listed one being the 256th in file order), `every_read_a_collection_makes_is_paid_for_before_it_is_made` (a collection and schema behind references and two columns, the second behind a reference with `/N` behind a reference: with exactly the 6 reads they take it is whole and not capped, with 5 the second column's `/N` read is not paid, so that whole column is dropped (one column listed) and it is capped, with 3 only the first column is listed, with 2 none, with 1 the collection is read and the schema is not, with 0 nothing is; a chain of 32 reference objects ends in the dictionary, which `Document::resolve` also reaches, and is paid for with 33 reads, one fewer being capped; a chain of 33 is not followed by either) and `a_collection_text_is_cut_at_127_characters` (127 characters whole, 128 cut and not `exact`, for a name and for a key, and `/D` cut at 127 with `initial_document_cut` set and a `/D` of 127 whole; a key that is not valid UTF-8 is not `exact`); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/portfolio_collection.rs`, through `portfolio_collection`: `the_collection_is_read_removed_saved_undone_and_redone` (a file with none reads exactly as empty; a file with a collection, three schema entries and an embedded file read exactly; `remove` false only reads and the saved bytes are equal; `remove` true with `removed` true, the saved file with no `/Collection` and its `/Names` still there, a second removal with `removed` false and the saved bytes equal and no undo step, undo with the label Remove portfolio collection and the saved bytes equal to before, redo with the same saved bytes as the first removal), `a_schema_of_many_columns_lists_the_first_256_and_counts_the_rest` (303 entries: 256 listed, a count of 303, `truncated` true, `capped` false, the 256th listed being the one in that position of the file; a direct `/Collection` with `/View` C and a `/D` of 128 characters read as `C`, 127 characters and `initial_document_cut` true, and one of 127 characters with it false), `a_call_that_changes_nothing_writes_nothing_and_a_bad_argument_is_named` (a document with none, and one whose `/Collection` is a null, `removed` false with the saved bytes compared before and after and no undo step; a `/Collection` that is a number reads as an entry and no portfolio and is removed; a string and an integer refused with `remove must be true or false`, a null read, the saved bytes equal and no undo step), `a_collection_that_is_a_page_tree_node_is_read_but_only_the_catalog_changes` (a `/Collection` that is the `/Pages` node read as written, removed from the catalog, the saved file with the node and its `/Kids`) and `a_modify_restriction_refuses_a_removal_and_still_reads` (a copy protected against changes refuses a removal of a present entry and still reads). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. Mutations, 41, restoring after each, on the final code: 41 failed a test and none survived. In the organize module, 30 (each run against the 5 organize tests): no charge before the first read of a value, no charge in a hop, the hops one short and one long, no charge for an entry, `/Type` counted and `/Type` listed, `truncated` at 256 and one extra column listed, `visible` false by default, `/V` ignored and `/E` ignored, the exactness of a name ignored and of a key ignored, `has_entry` always true, a null counted as an entry by `collection_removes`, the removal doing nothing, `is_field` always true, `capped` never set, `/O` ignored, `/Subtype` ignored, `/N` not read, `/View` ignored, `/D` ignored, `present` set before the dictionary check, the text limit 128, the allowance not stopping a read (a charge that never fails), `initial_document_cut` inverted and never set, and the UTF-8 check of `shown` in `crates/organize/src/role_map.rs` dropped (run against the 5 collection tests only). In the tool, 11 (each run against the automation tests of the tool): a removal made for `remove` false, `removed` true for no entry, the undo label changed, `has_entry` reported as `present`, the check that the entry is there dropped (the edit runs for a catalog without one), `visible` reported as `editable`, `truncated` reported as `capped`, `count` reported as the number listed, `capped` reported as `truncated`, the permission check for the edit dropped, and `initial_document_cut` always false. The first six of these (up to `visible` reported as `editable`) and `truncated` reported as `capped` were first run before `a_schema_of_many_columns_lists_the_first_256_and_counts_the_rest` was added, against the four tool tests that existed then: the six failed and `truncated` reported as `capped` survived. That test was then added, and `truncated` reported as `capped`, `count` reported as the number listed, `capped` reported as `truncated` and the dropped permission check were run against all five tool tests and failed; the first six were not run again after the test file changed again (the null-collection case was made to compare saved bytes and a `/D` cut check was added), which only added assertions. Two further mutations did not compile or were misplaced and are not counted: removing the edit's arm from the permission match (the match is exhaustive, so it was redone as a dropped permission check) and a `count` replacement that matched another tool's text (redone with more context). Not mutated: the values of the limits (256 is tested through the constant in the module tests and as a literal in the tool test, and 127 through the constants and in the tests; the allowance of 100,000 reads is tested only through `read_with` with small ones, and the real limits cannot reach it, see Cost), the cut of `/Subtype`, `/View` and a key at 127 bytes (`shown` in `crates/organize/src/role_map.rs`, which the role map tests cover; the cut of a key, of `/D` and of a name is tested here, and the cut of `/View` and `/Subtype` is not), a `/Schema` written directly in the collection (the schema is indirect in every test), `Document::collection` and `Document::collection_removes` when a document has no editor, the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (no test here reads the list; the undo label the tests read comes from the edit's own match), the re-exports in `crates/engine/src/lib.rs`, the schema (`type: boolean`), and the tool, parity and spec-parity texts. Not tested: a `/Subtype` or `/View` longer than 127 bytes, a `/Collection` that is a stream, a portfolio written by another program, extraction of the files (not done), `/Sort`, `/Folders` and `/CI` (not read), and a read that spends its allowance of 100,000 reads (not reachable). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`, then the same for `-p pdfcraft-automation` (run together in one command); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 343 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 894 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 72 in other targets passed (2 ignored); automation: 80 library tests passed, 675 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests in each); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; `cargo xtask i18n-report` (each of these with exit status 0, the tests with the one failure named below; i18n-report printed "Translation gaps: skipped, built without craft-i18n", so it checked nothing about the new label "Remove portfolio collection", which is English only). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Also, one full run of the automation tests failed `portfolio_collection::the_collection_is_read_removed_saved_undone_and_redone` and `reference_xobjects::a_call_that_changes_nothing_writes_nothing_and_a_bad_argument_is_named` besides that one; both passed run alone and in two further full runs, which had only the known failure (the counts above are from the last), and the cause was not found. Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched: the app does not use the new engine functions), the translations of the new undo label (i18n-report had no craft-i18n checkout to read), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading a file after its `/Collection` was taken away, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-21 (M11, reference XObjects, headless): What: `reference_xobjects` (new, headless) lists the document's reference XObjects, the form XObjects that have a `/Ref` dictionary (ISO 32000-2 8.10.4, which PDF/X-5g and PDF/X-5pg use; the form's own content is a proxy for a page of another file), and, given `remove`, takes the `/Ref` out of the one at that position. Nothing in this application read `/Ref`. It is read and remove only: it does not open the file `/Ref` names, draw its page in place of the proxy, add a reference or change one, and it checks no PDF/X-5 rule. The file is scanned by object number with `Document::scan_objects`, not through the pages, so a reference XObject that no page uses is listed as well (a full save writes what the trailer reaches, `/Root`, `/Info` and the other trailer entries, so such a form stays in a file only until one). A form counts when it is a stream whose `/Subtype` is the name `Form`, read as written and not through a reference, and whose `/Ref` is a dictionary after references (a `/Ref` that is a number or a dangling reference, and a dictionary that is not a stream, do not count). The result is `reference_xobjects`: `count` (every one found), `truncated` (more than 256 were found, so the first 256 are listed), `capped` (the scan's allowance of reads, below, was spent, so `count` is a lower bound and the objects after the stop were not looked at) and `items`, in object-number order: `index` (its position from 0, which `remove` takes), `object` ("number generation"), `file` (the `/UF` string of the file specification that is `/F` of `/Ref`, else its `/F` string, or `/F` itself when it is a string, decoded as a PDF text string and cut at 127 characters, null when there is none), `page_index` (`/Page` when it is an integer), `page_label` (`/Page` when it is a string, cut at 127 characters), `has_id` (`/ID` is an array, after references, of exactly two direct strings), `removable` (false when the form is the catalog, the `/Pages` node or a node under it, found by object number, the generation being ignored, by walking `/Kids` from the `/Pages` node in this module, or when that walk was cut short) and `exact` (false when `file` or `page_label` was cut). With `remove` the result also has `removed`: true when an item was listed at that position and its `/Ref` is gone, false when nothing is listed there (a position of 256 or more never is, though a form may be there), which is no change: nothing is written and no undo step is added. A listed form that is not `removable` is refused with an error (`that form is the catalog or a node of the page tree, which this tool does not change (see removable)`), as `named_destinations` does for a name it does not change; nothing is written. `remove` is an integer from 0 up to 9223372036854775807, which is as far as `Args::opt_int` goes; a negative one is refused (`remove must be 0 or more`), a larger or non-integer one is refused as not an integer, and a null is read as left out. A removal takes `/Ref` out of the form's dictionary and keeps the form's content and its other entries, so the form is an ordinary form that draws its own content; an indirect `/Ref` dictionary is left in the file. One removal is one undo step (`Edit::RemoveReferenceXObject`, labelled Remove reference XObject link, which is now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document; on a copy protected against changes a removal of a listed form is refused and the list is still read. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::reference_xobjects` (`read_reference_xobjects`, `reference_xobject_removal` with its `Removal`, `remove_reference_xobject`), run by the new edit, with `Document::reference_xobjects` and `Document::reference_xobject_removal`. Parity `optimize.pdfx5-reference-xobjects` goes from planned to partial, and the §8 row of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` names it. Why: `optimize.pdfx5-reference-xobjects` was a planned P3 feature with no tool, so an agent could not see which forms of a file are proxies for pages of other files, which file and page each names, or take the reference away from a form that a PDF/X-5 workflow left in a file that is meant to stand alone. Showing the target page would need the referenced file to be found, opened and its page drawn in place of the proxy, with a policy for files that are not there, which is not small, so the first slice is the read and the removal, which need no such model. The forms are found by scanning object numbers because following the page tree and each form's resources needs its own allowance and visited sets for shared resource dictionaries, which is a second feature; the tool text and the parity note say that nothing tells what draws a reference XObject. Cost: Each scan has its own allowance of 2,000,000 reads, charged before each read, and each walk of the page tree has another of the same size, so the allowances are per scan and per walk and not per call (a call that reads makes one scan and one walk, at most 4,000,000 charged reads, and a call that removes makes three of each, at most 12,000,000; the catalog's own read at the start of a walk is not charged). A scan pays one read for each object scanned (`Document::scan_objects`, charged before the iterator reads the object, with the last object paid for only when it is there; the scan keeps its own cache and does not fill the editing cache, but it allocates one number for each object of the file, which is not itself capped) and, for a form with a `/Ref`, one for each object a reference leads through (read with `Document::get`, which does fill the editing cache, and the library flushes that cache when it grows past its limits), as `Document::resolve` follows them (the first object and up to 32 more, so at most 33 for one value): `/Ref` (33), `/F` (33), `/UF` and then `/F` of a file specification (33 each), `/Page` (33) and `/ID` (33), so a reference XObject costs at most 198 reads on top of the one for its own scan, and the scan stops as soon as a read cannot be paid for and keeps what it found. At most 256 items are kept, each with at most two strings of 127 characters; every one past 256 is counted and not kept, and no stream is decoded. An array (`/ID`) is looked at only for its length and its first two items, and a shared object is simply read each time a reference leads to it (and scanned once, as an object). A call without `remove` scans once and, when anything is listed, walks the page tree once for `removable`: from the `/Pages` node, each node put on the stack once, at most 1,000,000 nodes, 2,000,000 `/Kids` entries and 2,000,000 reads (each read after the catalog's charged before it is made; when one is refused the walk ends and the forms are not removable), with a set of at most 1,000,001 node numbers. A call with `remove` that changes nothing scans to look the position up (the scan ends when the item at that position is listed, so one for position 0 ends at the first reference XObject, and a position with nothing listed scans to the end of the file or of the allowance), walks the page tree once when an item is listed there, and then scans and walks once more for the answer. A call that removes scans and walks in the tool, scans and walks again in the edit, copies the form's whole stream once in `Document::update_dict` (its dictionary and its data), and scans and walks once for the answer. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/reference_xobjects.rs`, 297 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 422 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (nine tests and their helpers) and 221 in `crates/automation/tests/it/reference_xobjects.rs`. Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `reference_xobjects_are_listed_with_their_file_page_and_id` (a document with none reads as empty; a saved and reopened file lists a direct `/Ref` with a string file, an integer page and an `/ID` of two strings; one whose `/Ref`, file specification and page are each behind a reference, whose `/UF` (UTF-16) wins over `/F`, whose page is a label and whose `/ID` has one item; one whose `/ID` is an array behind a reference of two direct strings (`has_id` true) and one whose `/ID` is an array of two references to strings (false); one whose `/UF` is a number so `/F` is used and whose `/ID` has a non-string; one with an empty `/Ref` and one whose `/F` and `/Page` are neither a string nor an integer, each with its file, page, `has_id` and `exact`; not listed: a form with no `/Ref`, a `/Ref` that is a number, a dangling `/Ref`, a stream whose `/Subtype` is Image, a `/Subtype` behind a reference and a dictionary that is not a stream; the object numbers rise with the index; reading changes no byte), `removing_a_reference_xobject_takes_only_its_ref_and_keeps_the_others_in_order` (positions 3, 256 and `usize::MAX` are not offered and change no byte; the middle of three removed: its content, its `/BBox`, its `/Subtype` and the dictionary its `/Ref` pointed at stay, the other two keep their order and the next takes the position, in the incremental and the full save; position 2 is then not offered; the first and then the last go by position 0), `a_reference_xobject_that_is_the_catalog_or_a_page_tree_node_keeps_its_ref` (the catalog, the `/Pages` node, an inner `/Pages` node and a page of the fixture each made a form with a `/Ref`: listed first and not `removable`, refused by `reference_xobject_removal` and unchanged, while an ordinary one after it is `removable` and still removed), `a_reference_xobject_that_is_a_page_tree_node_is_found_by_its_number_whatever_generation_the_kids_entry_names` (a page named in an inner node's `/Kids`, an array behind a reference, with generation 7: still refused; and the walk's three allowances, each met exactly and one short: 6 and 5 reads, 3 reads for the `/Kids` behind the reference, 5 and 4 nodes, 4 and 3 `/Kids` entries, the short ones answering that the walk was cut), `the_reference_xobject_listing_is_bounded_and_counts_what_it_does_not_list` (exactly 256 not truncated; 300 listed as 256 with `truncated` and a count of 300; position 255 offered and 256 not, though a form is there; position 256 changes nothing, 255 takes its form and the one before stays), `the_reference_xobject_scan_stops_when_its_allowance_is_spent_and_when_it_has_what_it_wants` (an allowance of the number of objects finds all 300 forms and one less finds 299 and is capped; none is capped; a search for position 0 with just enough reads to reach the first form is not capped while the whole scan is, and one for position 3 likewise), `every_object_a_reference_xobject_leads_through_is_paid_for_before_it_is_read` (six forms: `/F`, `/Page` and `/ID` each behind a reference, `/F` a file specification behind a reference with `/UF` behind a reference, one with `/UF` a number and `/F` behind a reference, and `/Ref` behind a reference: with exactly the reads they take the form is found and not capped, with one fewer it is capped and not found), `a_reference_xobjects_ref_behind_references_is_followed_as_document_resolve_follows_it` (a chain of 32 reference objects ends in the dictionary, which `Document::resolve` also reaches, and is paid for with 33 reads, one fewer being capped; a chain of 33 is not followed by either; a reference to itself is not a dictionary and the scan ends) and `a_reference_xobjects_file_name_and_page_label_are_cut_at_127_characters` (127 characters whole, 128 cut and not `exact`, for the file and for the label, and two forms sharing one `/Ref` dictionary both listed); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/reference_xobjects.rs`, through `reference_xobjects`: `the_reference_xobjects_are_read_one_is_removed_saved_undone_and_redone` (a file with three read exactly, one with its `/Ref` behind a reference, a `/UF`, a label and an `/ID`; the middle one removed with `removed` true and the next in its position, the saved file's `/Ref`s, undo with the label Remove reference XObject link and the saved bytes equal to before, redo with the same saved bytes as the first removal, four positions with nothing listed each with `removed` false and no new step, the rest removed, the saved file with none, three undos), `a_call_that_changes_nothing_writes_nothing_and_a_bad_argument_is_named` (a document with none; a negative, a string, a fraction and a number above i64::MAX refused, with their messages, `i64::MAX` itself `removed` false, a null read, the saved bytes equal and no undo step) and `a_form_that_is_a_page_keeps_its_ref_and_a_modify_restriction_holds` (a page that is itself a form: listed first as not `removable` and the others `removable`, a removal of it refused with an error naming `removable`, the saved bytes equal and no undo step; a copy protected against changes refuses the removal of a listed form, still reads, and a position with nothing listed is no change there). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. Mutations, 48, restoring after each, on the final code: 44 failed a test and 4 survived. In the organize module, 39 (each run against the 9 organize tests): no charge before an object is scanned, the last object charged for as well, no charge in the read that follows a reference, the reference hops one short and one long, hops not charged, no check of `/Subtype`, a `/Ref` that is not a dictionary counted, 257 items listed, `truncated` always false, the search for a position ending one item early and never ending early, the catalog not in the set of structural nodes, a `/Kids` entry with generation 7 not descended into, a `/Kids` array behind a reference not followed, `removable` always true, a refused form answered as no entry, the walk not reporting that it was cut when its reads, its entries, its nodes or the read of the `/Kids` behind a reference ran out (four mutations), `/UF` and `/F` swapped, a name cut one character late and one early, `exact` ignoring the page label and ignoring the file, `has_id` true for any array and for any array of two items, the removal doing nothing, `capped` not set when a reference's reads run out and not set when an object's read runs out, `/Page` as an integer ignored and as a string ignored, `index` not set, `count` counting only the listed, and `/ID` not followed through a reference: 36 failed one or more of the nine tests, and 3 survived: an index limit of `>` for `>=` MAX_LISTED in the target lookup, which is equivalent (a search for position 256 can list at most 256 items, so nothing is there to find, and the check only saves the scan), and the walk's cut ignored in `read_reference_xobjects` and ignored in the target lookup, which no test can reach, because the cut with the real allowances needs a page tree of more than 1,000,000 nodes or 2,000,000 `/Kids` entries, or one that spends 2,000,000 reads (about 59,000 nodes whose `/Kids` are each behind a chain of 32 references) (the cut itself is tested through `structural_with` with small allowances). In the tool, 9 (each run against the 3 tool tests): a negative `remove` cast instead of refused, the removal applied for a position with nothing listed, `removed` true for a position with nothing listed, a refused form answered as no entry (made in the organize function, run against the tool tests), `object` written as "generation number", the undo label changed, `page_label` taken from the file, `removable` always true, and `capped` reported as `truncated`: 8 failed one or more of the three tests; the 9th, `capped` reported as `truncated`, survived: no tool test can make `capped` true, because it would need 2,000,000 reads (the module tests give the scan a small allowance instead). Not mutated: the values of the limits (256, 127, 2,000,000 reads, 1,000,000 nodes and 2,000,000 `/Kids` entries: the first two are tested through the constants, and the others only through `scan` and `structural_with` with small allowances), the order of `Document::scan_objects` (a library function; the tests rely on the order the objects were added), that the scan leaves the editing cache alone (read from the library's code, not tested), the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (no test here reads the list; the undo label the tests read comes from the edit's own match), `Document::reference_xobjects` and `Document::reference_xobject_removal` when a document has no editor, the permission arm of `check_permission` (the protected-copy test shows its effect; the arm itself was not mutated), the schema (`minimum: 0`), and the tool, parity and spec-parity texts. Not tested: the walk of the page tree with a real file's allowances (the cut is tested with small ones), a `/Page` that is negative, a form inside another form or an annotation's appearance (no page or form is followed, so what draws a reference XObject is not told), a file with millions of objects, a `/Ref` of a real PDF/X-5 file, and the use of the reference by any viewer. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`, then the same for `-p pdfcraft-automation`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 338 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 894 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 72 in other targets passed (2 ignored); automation: 80 library tests passed, 670 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests in each); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; `cargo xtask i18n-report` (each of these with exit status 0, the tests with the one failure named below; i18n-report printed "Translation gaps: skipped, built without craft-i18n", so it checked nothing about the new label "Remove reference XObject link", which is English only). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched: the app does not use the new engine functions), the translations of the new undo label (i18n-report had no craft-i18n checkout to read), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading a file after a `/Ref` was taken away, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-21 (M12, named destinations, headless): What: `named_destinations` (new, headless) reads the document's named destinations, the names a link or an action can go to (ISO 32000-2 12.3.2.4), from the `/Dests` name tree under the catalog's `/Names` (7.9.6) and then from the catalog's `/Dests` dictionary of PDF 1.1 files, and, given `remove`, takes the name at that position of the listing out. No tool listed the names a file defines or took one away. It is read and remove only: it adds no name, renames none and changes no target. The result is `named_destinations`: `truncated` (more than 256 names, so the first 256 are listed), `tree_capped` (the walk of the tree was cut: a node was due after 1024 had been entered, a node at level 16, the root being level 0, had a kid, or 100000 kids and `/Names` pairs had been scanned, whether they were names or not, and another remained; a cut ends the walk of the tree, and the catalog's dictionary, which has an allowance of its own of 100000 entries, is still read after it), `destinations` and `unreadable`. A destination is `index` (its position in the listing from 0, the tree first and then the dictionary, which `remove` takes), `source` (`names` for the tree, `dests` for the dictionary), `name` (cut at 127 characters; a tree key is decoded as a PDF text string, and a dictionary key is read as UTF-8 when it is UTF-8 and otherwise as one character for each byte), `name_exact` (false when the name was cut), `page` (the 1-based page when the destination's first element is a reference to a page of this document, otherwise null), `view` (the second element when it is a name of at most 16 bytes, such as `XYZ` or `Fit`, otherwise null) and `removable`. A destination is read as an array or as a dictionary whose `/D` is an array. A node is a direct dictionary or a reference to a dictionary (one hop: a reference to a reference is not a node); a tree key that is not a direct string is skipped, as is an odd last item of a `/Names` array. `unreadable` holds `names` when the catalog's `/Names`, or its `/Dests`, is there but is neither a dictionary nor null, or the tree root is the catalog, the catalog's `/Names` dictionary (by its first reference), the `/Pages` node, a node under it or a page, and `dests` likewise for the catalog's `/Dests`; those are not listed and not changed (the check of the page tree is the one `role_map` and `class_map` use, and only an object of its own is checked). Any other dictionary is not guarded: a catalog `/Dests` that is the catalog's `/AcroForm`, `/Outlines` or `/Info`, or the tree root object itself, is read and a name in it can be removed. A node object, and a `/Names` or `/Kids` array that is an object of its own, is read once however many nodes name it, judged by the first reference only (a chain of references is not followed for this), so a name in such a node is listed once; a dictionary object that is both a tree node and the catalog's `/Dests` is listed under both sources. With `remove` the result also has `removed`: true when there was a name at that position and it is gone, false when there was none (any non-negative integer up to 9223372036854775807, the largest the tool takes), which changes nothing, writes nothing and adds no undo step. A `remove` that is negative is refused (`remove must be 0 or more`), one that is not an integer, or is above 9223372036854775807, is refused (`remove must be an integer`), and a null is read as left out. Only a name in a leaf that is an object of its own with its `/Names` array written in it, or in the catalog's `/Dests` dictionary (written in the catalog or an object of its own), is `removable`; a name anywhere else (a leaf that is a direct dictionary, or a `/Names` array that is an object of its own) is listed with `removable` false, and `remove` of it is refused (`that name is stored in an object this tool does not change (see removable)`) with nothing changed. A removal takes the name's pair out of its leaf, or its key out of the dictionary, and keeps the other names in order; an emptied leaf or dictionary stays. The page, the links that use the name and every `/Limits` are not changed, so a leaf that lost its first or last name still claims it, and a lookup of that name then finds nothing. One removal is one undo step (`Edit::RemoveNamedDestination`, labelled Remove named destination, which is now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document; on a copy protected against changes a removal of an existing, removable name is refused and the names are still read. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::destinations` (`read_destinations`, `destination_removal`, `remove_destination`), run by the new edit, with `Document::named_destinations` and `Document::destination_removal`. Parity `view.destinations-panel` goes from planned to partial; the parity entry says there is no panel and no way to add a name, and the row for sections 12.1 to 12.3 of `docs/pdf-spec-parity.md` names the entry. No guard refuses a leaf that another object also uses (a leaf a page lists as its `/Names`, say): it is changed where it is, for both. After a removal the tree can claim in `/Limits` a key a leaf no longer holds, so it no longer conforms to 7.9.6, which gives a node's `/Limits` as its least and greatest keys; the tool text and the parity note say so. Why: `view.destinations-panel` was a planned P2 feature with no tool, so an agent could not see which names a file defines or where they go, and could not take away a name that a producer left behind. Adding a name needs a place in a sorted tree with the `/Limits` of every node above it kept right, which is not small, so the first slice is the read and the removal (reading is also bounded by an allowance of 100000 items scanned, because a name tree is untrusted and a shared array would otherwise be read once for each node that names it), which need no such model. A removal leaves the `/Limits` alone because working them out up the tree would be a second feature with its own cases, and a name in a node that is a direct dictionary, or whose `/Names` array is an object of its own, is refused rather than guessed at, because changing it means rewriting a parent or another object; the tool text and the parity note say so. Cost: Reading takes one read of the catalog and borrows `/Names`, the node dictionaries and their arrays where they are direct. A node, the root of the tree and the catalog's `/Dests` are followed by one `Document::get`; the values of a node's `/Names` and `/Kids`, a destination and its `/D` are followed by `Document::resolve` (at most 33 gets each). The walk is bounded by three limits: at most 1024 nodes entered, 16 levels, and 100000 kids and `/Names` pairs scanned in all (each one is counted whether or not it is a name or a node, and a kid costs one get), and it stops as soon as one is reached; the catalog's dictionary has an allowance of its own of 100000 entries (an entry costs no get until it is listed); a node, and an array that is an object of its own, is read once. The nodes cost at most 1024 * (33 + 33) gets, the kids at most 100000 gets, and the listed names at most 256 * (33 + 33) gets (the walk stops when the 257th is found), 184,480 in all for the tree, plus the catalog's at most 1 + 33 + 1 and the root's one, and the dictionary's entries at most 100000 more scanned. These figures are for one walk, and a call that removes makes three. A call with a position far past the names scans the allowances and no more. A dictionary key is checked for UTF-8 in place and a name is decoded lazily, 128 characters at most, so at most 256 names of 127 characters are copied. A `/Dests` dictionary or a tree root that is an object of its own is checked against the page tree by `role_map`'s walk, which can visit 1,000,000 nodes and scan 2,000,000 `/Kids` entries, once for each of the two. The pages are listed (one walk of the page tree, which copies each page's inherited attributes as the other organize functions do) at most once per call and only when a destination's first element is a reference; each such name then scans that list once. A call that only reads walks the names once. A call that removes walks them to the position in the tool, again in the edit (which copies a dictionary key once, for a dictionary entry), and once more for the answer; `Document::update_dict` copies the one dictionary that holds the name (the leaf, the dictionary object or the catalog), and the pair or the key is taken out of that copy in place, so a long `/Names` array is copied once. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/destinations.rs`, 429 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 415 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (seven tests and their helpers) and 212 in `crates/automation/tests/it/named_destinations.rs`. Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `named_destinations_are_listed_from_the_tree_and_the_dictionary_with_their_page_and_view` (a document with no names reads as empty; a saved and reopened file lists a leaf object with `/Limits` and six names, an array with `/XYZ`, a value that is a dictionary with `/D`, a UTF-16 key, a destination whose page is an integer, a value that is a number, and a view name of 23 bytes, a leaf written directly in `/Kids` whose first key is an integer and is skipped, a kid that is a number and a dangling kid, and the catalog's dictionary with a key that is not UTF-8, each with its index, source, name, page, view and `removable`, and reading changes no byte), `a_destinations_entry_that_is_not_a_dictionary_is_named_and_not_listed` (a `/Names` that is a number and a `/Names /Dests` that is a name are named `names`, a catalog `/Dests` that is an array is named `dests`, and a null `/Dests` in either place names nothing; none lists a name or offers a removal), `removing_a_named_destination_keeps_the_others_in_order_and_everything_else` (a position past the end, including `usize::MAX`, is not offered and changes no byte; a name in a direct node and one in a `/Names` array that is an object of its own are called unsupported, refused and change no byte; the middle name of a leaf removed, the others kept in order in the full and the incremental save, the leaf's `/Limits` and the page count unchanged; a name of the dictionary written in the catalog removed; both names of the first leaf removed leaving an empty `/Names`; the last name of the dictionary leaving an empty dictionary in the catalog), `a_destination_dictionary_held_as_an_object_has_its_name_removed_where_it_is` (the catalog's `/Dests` as a reference: one removal leaves one name and the catalog still holds the reference) and `the_destination_listing_and_the_name_tree_walk_are_bounded` (300 names listed as 256 with `truncated` and the name at position 280 removed by its position; exactly 256 not truncated; a name of 128 characters cut at 127 and not exact, one of 127 whole; 1023 distinct kids with the name in the last one listed and not capped, 1024 capped with the name not listed; a chain of 17 levels listed and not capped, one of 18 capped with the name not listed; a node that is its own kid read once; a small leaf named twice by one parent, and by two parents, listed once with exactly the names `s` and `t`; a `/Names` array shared by two leaves read once; an empty `/Kids` at level 16 not cut), `the_destination_walk_scans_a_huge_array_only_as_far_as_the_allowance` (a `/Kids` array of 300000 entries that are not nodes is cut with exactly 100000 scanned, a `/Names` array of 600000 pairs that are not names is cut the same way for a removal at `usize::MAX`, a name behind 100000 other kids is not found and the cut is reported, a dictionary of 100001 keys scanned against an allowance of its own, whole after a tree cut by the allowance, and a dictionary name read after a tree cut by the allowance and after one cut by the node limit, where no kid past the limit is scanned), `a_structural_dictionary_is_never_read_as_the_destinations_or_written` (the catalog, the `/Pages` node, a node under it and a page, each as the catalog's `/Dests` and as the tree root, are named unreadable, list nothing, offer no removal and change no byte; the catalog's `/Names` dictionary as an object of its own is not a `/Dests` dictionary; an ordinary dictionary object is read); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/named_destinations.rs`, through `named_destinations`: `the_names_are_read_one_is_removed_saved_undone_and_redone` (a file with five names read, the second removed with `removed` true, the saved file's names, undo with the label Remove named destination and the saved bytes equal to before, redo with the same saved bytes as the first removal, three positions with no entry each with `removed` false and no new step, one undo and then none), `a_name_in_an_object_the_tool_does_not_change_is_refused_and_a_bad_argument_is_named` (a document with no names, a direct leaf and an array of its own each refused with the text above and no byte changed, a negative, a string, a fractional and an above-i64::MAX `remove` refused with the texts above, i64::MAX itself no change, a null read, no undo step) and `the_dictionary_and_a_leaf_are_changed_where_they_are_and_a_protected_copy_is_left` (the dictionary's name removed and the dictionary kept empty in the saved catalog; both names of a leaf removed with its `/Limits` still in the saved file; a copy protected against changes refuses the removal of an existing name, still reads, and a position with no entry is no change there) and `a_dictionary_that_is_the_page_tree_is_named_unreadable_and_never_written` (the `/Pages` node as the catalog's `/Dests`: the tree's names listed, `dests` named, a position past them `removed` false and the saved bytes equal). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. Mutations, in two rounds, restoring after each. First round, 34 on the code before the fix described next, all caught on the tests of that round (after two that survived its first run were dealt with: a `/Names` array that is an object of its own treated as removable, which the test could not tell apart because its node was a direct dictionary and now is an object, and the removal of a name from a dictionary object, whose two tests were not selected by the word the run filtered on and were renamed); 28 in the organize module (the name cut one character late, `name_exact` always true, `truncated` one name late, one name fewer listed, the node cap and the level cap one late and one early, neither cap reported, an array of its own treated as removable, the removal draining the wrong items, no removal from the dictionary in the catalog and none from a dictionary object, a dictionary key that is not UTF-8 read as question marks, a view name of any length listed, the page counted from 0, the dictionary's positions counted from 0, the walk going on after the position was found, a destination dictionary's `/D` ignored, `names` and `dests` not named as unreadable, an unsupported name offered as removable and `removable` always true, the lookup of a position one late, the level never growing, the dictionary's names given the source `names`, and a direct kid skipped) and 6 in the tool and the edit (a negative `remove` not refused, a position with no entry answered `removed` true, an unsupported name answered `removed` false instead of refused, the sources swapped, the edit's label changed, and `removed` false for a removal). Second round, 28 on the code after the fix, in the organize module: 14 on what the fix added (a node, a `/Names` array and a `/Kids` array read again each time they are named; the allowance one late; pairs, kids and dictionary entries each not charged; the node cap and the level cap not ending the walk; the catalog, the catalog's `/Names` dictionary and the page tree each not treated as structural for the dictionary; the tree root not checked; the dictionary not read after a cut tree) and 14 again on lines the fix rewrote (the node cap and the level cap each one late and one early, the level cap applied to an empty `/Kids`, an array of its own treated as removable, the level never growing, a direct kid skipped, the dictionary walk not stopping, a stop not recorded, the position not advancing, `names` and `dests` not named as unreadable, and the dictionary not checked as structural). The second round's first run had 11 survivors because the new tests did not contain the word `destination` the run filtered on; they were renamed and the 11 were run again and 10 were caught. The 11th, the dictionary entries not charged, and a 12th, the level cap applied to an empty `/Kids`, survived with the tests as they were and were caught after a test was added for each (the catalog dictionary of 100001 keys, and an empty `/Kids` at level 16). All 28 of the second round failed a test on the final tests. The first round's mutations that were not rewritten by the fix (the name cut, `name_exact`, `truncated`, the listed count, the removals, the key decoding, the view, the page, `D`, `removable`, the lookup and the source) and the 6 in the tool were not run again on the new code. A third round after a re-review, 7 on the code after it (the dictionary given its own allowance, a tree cut now not stopping the dictionary being read): a node object read again each time it is named, a `/Names` array read again, the dictionary not read after a cut tree, the dictionary sharing the tree's allowance, not charged against its own, and the allowance one late, and the node limit not ending the walk; all 7 were caught, after a test was changed for two that survived (the check that a leaf named by two parents is read once was empty because one leaf alone filled the listing, and no test showed that the node limit stops the scan of later kids) and the mutation that did not report a cut of the dictionary was dropped, because a cut of the dictionary is not observable (a listing stops at its 257th name long before 100000 entries). Removing the permission arm instead does not compile (the match is exhaustive) and is not counted. Not mutated: the values of the five limits (256, 127, 1024, 16 and 100000 are tested through the constants themselves, not mutated, and the 16-byte view limit only through a longer name), a null `/Dests` as no names (tested, not mutated), the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (no test here reads the list; the undo label the tests read comes from the edit's own match), `Document::named_destinations` and `Document::destination_removal` when a document has no editor, the schema (`minimum: 0`), the engine's re-exported limits, and the tool and parity texts. Not tested: a tree key in PDFDocEncoding above the ASCII range (a UTF-16 key is tested), a node that has both `/Names` and `/Kids`, a node that is a stream, a page tree too large for the structural check (it answers that the container is structural), a catalog `/Names` that reaches the structural check through a chain of references (the check compares the first reference only), a `/Dests` that is another dictionary such as `/AcroForm` or the tree root object (read, not guarded), a `/Kids` array that is also another node's `/Names` array, a `/Names` or `/Kids` value or a catalog `/Dests` that is a dangling reference or a reference to a reference, a leaf or a dictionary that another object also uses (it is changed where it is, for both), a name listed in both the tree and the dictionary, the incremental save of a removal from a dictionary (the leaf is saved both ways), the use of the names by links (not changed), and a viewer other than PdfCraft. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`, then the same for `-p pdfcraft-automation`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 329 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 894 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 72 in other targets passed (2 ignored); automation: 80 library tests passed, 667 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests in each); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm` (each of these with exit status 0, the tests with the one failure named below). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched: the app does not use the new engine functions), `cargo xtask i18n-report` (no translated text added), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading a file after a name was removed, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-21 (M12, article threads, headless): What: `article_threads` (new, headless) reads the document's article threads, the catalog's `/Threads` array (ISO 32000-2 12.4.3), where each entry is a thread dictionary whose `/F` is the first bead and whose optional `/I` dictionary holds the title, and, given `remove`, takes the thread at that position out of the array. Nothing in this application read `/Threads`. It is read and remove only: it adds no thread or bead and changes no title, and it does not read a bead's page (`/P`) or rectangle (`/R`). The result is `article_threads`: `count` (every entry), `truncated` (more than 256 entries, so the first 256 are listed), `threads` and `unreadable`. A thread is `index` (its position from 0, which `remove` takes), `title` (the `/Title` string of its `/I` dictionary, decoded as a PDF text string and cut at 127 characters, null when there is none), `title_exact` (false when the title was cut), `beads` (how many beads are reached from `/F` along `/N`, counting `/F`; at most 4096; 0 when `/F` is not a reference to a dictionary or the entry is not a dictionary), `beads_capped` (the walk stopped at 4096 beads before the ring ended or closed, which also happens for a loop that does not pass through `/F`) and `closed` (the last bead's `/N` leads back to `/F`, as a thread's ring does). `/F` and `/N` are read only when they are references, so a direct dictionary is not a bead. `unreadable` is `threads` when `/Threads` is there but is neither an array nor null, or is behind more than 33 references (as `Document::resolve` follows them: a chain of 32 reference objects is followed, one of 33 is not); then nothing is listed and nothing is written. A null, a dangling reference and an empty array are no threads. With `remove` the result also has `removed`: true when there was an entry at that position and it is gone, false when there was none (any non-negative integer up to 9223372036854775807, the largest the tool takes) or `/Threads` is absent or unreadable, which changes nothing, writes nothing and adds no undo step. A `remove` that is negative is refused (`remove must be 0 or more`), one that is not an integer, or is above 9223372036854775807, is refused (`remove must be an integer`), and a null is read as left out. A removal keeps the other entries in order, and takes an emptied `/Threads` out of the catalog (an indirect array's object is left in the file, unreferenced by the catalog); an array behind references is changed where it is. The thread's own dictionary, its beads and the `/B` arrays of the pages that list those beads are not changed, so the beads stay in the file with a `/T` that names a thread no longer in `/Threads`. One removal is one undo step (`Edit::RemoveArticleThread`, labelled Remove article thread, which is now in `EDIT_LABELS`) that needs permission to modify the document; on a copy protected against changes a removal of an existing entry is refused and the threads are still read. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::articles` (`read_article_threads`, `article_thread_removes`, `remove_article_thread`), run by the new `Edit::RemoveArticleThread`, with `Document::article_threads` and `Document::article_thread_removes`. Parity `view.articles-panel` goes from planned to partial; the parity entry says there is no panel and no article reading. No guard refuses a `/Threads` array that is also another object's array (a page's `/Kids`, say): it is changed where it is, and the tool text says so. Why: `view.articles-panel` was a planned P3 feature with no tool, so an agent could not see which article threads a file defines, what they are called or how long they are, and could not take away a thread that a producer left behind. Adding a thread would need beads with a page and a rectangle each and a consistent ring of `/N` and `/V` links, with the pages' `/B` arrays to match, which is not small, so the first slice is the read and the removal, which need no such model. A removal leaves the beads and the pages' `/B` alone because cleaning those up would be a second feature with its own cases; the tool text and the parity note say so. Cost: Reading borrows the catalog and the array where they are direct; only a reference is resolved, up to 33 gets as `Document::resolve` does. The listing takes at most 256 entries; for each, the entry is resolved (at most 33 gets), its `/I` and then its `/Title` are resolved (at most 33 gets each), and the bead walk makes at most 4096 gets (one for each bead; `/F` and `/N` are not followed through chains), so an entry costs at most 33 + 33 + 33 + 4096 = 4195 gets and the listing at most 256 * 4195 = 1,073,920 gets. A title is decoded lazily, 128 characters at most, and the string is not copied; at most 256 titles of 127 characters are copied. The count of all entries is the length of the array. A call that removes nothing reads the array once (to look the position up) and then lists once. A call that removes looks the position up in the tool; the edit then reads the array once, copies it once into the new array (which is moved, not copied again) and, when the array is direct or is emptied, `Document::update_dict` copies the catalog's dictionary once; the listing is read once for the answer. No new dependency, asset or workspace crate, and no change to a saved file that does not call the tool. One new module (`crates/organize/src/articles.rs`, 185 lines), one new edit and one new tool, 236 added lines in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs` (five tests and their helpers) and 222 in `crates/automation/tests/it/article_threads.rs`. Tested: in `crates/organize/src/tests.rs`: `article_threads_are_listed_with_their_title_and_how_many_beads_they_reach` (a document with no `/Threads` reads as empty; a saved and reopened array lists a titled closed thread of three beads, an open chain of two, a thread whose title is UTF-16, one whose `/I` title is a number, an entry that is a number, a thread whose `/F` is a reference to a number, one whose `/F` is a direct dictionary and a dangling reference, each with its title, beads and `closed`, and reading changes no byte), `removing_an_article_thread_keeps_the_others_in_order_and_leaves_the_beads` (a position past the end, including `usize::MAX`, is not offered and changes no byte; the middle thread of three removed, the order kept, in the full and the incremental save; the thread dictionary and the beads still there; the last removal takes `/Threads` out of the catalog, also after a save), `an_article_thread_array_behind_references_is_changed_where_it_is` (the array behind two references: one removal changes the array object and leaves the catalog's and the middle reference; the last removal takes the catalog's entry and leaves the array object), `an_article_threads_entry_that_is_not_an_array_is_named_and_never_replaced` (a chain of 32 reference objects read and one of 33 named, both compared with `Document::resolve`; a number, a string, a dictionary and a name each named in `unreadable`, not removable and the saved bytes equal; a null, a dangling reference and an empty array absent with nothing unreadable) and `the_thread_listing_and_the_bead_walk_are_bounded` (300 entries listed as 256 with `truncated`, an entry past the 256th removed by its position, exactly 256 not truncated; a title of 128 two-byte characters cut at 127 and not exact, one of 127 whole; a ring of exactly 4096 beads counted whole and closed, an open chain of 4096 counted whole, a ring of 4097 and a loop that skips `/F` each cut at 4096 and capped); and in `crates/automation/tests/it/article_threads.rs`, through `article_threads`: `the_threads_are_read_one_is_removed_saved_undone_and_redone` (a file with two threads read, the first removed with `removed` true, the saved file's list, the thread and its three beads still in the saved file, undo with the label Remove article thread and the saved bytes equal to before, redo with the same saved bytes as the first removal, three positions with no entry each with `removed` false and no new step, the last removal taking `/Threads` out of the saved catalog, two undos), `a_call_that_changes_nothing_writes_nothing_and_a_bad_argument_is_named` (a document with no threads, a negative, a string, a fractional and an above-i64::MAX `remove` refused with the texts above, i64::MAX itself no change, a null read, the saved bytes equal and no undo step) and `an_array_behind_a_reference_is_changed_where_it_is_and_an_unreadable_entry_is_left` (an indirect array changed where it is with the catalog still holding its reference; a number, a dictionary and a page as `/Threads` each named in `unreadable`, `removed` false, the saved bytes equal and no undo step; a copy protected against changes refuses the removal of an existing entry, still reads, and a position with no entry is no change there). None of them could fail before the change, because the module and the tool did not exist. Mutations, 26, restoring after each, on the final code: 25 failed a test and 1 survived. In the organize module, 23: the title cut one character late, `title_exact` always true and always false, the bead cap one short and one long, a ring never reported closed, a walk that does not stop at `/F`, a bead that is not a dictionary counted, `beads_capped` always false, `truncated` true at exactly 256, one extra thread listed, the removal check accepting a position equal to the length, the removal doing nothing, an emptied direct array kept and an emptied indirect array kept, an indirect array written into the catalog, the reference hops one short and one long, a non-array read as absent, `threads` not named as unreadable, the title read from the wrong key, and a last bead without `/N` not counted: 22 of these failed one or more of the five organize tests. The 23rd, removing the check that `/Threads` is readable from `article_thread_removes`, survived, and is equivalent: a `/Threads` that is not an array has no array to look a position up in, so the check that follows answers false either way. In the tool, 3: a negative `remove` cast instead of refused, the removal always applied, and `removed` always true for no change: each failed one or more of the three tool tests. The first run of the organize mutations selected the tests by the word `article`, which skipped `the_thread_listing_and_the_bead_walk_are_bounded`; the eight that survived that run (the seven that need it and the equivalent one) were run again with that test selected and the seven were caught by it. Removing the permission arm instead does not compile (the match is exhaustive) and is not counted. Not mutated: the values of the three limits (256, 127 and 4096 are tested through the constants themselves, not mutated), a null or dangling `/Threads` as absent (tested, not mutated), the `EDIT_LABELS` entry (no test here reads the list; the undo label the tests read comes from the edit's own match), `Document::article_threads` and `Document::article_thread_removes` when a document has no editor, the schema (`minimum: 0`), the tool and parity texts. - 2026-11-21 (M12, structure tree maps, guard): `role_map` and `class_map` now refuse a `/RoleMap` or `/ClassMap` that is the same object as the `/Pages` node, an inner `/Pages` node or a page, or as the tree root's other map, and a `/StructTreeRoot` that is the `/Pages` node or a node under it: it is named in `unreadable`, reads as empty and is never written (before, a `/RoleMap` that was the `/Pages` node could be given a `Kids` entry, which broke the page tree). The page tree is walked from the `/Pages` node for a tree root or map that is an indirect object, which a real tagged file has, so a read walks it once or twice and a call that writes up to eight times (time not measured); a walk puts each node on the stack once and refuses when it sees more than 1,000,000 nodes or scans more than 2,000,000 `/Kids` entries (a shared `/Kids` array of a hostile file could otherwise make the stack and the scan quadratic). No tool, edit or parity status changed; a page in no `/Kids` list, a `/ParentTree`, an attribute object and any other dictionary are still not guarded and the tool descriptions say so. Fourteen mutations were run on the final code and all were caught by a test; what was not mutated or tested is in the commit. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match` (organize and engine together, then automation); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on each of the three crates in turn (organize: 317 library tests passed and 2 in other targets passed; engine: 888 library tests passed (2 ignored) and 71 in other targets passed; automation: 78 library tests passed, 659 integration tests passed and 1 failed, and 2 in another target passed; 0 doctests); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm` (each of the four with exit status 0). Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched: the app does not use the changed functions), the workspace sweep, the new tests on Windows or macOS (only Linux was run), a viewer other than PdfCraft reading a file after a tool call, and the mutations listed under Not mutated. - 2026-11-21 (M12, structure tree class map, headless): `class_map` (new) reads the `/ClassMap` of the catalog's `/StructTreeRoot`, which maps a class name to an attribute object or an array of them (ISO 32000-2 14.7.5.4), and, with `remove`, takes the entry of one class name away; it adds no class and changes no attribute object. The result lists at most 256 entries with `kind` (dictionary, stream, array or other, after references), `items`, the `/O` owner of the first 16 attribute objects and `exact`, and names what is `unreadable` (a `/StructTreeRoot` or `/ClassMap` that is neither a dictionary nor null or is behind more than 33 references, a `/ClassMap` that is a stream, which is unreadable while a stream tree root counts by its dictionary, and a `/ClassMap` or tree root that is the catalog or the tree root); `removed` says whether an entry was taken away. Any string is looked up as the stored key, so a stored empty, NUL or over-long key goes by its exact name; a class that is not stored, an absent map and an unreadable one are no change, write nothing and add no undo step, and one removal is one undo step (Remove class map entry) that needs permission to modify; an indirect attribute object stays and an emptied `/ClassMap` goes; a `/ClassMap` that is another live dictionary (the `/Pages` node, a page, the `/RoleMap` dictionary) is read and changed as one and nothing refuses it, so `remove` of `Kids` there would break the page tree, as for `role_map`. `a11y.class-map` goes from planned to partial, and `organize.associated-files`, a duplicate of `core.pdf20-associated-files`, stays planned and gets a note saying so. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::class_map`, `Edit::RemoveClassMapEntry`, sharing the role map's finding and writing. Thirty mutations were run, twenty-nine were caught by a test and one survived (named in the commit); what was not mutated or tested is in the commit. - 2026-11-20 (M12, move a guide, headless, gap 16): What: `doc_guide_move` (new, headless) moves one ruler guide, by `id` from `doc_guides`, along its axis to `position`, as dragging it out of a ruler would: a vertical guide to that many points from the displayed page's left edge, a horizontal one from its top edge. The guide keeps its id, page, axis and place in the list. The position must lie on the page, edges included, and is rounded to a thousandth of a point (-0 becomes 0), as `doc_guide_add` does; the result is the guide with `moved`, false when it was already at that rounded place. It is refused, with nothing changed, for an `id` no guide has, a guide whose page the document no longer has (the error says so and names `doc_guide_remove`: there is no page to measure against, so it can only be removed), a position off the page or not a finite number, a missing or wrongly typed `id` or `position`, and a position where another guide of that page and axis already lies (the error names that guide: guides are never doubled, so remove one instead). View state only, as for the other guide tools: not saved into the file, no undo step, the document is not marked changed. The app has no rulers to drag a guide from, so it does not call this. Engine: `Session::move_guide` (`pdfcraft_engine::guides`), whose page check `add_guide` now shares. Why: Gap 16: an agent could add and remove guides but not move one, so repositioning a guide meant removing it and adding another under a new id, and could leave two guides at one place or none if the second step failed. Cost: one scan of the document's at most 200 guides per call; nothing is copied from the file and no new limit is needed. - 2026-11-20 (M12, document part parents, headless, gap 22): What: `dpart_list` (headless, read-only) takes a new flag per part, `parent_mismatch`, and a count in all, `parent_mismatches`: a node other than the root node whose `/Parent` is not an indirect reference to the node it was reached from (ISO 32000-2 Table 411 requires one): the key is missing, holds a direct value (a number, a dictionary, a null) or points at another object, or at the same number with another generation. The root node is never flagged (its `/Parent` names the `/DPartRoot`, which is not checked) and a node listed by several parents is judged against the node given as its `parent` (the one it is listed under first in document order). References are compared as written (object number and generation, no chain followed), so on a file whose cross-reference table was repaired a child may be flagged though it names the same object under another accepted generation. Nothing else about the tree is checked and nothing is written. Engine: `DocumentPart::parent_mismatch` and `DocumentPartList::parent_mismatches` (`pdfcraft_organize`). Why: Gap 22: `parent` in the listing is where the walk came from, so an agent could not tell a tree whose nodes disagree about their parent (a stale `/Parent` after a copy or an edit outside a PDF tool) from a sound one. Cost: one key lookup and one comparison of two references per node; nothing is copied, followed or allocated, and no new limit is needed (the 5,000-node, 64-level and 5,000-children limits are unchanged and the tests of them still pass). Tested: `a_part_whose_parent_is_not_the_node_listing_it_is_flagged` in organize (a root with a `/Parent`, a correct child, and children with a missing `/Parent`, one naming a sibling, a direct integer, a direct null and the right number with generation 1, plus a node listed by two parents; six flagged, one skipped) and the end-to-end `a_part_whose_parent_is_not_the_node_listing_it_is_flagged_through_the_tool` (a hand-built file whose first part names the other part and whose second has a direct number; two flagged, and the ordinary file reports 0). Mutations, each restored: reading only the key's presence, comparing the object number alone, flagging the root node and never counting each failed the organize test; the tool leaving out the flag and key presence only each failed the automation test. Not mutated: the automation test against the root and object-number mutations, and the tool's count field alone.
