- 2026-11-20 (M12, grid preferences, headless, gap 16): What: `grid_preferences` (new, headless, not read-only, announced idempotent to MCP clients) stores a grid for this tool session, which `doc_grid_snap` then uses for any of `spacing`, `subdivisions`, `offset_x` and `offset_y` that a call leaves out (`spacing` is no longer required by the tool's schema; with nothing stored it is still required and the rest default to 1 and 0, as before). It takes `spacing` (`pt`, `in`, `mm` or `cm` through `unit`, default `pt`; 0.01 to 14,400 points), `subdivisions` (1 to 100; lines no closer than 0.01 point), `offset_x` and `offset_y` (within a million points either way) and `clear: true` (not together with grid values); a value left out stays as stored, or is 72 points, 1 and 0 when nothing is stored; with no arguments it reads. It returns `{ set: false }` or `{ set: true, spacing_pt, subdivisions, pitch_pt, offset_x_pt, offset_y_pt }`, numbers in points (`subdivisions` an integer). `doc_grid_snap` now also answers `from_preferences`, the names it took from the stored grid; the stored grid is always in points, so `unit` does not convert it. The grid is checked as `doc_grid_snap` checks one, and a call refused for a bad value, wrong type or unknown unit stores nothing. Held by the tool session only, not kept between runs, no document touched; the app has no grid, no grid Preferences, grid colour or separate line height. Engine: `Session::grid_preference` and `Session::set_grid_preference` (`pdfcraft_engine::guides`). The missing-`spacing` error of `doc_grid_snap` now says to give it or store a grid with `grid_preferences`; `from_preferences` lists names in the order `spacing`, `offset_x`, `offset_y`, `subdivisions`; an offset of -0 is stored as 0. Both tools share one unit conversion, which now multiplies by 72 and divides by the unit's size last, so a value exactly at a limit converts exactly: 5080 mm is exactly 14,400 points, where the pre-divided factor made it 14400.000000000002 and `doc_grid_snap` refused it. Why: gap 16 had arithmetic only; a grid had to be restated on every call. Cost: one stored Copy struct on the session; no document is read. Tested: `guides::grid_tests::the_grid_preference_is_stored_checked_and_forgotten` (engine) and the end-to-end `guides::grid_preferences_are_set_read_cleared_and_fill_in_what_doc_grid_snap_is_not_given` (set, read, partial update in a unit, same call twice, clear, `doc_grid_snap` filling in and overriding, the unit not converting the stored grid, 5080 mm through `doc_grid_snap`), `guides::grid_preferences_accept_the_limits_and_refuse_beyond_them_storing_nothing` (exactly 14,400 points also as 5080 mm, 508 cm and 200 in, 0.01, 100 parts, offsets of 13,888 in and a million points accepted; 23 refusals including 5080.01 mm, each leaving the stored grid or the unset state as it was; a refusal caused only by merging with the stored grid; negative zero stored as 0) and `automation::grid_preferences_is_announced_as_a_writing_idempotent_tool`. Not mutated: the refusal caused only by the merge with the stored grid, the conversion of `offset_y` by `unit`, the stored grid being converted by `unit`, the negative-zero fix on `offset_y`, each type check on its own, and the tool, doc and parity text. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched), the workspace sweep. - 2026-11-20 (M12, set alternate text of several figures, headless, gap 23): What: `accessibility_set_alts` (new, headless) sets, clears or marks decorative the alternate text of 1 to 100 figures in one call and as one undoable edit named Set alternate text (a batch of the existing `SetAltText` and `MarkDecorative` edits; no new edit variant, so the labels, history scope, run and permission check already cover it). `figures` is a list of `{ figure, alt }` (a figure number from `accessibility_figures`; `alt` a string of at most 4000 bytes, trimmed as `accessibility_set_alt` trims it, an empty one clears the text) or `{ figure, decorative: true }`. Every item is checked before anything is changed, and a refused call writes nothing and leaves no undo step: a figure named twice, an item with both `alt` and `decorative: true` or with neither, a value of the wrong type (`figure` not a positive integer, `alt` not a string, `decorative` not a boolean), an unknown key, an item that is not an object, an empty list, more than 100 items, an `alt` over 4000 bytes, and a number that is not a figure of the document. A figure whose trimmed text already equals the new text is reported in `unchanged` and left alone, and a call that changes nothing makes no undo step. It returns `changed` (how many figures it edited) and `set`, `cleared`, `decorative` and `unchanged` (figure numbers). The descriptions of `ai_alt_text_suggest` and `accessibility_set_alt` now point at it, and the parity entries `a11y.alt-text-workflow` (new tool and its three tests) and `a11y.alt-text-suggestions` (accepted suggestions are applied in one step; still partial) say so. The app is unchanged: its Set Alternate Text dialog already saves as one undo step. Why: Gap 23: `ai_alt_text_suggest` is suggest-only and told the person to apply the ones they accept with `accessibility_set_alt`, which takes one figure per call, makes one undo step per figure and returns the whole figure list each time, so applying a list of suggestions meant N calls, N undo steps and N full listings, and a call that failed halfway left the earlier figures changed. Cost: one scan of the document's figures per call (the same as `accessibility_figures`), and work in the length of the list, which is capped at 100 items of at most 4000 bytes each before any is read; the engine clones the document once, and each decorative figure is rewritten on its page as `accessibility_set_alt` does it (the page list, a structure walk when the figure has no `/Pg`, and that page's content streams decoded and rewritten). Tested: three tests in `crates/automation/tests/it/alt_text_batch.rs` on a one-page tagged file with three figures (one already described) and a non-figure structure element: `several_figures_change_as_one_undo_step_with_exact_text` (set with surrounding spaces, quotes, an ampersand, angle brackets and non-Latin letters, clear one that had text and mark one decorative in one call; the figures list shows the trimmed exact text, a saved and reopened copy has it, one `edit_undo` named Set alternate text restores all three), `a_figure_whose_text_would_not_change_makes_no_undo_step` (the same text with spaces round it, and blank text for a figure with none: `changed` is 0, the document is not marked changed and has no undo step) and `a_refused_call_writes_nothing` (17 refusals, each leaving the figures as they were, the document unchanged and no undo step, including exactly 100 items, which pass the item limit and are then refused as not figures of the document (Failed, not InvalidArgs), and a 4000-byte text accepted). Mutations, each restored: always editing instead of leaving an unchanged figure alone failed the no-undo-step test, not refusing a repeated figure failed the refusal test, removing the 4000-byte limit failed it, removing the 100-item limit failed it, and applying the edits one by one instead of as one batch failed the one-undo-step test. Not mutated: the check that each figure exists before any edit (the engine refuses a non-figure on its own copy of the document and discards it, so the result is the same either way), each type check on its own, the warnings carried over from the edit, and the tool, doc and parity text. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-automation` (74 library tests and 596 integration tests passed, one integration test failed, and 2 in another target passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`. - 2026-11-20 (M12, Will Close, Did Save and Did Print, headless, gap 15): What: three more document actions of the catalog's `/AA` run, headless: `doc_close` runs Will Close (`/WC`) before a close that goes ahead and reports it as `will_close`; `doc_save` runs Did Save (`/DS`) once the file is written and reports it as `did_save`, and its `document` summary is now read after that, so it shows the unsaved changes Did Save left; `doc_print` runs Did Print (`/DP`) once the print-ready file is written or the job is queued and reports it as `did_print`. Each runs in the document's script realm like Will Save and Will Print, may only ask for highlighting, zoom, focus or a page, and is left out of the result when it showed nothing. A failing action is reported in its `errors` and stops nothing. A close refused for unsaved changes, a save or print that fails, and a document with JavaScript off, an XFA form or no such action run none. What Will Close changes goes with the document; what Did Save changes is not in the file and leaves the document with unsaved changes, so a later `doc_close` refuses until it is saved or `discard_changes` is true. Neither Will Print nor Did Print runs when the document's permissions forbid printing. `doc_save` is no longer marked idempotent in the MCP annotations, since Did Save can change the document it leaves open. The app does not run these three yet (parity `form.document-actions` says so, and stays partial). Engine: `DocumentScriptEvent::{WillClose, DidSave, DidPrint}` (`pdfcraft_engine::js`) and `DocumentTrigger::{WillClose, DidSave, DidPrint}` (`pdfcraft_forms`), whose `/AA` is now read in place when it is direct. The descriptions of `doc_close`, `doc_save` and `doc_print` say so (doc_save and doc_print did not mention Will Save and Will Print before). Why: Gap 15: the catalog's Will Close, Did Save and Did Print JavaScript actions were listed by `doc_javascripts` but never run, so a form that logs, validates or stamps on those events behaved differently here than in Acrobat, and an agent could not test them. Cost: one lookup of `/AA` and its key per call, and a script runs only when one exists; the chain of actions is read to the existing 256-step limit, and a runaway chain can take up to 256 actions of up to 10 seconds each per event (`doc_save` now runs two chains, Will Save and Did Save). Tested: `will_close_did_save_and_did_print_run_only_their_own_actions` in engine (a catalog with all five actions, direct, as a reference and as a reference to a reference: each event runs only its own key and sees its own `event.name`; a catalog with only Will Save runs nothing for the three) and `did_save_did_print_and_will_close_run_after_the_file_or_before_the_close` in automation (a save to a path under a file fails and runs no Did Save; a save that works reports `did_save` and leaves the document changed while the saved file, reopened with JavaScript off, lacks the change; a print with no destination and one whose file cannot be written run no Did Print and one that works reports `did_print`; a close refused for the unsaved change runs no Will Close, `discard_changes` closes and reports `will_close`, a Will Close that changes a clean document still lets the close go ahead, discarding it and reporting `will_close`, and a document with no actions closes with nothing reported). Mutations, each restored: reading Did Save from `/WS` failed the automation test, running Did Print as Will Print failed it, running Did Print on close in place of Will Close failed it, running Did Save before the write failed it, reading Will Close from `/WS` failed the engine test, and following an `/AA` reference one hop only (`doc.get`, as a first version did) failed the engine test. Not mutated: the in-place read of a direct `/AA`, the print-write failure line, the JavaScript-off and XFA cases, a failing Will Close, Did Save or Did Print action (the code is shared with Will Save, whose failure is tested), and the doc and parity text. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-forms -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-forms -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation` (forms: 396 library tests and 1 in another target passed; engine: 886 library tests passed and 2 ignored, and 9, 2 and 60 in other targets passed with 2 ignored; automation: 74 library tests and 593 integration tests passed, one integration test failed, and 2 in another target passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched), the workspace sweep. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-20 (M12, document part ranges, headless, gap 22): What: `dpart_list` (headless, read-only) takes a new flag per part, `range_unresolved`, and a count in all, `unresolved_ranges`: a part whose `/Start` or `/End` is given (anything but a direct null; a reference to a missing or null object counts and is flagged) but names no page of the document, because it is not a reference or the object is not a page the page tree lists (a page past the first 100,000 reads so too, and then `truncated` is true). A part with neither key, or with a direct null, is not flagged; nothing else about the ranges is checked and nothing is written. Engine: `DocumentPart::range_unresolved` and `DocumentPartList::unresolved_ranges` (`pdfcraft_organize`). Why: Gap 22: `start_page` and `end_page` read null both when a key is absent and when it points nowhere, so an agent could not tell a dangling range from a part that simply has none. Cost: two key lookups and two comparisons per node, from the page numbers already read; nothing is copied or scanned again. Tested: `a_part_whose_start_or_end_names_no_page_is_flagged_unresolved` in organize (a good range, a part with no range, an `/End` pointing at the catalog, a direct integer `/Start`, direct nulls, and a `/Start` reference to an object that does not exist; three flagged) and `a_part_whose_start_or_end_names_no_page_is_flagged_unresolved_through_the_tool` in automation (a direct string `/End` and a catalog `/End` in a file; two flagged, and the ordinary file reports 0). Mutations, each restored: dropping the `/End` half of the check, dropping the `/Start` half, and treating a direct null as given each failed the organize test, and the first also failed the automation test. Not mutated: the automation test against the other two mutations, the 100,000-page limit and the doc and parity text. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-automation` (organize: 279 library tests and 2 in other targets passed; automation: 74 library tests and 592 integration tests passed, one integration test failed, and 2 in another target passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched), the workspace sweep. - 2026-11-20 (M12, identity login name, headless, gap 19): What: `identity` (headless) takes two new arguments beside `name` and `email`: `login_name` (a separate setting of the session, not the operating system's login that the desktop app takes its default author from; at most 200 characters after surrounding spaces are trimmed, empty clears it, empty by default) and `author_from_login_name` (a boolean, Preferences ▸ Commenting ▸ Always use Log-in Name for Author name, off by default). When the switch is on and a login name is set, the login name rather than the name is the default author of new comments, replies, review statuses, marked-state changes, stamps, measurements, redaction marks and Fill & Sign marks when a call gives no `author`; with no login name the name is used, and the name stays the text a typed signature starts from. A call changes only the settings it names (setting the name or email no longer touches the login settings) and is refused whole, with nothing changed, when any value is over 200 characters after trimming or of the wrong type (all values are read and checked before the first is stored). The result gains `login_name` and `author_from_login_name`. Session only, like the name and email; the desktop Preferences has no login name field, so the gap 19 line and parity `misc.prefs-identity` (still partial) say it is headless only. Engine: `Identity::login_name`, `Identity::author_from_login_name`, `Identity::author()` and `Session::set_login_name` (`pdfcraft_engine`); `Session::set_identity` now leaves the login settings alone. Why: Gap 19: the Identity preferences listed no login name, and Acrobat's Always use Log-in Name for Author name option had no equivalent, so an agent could not make a login name the author of comments. Cost: the tool trims and counts each given value once (linear in its length, which a very long value makes no worse) and stores at most 200 characters of it; nothing is scanned or copied twice. Tested: `the_login_name_authors_comments_only_when_asked_and_a_refused_call_changes_nothing` in automation (defaults empty and off; a login name set while the switch is off leaves the name as author and trims the login name; the switch on makes comments authored `ghopper` as listed by comment_list; renaming leaves the login name and switch alone; with the switch on a Fill & Sign signature with no text and no author is authored `ghopper` and drawn with the name's text (the same size as one given the text `G. Hopper`, and not the same as one given `ghopper`, checked in a second session); clearing the login name falls back to the name with the switch still on; a login name of 200 two-byte characters and one padded with spaces around 200 characters are accepted (trimmed), 201 two-byte characters are refused, and three refused calls, a 201-character login name beside a valid name, a non-boolean switch beside a valid login name and a numeric login name, each return InvalidArgs and an identity read afterwards equals the one before). Mutations, each restored: `Session::set_identity` resetting the login settings (as the first draft did) failed that test at the rename step; `Identity::author` ignoring the switch failed it at the switch-on step; the signature text taking the login name failed it at the signature step; counting bytes instead of characters and not trimming before the count each failed it at the cap checks. Not mutated: the 200 value itself, the stamp, measurement, redaction, reply, review status and marked-state callers of the author (they share `author_arg` with comment_add and are covered only through it), and the doc and parity text. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation` (engine: 885 library tests passed and its other targets passed; automation: 74 library tests and 591 integration tests passed, one integration test failed, and 2 in another target passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched), the workspace sweep. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-20 (M12, Submit a Form names checked, headless, gap 15): What: `form_submit_actions` (headless, read-only) takes a new count per action, `unmatched`: how many entries of an only or except field list match no field of the form. A name matches a field's fully qualified name, or a dotted ancestor of one (a parent whose children it stands for); names are compared exactly, letter for letter, each entry counts (repeats too), a partial name that itself holds a dot is read as two, and `unmatched` is 0 for all and too_many. The fields of the form are those the form reader returns (terminal text, button, choice and signature fields from the form's `/Fields`, and widgets only the pages list); a field with no or an unknown `/FT`, one deeper than 64 levels, or a parent whose only kids are such fields is not read, so a name for it counts as unmatched and the count can be too high. Nothing is written and nothing is sent. A reference in the list to a field under a parent whose `/T` is empty is now named without the empty part, as the form reader names that field (`x`, not `.x`; `qualified_name_within` joins the parts as the field walk does), which also changes the names of other callers that name a field by reference (Reset a Form, Show/Hide, and the like) in that one case. It replaces the old limitation that names were never checked against the form. Engine: `SubmitAction::unmatched` (`pdfcraft_forms`). Why: Gap 15: a Submit a Form action that names a field that does not exist (a typo, a renamed or deleted field) silently sends less or more than intended; an agent had to list every field and compare by hand. The check sorts the form's field names once per call, only when an action lists names, as slices of the names already read (nothing is copied), and looks each listed name up with a binary search plus one check of the next name for a dotted ancestor, so it takes O((F + N) log F) comparisons for F fields and N listed names; a list is already capped at 65,536 entries and 4 MiB. A first version that hashed every dotted prefix of every field name took 68 seconds on one field whose name was 300,000 dots. Tested: `submit_actions_count_the_listed_names_that_match_no_field` in forms (a form with a parent `address` and child `address.city` and a radio group `size`: only and except lists holding matched names, a repeat, a parent name, a reference to the parent, a case difference, a name no field has, a trailing dot, a child that does not exist and a prefix that is not a whole name; 5, 5 and 0 for an all action), `a_field_named_with_hundreds_of_thousands_of_dots_is_matched_quickly` (one field whose `/T` is 300,000 dots and a list naming it by dots; must finish in under 10 seconds), `a_reference_to_a_field_under_an_empty_named_parent_is_matched` (a reference to a field under a parent with an empty `/T` is listed as `x` and matches) and, end to end through the tool, `submit_form_actions_are_listed_through_tools_and_send_nothing` (an except list `a`, `b` on a form that has neither: `unmatched` 2; a tool-written action naming all fields: 0). Mutations, each verified and restored: with the first hash-every-prefix version the dots test failed after 68 seconds; making the count always 0 made the forms test and the automation test fail (earlier build, same count line); not adding dotted ancestors made the forms test fail (earlier build; that code was replaced by the sorted search); joining name parts without skipping an empty first part made the empty-parent test fail. Not mutated: the 0 for all (an unchanged branch), the 65,536-entry cap (no new flag; covered by the existing `submit_actions_refuse_to_copy_an_address_or_list_past_their_caps`), the binary search's ancestor check (only the end-to-end counts in the first test exercise it) and the other callers of `qualified_name_within`, which were not given tests of their own. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-forms -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-forms -p pdfcraft-automation` (forms: 396 library tests passed; automation: 74 library tests and 590 integration tests passed, one integration test failed, and 2 in another target passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched), the workspace sweep. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-20 (M12, find and replace across JavaScripts, headless, gap 15): What: `doc_javascript_replace` (new, headless) replaces the plain text `find` with `replace` in every JavaScript `doc_javascripts` lists (document scripts, open action, document actions, field, link, annotation, page and bookmark actions), as one undoable edit named Replace in JavaScripts that keeps every other key and action of each script; nothing is run. `find` is at most 1,024 bytes and `replace` at most 65,536 bytes and may be empty; case is matched unless `match_case` is false, which folds only the ASCII letters; matches are found left to right without overlapping. `dry_run` returns the same report and writes nothing. The result has `searched` (listed scripts read whole and exactly), `occurrences`, `changed` (each script that would read differently, once, with its `id`, `kind`, `trigger` and `occurrences`), `applied` and `skipped_unreadable`. A script is not searched, and is left as it is, when its code cannot be read whole and exactly: no readable text, longer than 1 MiB or past the listing's 16 MiB of code, a stream `/JS` that is not plain UTF-8 (PDFDocEncoding, UTF-16, or a byte-order mark) or a string that does not decode cleanly, since writing it back would damage it (new `exact` on the listing's entries and in `doc_javascripts`). A changed script is written as a string in its `/JS`: a stream `/JS` is not kept as a stream, and the old stream is left in the file, not referenced by that action. Scripts are written where they physically are: an action that several places refer to changes for all of them, two ids that lead to one shared dictionary are one script (listed once, under the first), and each object that holds changed actions (a catalog with thousands of direct named scripts, a page with direct annotations) is copied and rewritten once, not once per script. A replace that finds nothing, or whose result reads as the script already does (case ignored, a find equal to its replacement but for case), changes nothing and adds no undo step or unsaved mark; that holds for the engine edit alone, inside a Batch it stays part of the batch. It is refused, with nothing changed, for an empty `find`, a `find` equal to `replace` with case matched, a script that would be longer than 1 MiB after the replace or rewritten scripts that together would hold more than 16 MiB (both worked out with checked arithmetic before any script is built), and a script listing that hit a limit (over 5,000 scripts, the page annotation visit limit, or a chain, name tree, field tree, outline or array past its limit, which the listing now also reports as `capped`), since a replace could not reach every script. Every object that holds a changed action is built before the first is written, so a script that cannot be written back refuses the whole edit with the document as it was. A document that does not allow changes refuses the edit. Engine: `pdfcraft_forms::replace_in_javascripts` and `preview_javascript_replace`, run by the new `Edit::ReplaceInJavaScripts` (re-exported as `JavaScriptReplaceReport` and `JavaScriptReplaced`); `Document::preview_javascript_replace`. The tool lists the scripts up to three times per apply (its own preview, the engine's check for nothing to change, and the edit itself), each bounded as above, and `rewrite_places` writes a shared object in place with `doc.set`, as `set_javascript` does. Parity `form.edit-all-javascripts` stays partial; the window has no replace. Why: gap 15, forms and JavaScript; Edit all JavaScripts could list and edit scripts one by one, so a function renamed or a URL moved in a file meant one edit per script by id, each with its fingerprint; a headless find and replace over every script, with a preview, is the part the gap listed as missing that needs no new window. Tested: in automation `replace_rewrites_every_kind_of_script_in_one_undoable_step` (a hand-built form with eight scripts, one of every kind: a preview of `();` reports 7 finds and changes nothing, the replace changes 7 scripts and leaves the eighth, the ids and the field's chained action stay, and one undo restores the list), `replace_matches_case_only_when_asked_and_a_miss_adds_no_step`, `a_refused_replace_writes_nothing` (a 600 KiB script that would pass 1 MiB when `a` becomes `aa`, an empty find, an unchanged replacement, a 1,025-byte find, a 65,537-byte replacement, and the same preview: every call is refused and the saved bytes and the list are equal), `replace_skips_scripts_it_cannot_read_whole_and_counts_them`, `replace_refuses_a_document_with_more_scripts_than_a_listing_reads` (5,010 scripts), `a_chain_past_its_limit_is_reported_capped_and_a_replace_refuses` (an open action array of 300 actions: 255 listed, `capped` true, refused, saved bytes equal), `replace_leaves_scripts_in_other_encodings_alone_and_rewrites_a_utf8_stream_as_a_string` (a PDFDocEncoding stream and a UTF-16BE stream with a byte-order mark are skipped and unchanged, a UTF-8 stream is rewritten; with only the first two the saved bytes are equal), `replace_changes_a_shared_action_for_every_place_that_holds_it` (one action held by two links and a `/Next` chain: a fresh listing and the saved file have no find left), `a_dictionary_shared_by_two_fields_is_one_script_and_the_replace_succeeds` (two ids, one script, the apply succeeds, and a fresh listing and the saved file read the new text under both ids; the tool reports the plan it applied, so the dry run is no separate proof), `thousands_of_direct_scripts_are_rewritten_with_one_copy_of_their_container` (4,990 direct named scripts of about 2 KiB in the catalog: all rewritten; the whole `edit_all_javascripts` test target, this test included, runs in about 1.5 s), `rewritten_scripts_together_past_16_mib_are_refused` (17 scripts of 960,000 bytes growing to 1,010,000 are refused, 17,170,000 bytes against 16,777,216, with the saved bytes equal; 16 of them pass), `a_replace_that_changes_no_text_is_not_a_change` and `a_document_that_does_not_allow_changes_refuses_a_replace` (a copy protected with changes none: refused, list and saved bytes equal); in engine `replace_in_scripts_is_one_undoable_step_needing_modify_permission` and `a_replace_that_finds_nothing_leaves_no_step_and_nothing_unsaved`; and in forms the unit tests `matches_do_not_overlap_and_fold_only_ascii`, `a_length_that_overflows_is_none`, `a_field_tree_65_levels_deep_is_capped_and_one_64_deep_is_not`, `an_outline_65_levels_deep_is_capped_and_one_64_deep_is_not`, `a_name_tree_kids_array_past_the_node_limit_is_capped` and `a_next_chain_of_300_actions_is_cut_at_256_steps_and_capped` (the listing's `capped` set at a chain of 256 steps, nesting of 64, and 100,000 `/Kids`; the last also checks a chain of 256 is not capped). Mutations, each restored: reading inexact scripts failed the encodings test; counting a shared place twice failed `a_dictionary_shared_by_two_fields_is_one_script_and_the_replace_succeeds`; the 16 MiB check removed failed `rewritten_scripts_together_past_16_mib_are_refused`; keeping a no-op rewrite failed `a_replace_that_changes_no_text_is_not_a_change`; the chain limit not flagged failed `a_chain_past_its_limit_is_reported_capped_and_a_replace_refuses` and `a_next_chain_of_300_actions_is_cut_at_256_steps_and_capped`, and each of the field-depth, outline-depth and name-tree `/Kids` flags removed failed its own forms test; the engine's skip of an empty replace removed failed the engine no-step test; earlier ones: ASCII folding removed, the 1 MiB check removed, reading cut scripts and the 5,000-script refusal removed each failed their tests. Not mutated: the shared-action test (the writer has no switch to put back), the 4,990-script test (no fast run of the old per-script copying was made, so it is a bound on the time, not a proven failure of the old code), the two helper unit tests and the engine permission test; the node-count limits (more than 100,000 nodes visited in a name tree, field tree or outline) and the field tree's oversized `/Kids` flag set `capped` too but have no test. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-forms -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-forms -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation` (forms: 393 library tests passed; engine: 885 library tests passed and its other targets passed; automation: 74 library tests and 590 integration tests passed, one integration test failed, and 2 in another target passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched), the workspace sweep, a replace on a real-world file. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-20 (M12, associated file relationship fixer, headless, gap 22): What: `af_set_relationship` (new, headless) sets the `/AFRelationship` of the document-level associated file at `index` (1-based, from `af_list`) to one of Source, Data, Alternative, Supplement, FormData, Schema or Unspecified, replacing the name it had or adding one where it had none or a value that was not a name; it is the fix for the entries `af_list` counts in `without_relationship`. Only the catalog's `/AF` is changed (page arrays are read, to find who else lists the file). A file specification given by reference changes where it is written, so a page's `/AF` that lists the same object shows the new name too; `shared_with_other_af` says so, and errs towards true: it is also true when the page tree cannot be read or has over 100,000 pages, though only the first 256 entries of each page's `/AF` array are looked at, so a listing past them is not seen. A specification written inside the array itself is changed in a new direct array for the catalog; when the catalog's `/AF` was an indirect array (`array_copied` is true whenever that was so and the specification was written inside it, whether or not a page lists it) the old array object is left as it was, so a page that lists it keeps the old entry with no relationship, `af_list` then lists that array again for the page, and `without_relationship` can go up. It returns the `previous` name (null when there was none). It refuses, with nothing changed: an unknown name, `EncryptedPayload` (its file specification needs an `/EP` encrypted-payload dictionary, which this does not write), a catalog with no `/AF`, an `/AF` that is not an array or holds more than 256 entries (checked before any entry is copied), an `index` outside the array, an entry that is not a file specification dictionary (a number, a string, a stream, or a dictionary whose `/Type` is not `Filespec`, or that has none of `/F` or `/UF` as a string, `/EF` as a dictionary and `/FS` as a name, references followed: the catalog, a page, an annotation with its `/F` flags number, and a file attachment annotation with its `/FS` dictionary), and a name the entry already has (nothing would change). A document that does not allow changes refuses. One undo step. Engine: `pdfcraft_organize::set_associated_relationship` and `catalog_af_is_indirect`, run by the new `Edit::SetAssociatedRelationship`; `Document::associated_array_is_indirect`. Parity `core.pdf20-associated-files` stays partial. Why: gap 22, PDF 2.0 extras; `af_list` could find the associated files with no relationship name but nothing could fix one short of removing the file and adding it again, which also re-embeds it and changes its attachment. Tested: `a_relationship_is_set_on_an_entry_with_none_and_every_refusal_changes_nothing`, `setting_a_relationship_refuses_an_af_that_is_not_a_usable_array_or_a_specification_that_is_not_a_dictionary` and `an_entry_that_points_at_a_stream_the_catalog_or_a_non_specification_is_refused_and_nothing_is_written` in organize (a catalog and a page sharing one indirect array holding a specification by reference with none, one written in the array with none, and a number: eight refusals leave the saved bytes equal, the direct one gets Schema with the page's array still 3 entries and unchanged and the catalog's array no longer indirect, the referenced one goes Data then Source and the page's array shows it, a repeated name is refused, and the change survives a save; a missing `/AF`, a non-array, a 257-entry array whose first entry is a specification, and a reference to a string are refused; and an `/AF` of eleven entries: a stream, the catalog, an annotation-typed dictionary, a dictionary with none of the keys, a direct page-typed dictionary, an untyped annotation whose `/F` is a number and an untyped file attachment annotation whose `/FS` is a dictionary are seven refusals that leave the saved bytes equal, and four are accepted: a specification with `/F`, one with only `/UF`, one with `/FS /URL` and a string `/F`, and a typed one with only an `/EF` dictionary) and in automation `a_missing_relationship_is_set_through_the_tool_in_one_undo_step` (a hand-built file with entries Data, Source and none: entry 3 gets Supplement and `without_relationship` goes 1 to 0, entry 1 goes Data to Alternative and reports `previous` Data, two undos and a redo, and the saved file's own objects), `setting_a_relationship_refuses_with_nothing_written_and_a_modify_restriction_holds` (five bad calls and two missing arguments leave the list and the saved bytes the same; a copy of the file protected against changes refuses and keeps its count), `a_shared_array_is_reported_and_a_direct_specification_moves_the_catalog_to_its_own_array` (the catalog and a page list one array of a referenced and a direct specification: setting the direct one reports `array_copied` true and `shared_with_other_af` false, and `af_list` goes from 2 rows and 2 without a relationship to 4 rows and 3; - 2026-11-20 (M12, associated files without a relationship or an embedded file, headless, gap 22): What: `af_list` (read-only, headless) takes two new counts of the entries it listed: `without_relationship`, those whose `/AFRelationship` is missing or not a name (ISO 32000-2 requires one on a file specification an `/AF` array lists; a reader treats the entry as Unspecified), and `not_embedded`, those with no embedded file stream (a link to a file outside the document, which a PDF/A-3 file may not rely on). Both are worked out from the rows already read (at most 2,000, document and pages together), so they copy and allocate nothing more, and they count only the rows listed, not the document. Nothing else is validated and nothing is written. Engine: `AssociatedList::without_relationship` and `AssociatedList::not_embedded` (`pdfcraft_organize`), shown by the existing `Document::associated_list`. Parity `core.pdf20-associated-files` stays partial. Why: gap 22, PDF 2.0 extras; an associated file with no relationship name or no embedded stream is a common defect, and an agent had to scan every row of `af_list` to find one. Tested: `entries_without_a_relationship_name_or_an_embedded_file_are_counted` in organize (an embedded file with relationship Data, then three links added by hand to the catalog `/AF`: one named Source, one whose relationship is a string, one with none, and a number that is skipped; four rows, `without_relationship` 2 and `not_embedded` 3; a file with no `/AF` counts 0 and 0) and the end-to-end `entries_without_a_relationship_or_an_embedded_file_are_counted_through_the_tool` (a hand-built file whose `/AF` lists an embedded file with a relationship, a link with one and a link with none, so 1 and 2; a blank document counts 0 and 0). Making both counts always 0 made both new tests fail (verified twice, restored). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-automation` (organize: 275 library tests passed; automation: 74 library tests and 573 integration tests passed, one integration test failed, and 2 in another target passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched), the workspace sweep. - 2026-11-20 (M13, alternate-text suggestions, headless, gap 23): What: `ai_alt_text_suggest` (new, read-only, headless, suggest-only) asks the configured AI endpoint for alternate text for the tagged figures that have none: it sends the text of the pages those figures are on (never the pictures, so a suggestion rests on captions and nearby words) and asks for a JSON list of one short description per figure. `figures` names at most 100 figures to describe instead (numbers from `accessibility_figures`; more than 100, a number that is not a figure, or a figure drawn on no page is refused before anything is sent). At most 100 figures are asked about, only those that sit on a page, and at most 200 reply entries are read. A reply entry counts only for a figure that was asked about and whose page was really sent (the text is cut to about 60,000 characters); the first answer for a figure wins, and an `alt` is one line of at most 250 characters with whitespace folded. The result has `suggestions` (figure, page, alt), `asked`, `unanswered`, `ignored`, `not_sent`, `pages_sent` and `truncated`. With no figure to describe the endpoint is not contacted. Nothing is changed: the person reviews the list and applies the accepted ones with `accessibility_set_alt`. Engine: `pdfcraft_ai::alt_text_question` and `pdfcraft_ai::parse_alt_texts`; the tool shares the size limits, settings and sender (`ai_send`, which now takes an optional page list that replaces `pages`) of the other AI tools; it sends the pages its figures are on. Parity `a11y.alt-text-suggestions` goes from planned to partial. Why: gap 23, local AI provider; alternate-text suggestions were the missing half, and an agent had to read each figure's page and write descriptions by hand. Tested: `alt_texts_are_read_from_a_wrapped_reply_and_junk_is_dropped` and `the_alt_text_question_names_each_figure_and_its_page` in ai, and in automation `alt_text_suggestions_answer_only_the_figures_asked_about_and_change_nothing` (a three-figure page, figures 7 and 9 without text and 8 with; the stand-in reply answers 7, 8, 99, 7 again and 9 with a blank text, so one suggestion is kept, `unanswered` is [9] and `ignored` is 3; naming figure 8 asks about it; figure 6 is refused with nothing sent, as are 101 names; a reply that is not a list is an error; `accessibility_figures` is the same before and after), `alt_text_suggestions_send_nothing_when_no_figure_needs_text` (an untagged page and an unreachable endpoint succeed with nothing asked) `a_named_figure_drawn_on_no_page_is_refused_before_anything_is_sent` (a figure with no page, named, is refused) and `a_figure_on_a_page_the_size_limit_left_out_is_not_described` (page 1 holds about 70,000 characters, so the figure on page 2 is listed in `not_sent` and its answer is ignored, with `truncated` true). The existing `hostile_args::no_tool_escapes_the_root` caught that `chatgpt_session` was not resolved when nothing was to be sent; it is now resolved first. Six mutations were each restored: no repeat check, every figure asked, the reader's filter on empty or control-character text removed, no page filter, no limit of 100 names and no refusal of a figure with no page; each made one of the new tests fail (the figure test, the figure test, the reader test, the size-limit test, the figure test and the no-page test). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-ai -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-ai -p pdfcraft-automation` (ai: 36 library tests passed; automation: 74 library tests and 572 integration tests passed, one integration test failed, and 2 in another target passed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched), the workspace sweep. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-20 (M12, document part page ranges, headless, gap 22): `dpart_list` (read-only, headless) flags a document part whose pages run backwards: each part gains `range_inverted`, true when both `/Start` and `/End` name pages of the document and the end page comes before the start page, and the result gains `inverted_ranges`, how many of the listed parts have it. A part with no `/End`, or an `/End` that names no page of the document, is never inverted. It is worked out from the `start_page` and `end_page` already read, so it copies and allocates nothing more; overlaps and gaps between parts are not checked and nothing is written. Engine: `DocumentPart::range_inverted` and `DocumentPartList::inverted_ranges` (`pdfcraft_organize`), shown by the existing `Document::document_parts`. Parity `core.pdf20-dpart` stays partial. Why: gap 22, PDF 2.0 extras; a part that ends before it starts covers no pages in order, and an agent had to compare every `start_page` with its `end_page` to find one. Making the flag never true failed both new tests (verified, restored). - 2026-11-20 (M6, form submit actions, headless, gap 15): `form_submit_actions` (read-only, headless) reads what a field's Submit a Form actions ask for, and sends nothing: for each trigger (`mouse_up`, `mouse_down`, `mouse_enter`, `mouse_exit`, `on_focus`, `on_blur`) whose action is a Submit a Form action it returns the `url` (`/F`, a string or a file specification's `/F`; empty when `/F` is absent, a name, a number or a dictionary with no `/F` string; an address over 64 KiB is not copied and `url_too_long` is set), the `format` (`fdf`, or `html`, `xfdf`, `pdf` from `/Flags` bits 3, 6 and 9; when several are set the specification's own rules give PDF, then XFDF, then HTML: SubmitPDF makes all other flags ignored except GetMethod, and ExportFormat is meaningful only when SubmitPDF and XFDF are clear), `fields` (`all` when there is no `/Fields`; `only` or `except` with the names, by the Include/Exclude bit; `too_many` past 65,536 entries or 4 MiB of names, not listed) and the other choices of `/Flags` (`get_method`, `include_no_value_fields`, `submit_coordinates`, `include_append_saves`, `include_annotations`, `canonical_format`, `excl_non_user_annots`, `excl_f_key`, `embed_form`) with the raw `flags` (low 32 bits). The booleans are reported as set, whether or not the format uses them: with `pdf` the whole document is sent and only `get_method` applies; `get_method` and `submit_coordinates` are for `html` only; `include_append_saves`, `include_annotations`, `excl_f_key` and `embed_form` for `fdf` only; `excl_non_user_annots` only with `include_annotations`. A field named by reference is listed by its fully qualified name (the partial names up its `/Parent` chain, joined by dots); entries that name nothing (a reference to nothing, a dictionary with no `/T`, a number, `null`) are left out and counted in `fields_left_out`, so an empty list can come from a non-empty `/Fields`. It reads only the field's first widget and the action each trigger starts, not actions chained after it with `/Next`; the names are not matched against the form. The flag bit positions were written from memory of the PDF specification and could not be re-read from it in this build environment. Nothing is gathered or sent, and there is no prompt, button or email in the app, so submitting is still not done. The name reader shared with Reset a Form and Hide actions now takes the rest of its 4 MiB allowance into each name and checks every partial name's encoded length before building its text (a chain of up to 65 ancestors sharing one huge `/T` used to build one copy per ancestor before the allowance was checked), and reads a direct `/Fields` array without cloning it (this last change is not observable in a test). Engine: `Document::field_submit_actions`, from `pdfcraft_forms::submit_actions`. Parity `form.submit-form` goes from planned to partial. Tested: `submit_actions_read_the_target_the_field_list_and_every_flag`, `submit_flag_bits_each_mean_what_the_specification_says` (each of nine bits sets exactly one boolean), `submit_actions_refuse_to_copy_an_address_or_list_past_their_caps`, `a_submit_action_chained_after_another_is_not_read`, `submit_field_names_are_qualified_and_entries_that_name_nothing_are_counted` and `a_name_is_not_built_past_the_allowance_it_is_given` (forms; the last also through a Reset a Form action) and the end-to-end `submit_form_actions_are_listed_through_tools_and_send_nothing` (an action written by `form_set_actions`, a hand-built file with an XFDF every-field-except-two action, an address over the cap, a field without one, an unknown field refused). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-forms -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-forms -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation` (forms: 388 passed; engine: 954 passed, 4 ignored in all targets; automation: 74 library tests, 566 integration tests and 2 in another target passed, one failed); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; two mutations, each restored: the address cap in `submit_actions` removed made `submit_actions_refuse_to_copy_an_address_or_list_past_their_caps` fail, and the allowance check in `qualified_name_within` removed made `a_name_is_not_built_past_the_allowance_it_is_given` fail. Failed, not related: the known root-only `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing` (fails when run as root). Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched), the workspace sweep. - 2026-11-20 (M12, grid snap, headless, gap 16): `doc_grid_snap` (read-only, headless) gives where an `x` and/or `y` on a page lands on the nearest line of a grid you describe: `spacing` in `pt`, `in`, `mm` or `cm` (default `pt`; 0.01 to 14,400 points), `subdivisions` (1 to 100; lines no closer than 0.01 point), `offset_x` and `offset_y` (a vertical line runs through `offset_x` from the left edge, a horizontal one through `offset_y` from the top edge), and an optional `tolerance` (0 to 72 points; without one the nearest line always wins; on a tie the lower line wins, the same tie-break as `doc_guide_snap`, which unlike this tool defaults its tolerance to 4). The grid is measured in the guides' frame, from the displayed page's left and top edges (after rotation and `/UserUnit`), a line beyond the page is never chosen, the point must lie on the page, and the position of a line it snaps to is rounded to a thousandth of a point (a point that does not snap is returned as given). It is arithmetic only: nothing is drawn, stored, added as a guide or marked changed, and the app has no grid, grid Preferences or snapping while dragging (`view.grid` is now partial, no longer planned). It was not compared with where Acrobat measures its grid from. Engine: `Session::snap_to_grid` with `Grid` (`pdfcraft_engine::guides`). - 2026-11-20 (M8, redaction pattern locales, US employer, headless, gap 17): `redact_mark` takes a new pattern `ein`, a US Employer Identification Number: two digits, one hyphen and seven digits (`12-3456789`), or nine plain digits only directly after a label (EIN, E.I.N., EIN no, EIN number, FEIN, FEIN no, FEIN number or Employer Identification Number, any case); the first two digits must be an IRS-listed prefix (01 to 06, 10 to 16, 20 to 27, 30 to 48, 50 to 68, 71 to 77, 80 to 88, 90 to 95, 98 or 99; the list is from the IRS's published prefix table and could not be re-checked against the IRS page from this build environment). There is no checksum, so a match is a candidate; a spaced or no-break-space form is not matched, and a hyphenated number that sits in a longer hyphenated run of digits (the tail of a telephone number, say) is not matched. It does not overlap `ssn` or `itin`, which use a 3-2-4 layout. The matcher reads characters with `get()` and allocates only for its label check (a bounded 30-character window). It is in the pattern registry the UI list reads, with an English-only label; headless in `redact_mark`. Parity `protect.redact-pattern-locales` stays partial. Tested: `patterns::tests::us_employer_identification_numbers` and the end-to-end `redacting_a_us_employer_identification_number_through_tools` (mark, apply, save, reopen: both numbers gone, a number with an unused prefix, a Social Security number and a bare order number stay); the unit test fails under each of three single mutations. Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-redact -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test -p pdfcraft-redact -p pdfcraft-automation --no-fail-fast` (redact: 536 passed, 3 ignored in all targets; automation: 74 library tests, 564 integration tests passed and 2 in another target, one failed; failed, not related: `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing`, which fails when run as root); `cargo xtask layers`; `cargo xtask assets`; `cargo xtask parity`; `cargo xtask wasm`; three mutations of the matcher, one at a time and each restored (range 71 to 77 widened to 70, the hyphenated-run guard removed, the `ein` label broken), each made `patterns::tests::us_employer_identification_numbers` fail. Not run: ui-egui tests (not touched; the Search and Redact pattern list reads the same registry and was not looked at), the engine and workspace sweeps. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-20 (M11, linearization report, headless, gap 22): `doc_linearization` checks that each `/H` hint stream offset starts an object: it reads the `N G obj` header at that byte (at most 24 bytes are read there, however hostile the offset) and each `hint_streams` entry gains `object`, the number found or `null`. A file whose hint stream offset starts no object, while the hint stream lies inside the file, is no longer `valid` and says so in `problems`; before, only an offset past the end of the file was caught, so a stale or shifted `/H` (for example after the file was edited outside a PDF tool) still read as valid. Read-only and headless; the hint tables themselves are not decoded, the order of objects is not checked, `/E` and `/T` are checked only against the file length, and PdfCraft still does not write linearized files. Offsets count from the start of the file, as the existing checks do. Parity `core.linearization` stays partial. Tested: engine `linearization::tests::a_hint_stream_offset_must_start_an_object` (shifted offset, a header cut off by a letter, a number too long, offset at or past the end, `u64::MAX`) and the end-to-end `a_hint_stream_offset_that_starts_no_object_makes_the_file_not_valid` through `doc_linearization`; the valid-file fixtures of both now carry a real hint stream object, which the valid-file tests assert. - 2026-11-20 (M8, redaction pattern locales, US, headless, gap 17): `redact_mark` takes a new pattern `itin`, an Individual Taxpayer Identification Number (`itin`): nine digits as 3-2-4 joined by one consistent hyphen, space or no-break space, or nine plain digits only directly after a label (ITIN, I.T.I.N., ITIN no or ITIN number, any case); it must begin with 9 and its fourth and fifth digits must be 50 to 65, 70 to 88, 90 to 92 or 94 to 99, the ranges the IRS publishes for ITINs. There is no checksum, so a match is a candidate; the spelled-out name is not a label (the label reader looks back 30 characters). Overlap with `ssn`: the Social Security pattern never matches a number beginning with 9, so a 3-2-4 number is an `ssn` match or an `itin` match, never both; an `itin` match needs the IRS middle digits, so a 9xx number outside them is matched by neither, and `ssn` also accepts mixed separators that `itin` does not. The matcher reads characters with `get()` and allocates only for its label check (a bounded 30-character window) and nine or eleven digits. It is in the pattern registry the UI list reads, with an English-only label; headless in `redact_mark`. Parity `protect.redact-pattern-locales` stays partial. Tested: `patterns::tests::us_individual_taxpayer_identification_numbers` and the end-to-end `redacting_a_us_individual_taxpayer_identification_number_through_tools` (mark, apply, save, reopen: both numbers gone, an out-of-range number, a Social Security number and a bare order number stay); the unit test fails under each of four single mutations. - 2026-11-19 (M12, never crash, revisions listing: `doc_revisions` matches each signed signature to the revision whose byte range its signed length falls in by one binary search over the revision ends, instead of scanning every signature for every revision. A file's revisions (one per incremental update, as many as fit in the file) and its signature fields (up to 20,000) are both file-controlled, so 100,000 revisions with 20,000 signature fields cost 2 billion comparisons before and cost 20,000 searches now. Output is unchanged: the same revisions, in the same order, each listing the signatures that sign exactly it in signature order, and a signature whose length is 0 or past the last revision signs none. Each signature lands in at most one revision, so the names listed are at most those the document already holds. Not covered: the number of revisions listed is still as many as the file has (output linear in the file), and `revision_ends` itself is in craft-pdf, which scans for `%%EOF` from each revision's cross-reference offset. Tests: automation lib `revision_signer_tests::many_revisions_and_signatures_are_matched_in_near_linear_time` (200,000 revisions and 200,000 signatures; 27.8 s with the scan back, milliseconds now) and `a_signature_belongs_to_the_revision_its_length_falls_in` (boundaries, 0, past the end, order; the existing `digital_ids_signing_and_validation_through_tools` still checks the tool). - 2026-11-19 (M12, never crash, document parts: `dpart_list` lists the `/DPM` entries of all nodes together from one allowance of 4 MiB (`MAX_DPM_BYTES`), each entry costing its key's length, its raw length when its value is a string or a name, and 32 (`DPM_ENTRY_COST`), charged before it is listed and again for every node that lists it. Once one entry does not fit, the allowance is spent: from then on no node's `/DPM` is read at all, and every node with one is flagged in `metadata_truncated` (a direct `/DPM` only when it has entries; an indirect one is flagged without being resolved, so even an empty one is; also set past 32 entries, as before). Before, a `/DPM` shared by up to 5000 nodes was parsed again for every node (a string over 32 MiB is not kept by the document's cache, about 70 ms per node), every direct value in it was cloned per node (strings, arrays, dictionaries) and scanned in full, a long name or invalid-UTF-8 key was copied in full, and the 5000 x 32 x 400-character output was bounded by nothing the file held. Keys and names are now cut after the 800 bytes that 200 characters can take, and a direct `/DParts` array, group or `/DPM` is borrowed instead of cloned. Listed text is unchanged. What is charged: key length, string or name length, 32 per entry; numbers, booleans and containers cost their key and 32. Outside the allowance: while it lasts, each node resolves its `/DPM` and up to 32 values in it, and an indirect value is resolved before its length is known and charged, so the one entry that does not fit is read in full once (a 33 MiB string costs one parse, then the allowance is spent); each such node prepares up to 32 entries of text (keys and values cut to 200 characters) that are dropped with the node, and nothing is kept between nodes (no memo); the node's `/DParts` references are still read up to 5000 per node, and a `/DParts` array shared by 5000 nodes costs 25,000,000 reference reads and, if it is large enough to be dropped from the document's cache, a parse per node; the catalog's `/DPartRoot` and `/RecordLevel` are read once; no other reader of `/DPM` exists (grep of the workspace), and `dpart_list` flags per node. Tested: organize `document_part_metadata_is_read_within_one_allowance_whoever_shares_it` (one assertion each for the key, string, name and per-entry charges, each shown failing with only that charge removed), `once_the_metadata_allowance_is_spent_later_dpms_are_flagged_and_not_read` (a large entry fails and a later small one would fit, in the same node and in later nodes, direct, indirect and empty; counts the `/DPM` dictionaries read, 2; failed with the spent state never set and with the skip removed), `a_metadata_object_too_big_to_list_is_not_parsed_again_by_every_node_that_shares_it` (a 33 MiB object in a file-backed document shared by 300 nodes, as a dictionary and as a value; 20 to 27 s with the old reader, with the spent state never set, and with the skip removed; under a second now) and `document_part_metadata_keys_and_values_are_cut_by_characters_not_bytes` (a regression test, which the old code also passed); automation `dpart_list_flags_the_nodes_whose_shared_metadata_did_not_fit_the_allowance` (failed with the allowance lifted). - 2026-11-19 (M12, never crash, JavaScripts listing: `doc_javascripts` (and the Edit all JavaScripts window) read field names, document script names and bookmark titles within an allowance of 16 MiB of stored text per listing, charged by stored length before each string is decoded (a field name by each of its parts, every time it is built, and only for fields and widgets that have an /A or /AA), and copy them into entries within a second 16 MiB, charged by decoded length before each copy. - 2026-11-19 (M12, never crash, fonts listing: `doc_info`'s fonts (the Fonts tab) no longer collect every page's font resources afresh. A page whose font dictionaries are the same ones, in the same order, as a page already read is skipped; the dictionaries are the ones the readers of a page's fonts take: the page's own `/Resources` written in it as a dictionary, and an indirect `/Resources` dictionary of the page or an ancestor (not an ancestor's direct one, not a stream), each with its `/Font` dictionary, indirect or direct. Many pages inheriting one shared font dictionary cost it once instead of its entries per page. The other pages are read within one allowance of 1 GiB of font resource entries, an entry costing its name's length plus 64 bytes and a shared font dictionary measured once, charged before the page's fonts are collected; a page that does not fit is skipped, and `doc_info`'s `warnings` then says `Some fonts may be missing from the list` (a refresh of the list after a content edit, `refresh_fonts`, replaces the note with the new reading's; for an XFA document the note is not kept among the notes carried across a full re-read). Each font dictionary is also turned into a listing entry once however many pages and resource names reach it, and a font's `/Subtype`, `/Encoding` name and descendant `/Ordering`, which can be indirect and so shared by many fonts, are read only when at most 256 bytes (a longer one is read as absent, and `warnings` says so with the same `Some fonts may be missing from the list` note, so its type, encoding or substitute may be wrong). The `/BaseFont` is a direct name in its own font dictionary and is copied once per distinct font. The fonts listed for an ordinary document are unchanged and a page's own font still hides an inherited one of the same resource name. What is charged: font resource entries (name length plus 64) per page read. Not charged from the allowance but capped: the three indirect-capable fields above (256 bytes each, checked before copying); the direct `/BaseFont` and descriptor reads are once per distinct font dictionary and bounded by the file. Not covered: a page that has a font dictionary of its own still pays, within the allowance, for the shared ones it inherits; the Fonts tab still says `No fonts are referenced` when every page was skipped (the warning is in `doc_info` and the document's warnings, not a separate field); the page-tree walk and per-page resource copies elsewhere are unchanged. Tested: `pages_sharing_a_font_dictionary_read_it_once` (counts reads), `font_resources_past_the_allowance_are_flagged`, `an_ancestors_direct_resources_do_not_hide_another_pages_fonts`, `stream_resources_do_not_hide_another_pages_fonts` and `a_font_reached_many_times_is_built_once` (render), `an_overlong_font_subtype_is_not_copied_per_font_and_is_flagged`, `ordinary_subtype_encoding_and_ordering_are_kept` and `own_fonts_still_shadow_inherited_ones_and_nothing_is_flagged` (behaviour kept) (render), `the_font_list_note_follows_the_latest_reading` (engine, the helper `replace_font_list_warnings`: kept once when still incomplete, dropped when complete, added when new, other warnings untouched; it fails with the removal of the old note reverted) and `the_font_list_warning_survives_refresh_fonts` (engine, through a session). - 2026-11-19 (M12, never crash, calculation order: the `/CO` entries that calculate nothing (`CalculationOrder::extra`, `form_calculation_order`'s `extra`, the Calculation order panel's count) are listed within an allowance of 16 MiB of text in all, each entry costing its text (the field's name, or the reference or `not a reference`) plus 24 bytes, charged before the name is copied; entries past it are counted in the new `extra_omitted` (tool output `extra_omitted`, 0 for an ordinary form) and not listed. Before, one long field name written in many `/CO` entries (a 1 MiB name in 20 entries) was copied once per entry, so a file of a few megabytes could cost gigabytes, on every read of the order and on every recalculation (which builds the same list and discards it). Refusals are flagged: `extra_omitted` is in the tool result and the panel adds it to its count, `fixable` is true when only omitted entries remain (setting the order writes `/CO` afresh with the calculated fields once each, so Fix order removes them), and the listed names are in `/CO` order. Covered: `calculation_order`, `set_calculation_order` and form recalculation (`run_order`), the only readers that build the list. Not covered: the `/CO` array itself is still copied whole per call (`co_entries`), and `keep_listed` copies and scans it once per field whose calculation is added or removed, so a bulk `form_set_props` of up to 1,000 fields over a `/CO` of a million entries still costs about that many entries per field; the removal of a deleted field from `/CO` (author/lifecycle.rs) is unchanged. - 2026-11-19 (M12, never crash, page boxes: a page box (media, crop, bleed, trim, art; direct, indirect or inherited) is read as the viewer reads a rectangle: its first four entries, each resolved, which must all be numbers; a non-number among them, or fewer than four entries, is no box, and no later entry is read. So one array shared by every page (for example 1,000,000 entries over 100,000 pages) costs at most four entries per box instead of its length per page and box. Applies to `page_boxes`, `page_set_box` and the Set Page Boxes dialog (`rect_of` in organize), the printer-marks box reader (`pdfcraft_organize_boxes`, edit) and the preflight page-size check (`page_box_out_of_range`, PDF/A 6.1.13). Behaviour: an array of exactly four numbers reads as before; an array of more than four entries whose first four are numbers, which the old readers refused (organize reported it `ignored`, edit defaulted it, preflight skipped its size check), now reads as its first four, and preflight now flags such a box that is too small or large; an array whose first four are not all numbers, but which holds four numbers among more entries, used to read and is now no box, as in the viewer. Not covered: the page-tree walk still clones each page's dictionary and copies a /Pages node's inherited values into each page, so a huge direct inherited /MediaBox, /CropBox or /Resources over many pages is still copied per page; other array readers (annotation /Rect in annot, redaction verify extract) are unchanged. - 2026-11-19 (M11, Print readiness thresholds in the dialog, gap 18): the Print readiness dialog has three fields, Total ink limit (%), Least bleed (mm) and Least resolution (ppi), for the `ink_limit`, `min_bleed_mm` and `min_ppi` of `print_readiness_with_options`. An empty field is the default (the engine's own, so the report and its wording are exactly what they were). A typed value is applied with Enter or when the field loses the keyboard, not on every keystroke, because applying measures the document again on the UI thread (the PDF/A check and every page and image); the typed text stays in the field and the line under it says `Press Enter to apply` while it waits. Each field has one line under it that is always there, so the dialog does not move: the allowed range and default, in red the reason when the text was refused (a value that is not a number, NaN, infinite or outside the engine's range: ink 100 to 400, bleed 0 to 50 mm, ppi 36 to 1200, from its own `TAC_LIMIT_RANGE`, `MIN_BLEED_RANGE` and `MIN_PPI_RANGE`; a refusal changes nothing and says to use digits 0-9). A comma is accepted as the decimal mark, full-width digits are read as digits, and -0 is 0. Reset to defaults (disabled at the defaults) clears all three. The findings, the Measured lines and Copy report (which gets a line naming each changed threshold) all come from the one measuring that follows the options; the engine's default constants (`TAC_LIMIT_PERCENT`, `MIN_BLEED_MM`, `WARN_PPI`) are now public and give the field hints. The report's scroll area is capped by the window height, so Close and Copy report stay in reach on a short window. The values are remembered in the settings JSON (`print_readiness` with `ink_limit`, `min_bleed_mm` and `min_ppi`; a default one is not written, each key is read alone, and a missing, mistyped or out-of-range saved value keeps the current choice). Headless: `ui.set readiness-ink-limit`, `readiness-min-bleed-mm` and `readiness-min-ppi` (a number or `default`; anything else is refused with `<key> must be a number from X to Y, or default` and changes nothing; an open report is measured again once; setting the value a field already has still puts its text right) and `ui.state.print_readiness` (each a number, or null for the default); the control docs say so. If the engine ever refused a threshold the error is shown, not replaced by a default report. The new labels and messages use `tl!` and are English only: translations are rows in craft-i18n and show once `craft-i18n.rev` moves, and a missing row falls back to English. The tools are unchanged (`print_readiness` already took all three). - 2026-11-19 (M12, Compare ignore punctuation, headless, gap 21): `doc_compare` takes `ignore_punctuation` (boolean, default false; `null` is the default, a non-boolean is refused): words that differ only in sentence punctuation are not counted as text changes, and each change still shows its own text. Removed: sentence punctuation (. , ; : ! ? quotes and apostrophes, brackets, hyphen, underscore, Unicode dashes, quotes, bullets and ellipsis, guillemets, inverted marks, pilcrow, middle dot, CJK stops). Kept, because they change a figure or name: symbols (`$ + = < > ^ | ~`), `# % & @ * / \`, the section sign, per mille and basis point signs, primes, the fraction slash, a `.` or `,` between two digits (10.5 is not 105, 1.000 is not 1,000) and a dash before a digit unless a letter precedes it (-5 is not 5). Words made only of such marks equal one another (a hyphen and an em dash), never a missing word or a word with another character; the section sign stays, so `§5` and `5` differ. It combines with `ignore_case`, affects the text comparison only (visual regions still see the pixels), and `doc_compare_report`, `doc_compare_mark` and the Compare dialog do not take it. The new text is English-only (tool descriptions). Engine: `Session::compare_in_options`, `pdfcraft_compare::compare_options` and `Options`. - 2026-11-19 (M12, never crash, headless): the XMP namespace scope that PDF/A and PDF/UA preflight use to read a metadata packet (`NsScope` in `crates/preflight/src/xmp.rs`, shared by the identification, property-type and extension-schema checks) is a shared chain of the declarations each element adds, not a full copy per element. Cause: `child` cloned every declaration in scope for every element, and the type and extension checks keep one scope per child of a property, array or schema container at once, so a packet whose ancestors declare hundreds of namespaces (the reader allows 128 attributes per tag and 4,096 in all) and holds tens of thousands of items copied the declarations once per item (list size x declarations of memory and time). An element that declares nothing now shares its parent's chain; lookups, results and findings are unchanged, and nothing is dropped or refused. Only the XMP reads in PDF/A, PDF/UA and PDF/X preflight reach this code. Tested: `pdfcraft-preflight` (`a_scope_is_shared_by_elements_that_declare_nothing`, which fails without the change because every item copied the declarations above it, and `a_wide_array_under_many_declarations_is_checked_within_memory`, a 40,000-item bag under 600 declarations, a regression guard that took 0.89 s before the change and 0.10 s after, in the same run, not one that fails outright, and `an_element_that_declares_gets_its_own_chain_and_shadows_as_before`). The engine test `printcheck::tests::many_form_drawings_with_a_big_resource_dictionary_stay_fast` failed once under load in the full engine run and passed alone, with and without this change (unrelated).
