- 2026-11-19 (M12, never crash, headless): the name-tree reader behind attachments, page templates and carried attachments (`name_tree_entries`) spends one allowance of 32 MiB (`MAX_NAME_TREE_KEY_BYTES`) of copied key text per tree, charged for every pair, and refuses a tree past it. Cause: it copied each pair's key, so a `/Names` array whose pairs all use one indirect key string (`7 0 R`, a 1 MiB string) copied 1 MiB per pair: measured 12 MiB of file against 2 GiB of entries at 2,000 pairs, and the attachment and template rewrites write every key back inline. Sharing the key in memory would not have helped the rewrite, and dropping entries would lose attachments silently, so nothing is dropped or deduplicated (attachments keep every pair, duplicate names included, to be made unique later): past the allowance the call returns an error. Adding, deleting, editing the description of or removing by reference an attachment, `set_template_hidden`, and Combine (of a source whose pages are all included, the only import that carries attachments) fail with an error naming the attachments (EmbeddedFiles) or the name tree's 32 MiB key total (Combine also names the file and says to combine a page selection to leave its attachments behind) and change nothing; `attachment_keys` and the template listing and spawn lookup show the pairs read before the allowance (a listing, never written back). Ordinary files, whose keys are file names, are unaffected (the allowance is thousands of times what the 4,096-name template cap reads). Tested: `pdfcraft-organize` (`attachment_names_sharing_one_large_key_are_refused_not_copied_per_pair`, which fails without the allowance at 104,857,801 bytes copied for 100 pairs on a 1 MiB key, `template_names_sharing_one_large_key_refuse_a_rewrite`, `attachment_names_that_repeat_a_small_shared_key_are_all_kept`, and the existing attachment and template tests). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-automation -p pdfcraft-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-automation -p pdfcraft-engine` (automation fails only the known unrelated `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing`); `cargo xtask layers`, `assets`, `parity`, `wasm`. Not run: the app UI. Known limit: a legitimate tree whose keys total over 32 MiB cannot be edited or carried. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-19 (M12, never crash, headless): Combine, Insert, Extract and Split resolve a link's, bookmark's or GoTo action's named destination from one index of the source's `/Dests` dictionary and `/Names /Dests` tree, built the first time one is needed and shared by the copiers made from one page copy (the sub-copiers for repeated pages and actions; the bookmark mapper of an Insert has its own, so an Insert builds the index twice), instead of walking the tree once per link. Cause: `Copier::map_dest` called `named_dest`, a full tree lookup, per link, so a file of N links to a tree of M names cost N x M steps (320,400 for 400 x 400 in the test; the earlier per-lookup shared-array guard did not help across lookups). Nothing is dropped or limited: the same name finds the same value as before (the dictionary beats the tree, the first tree entry beats a later one). The index is built in one walk that clones a key and value only when a name is new, and skips a key that is one indirect string shared by many pairs after the first, so pairs sharing one large key do not copy it per pair (an earlier draft collected every pair first and copied a shared 1 MiB key once per pair: about 2 GiB for 2,000 pairs). Memory is the distinct names and their values, bounded by the file, not by the number of links or pairs. Known limit: `name_tree_entries` (attachment listing, templates, carried attachments) still copies each pair's key, so pairs sharing one large key amplify there as before. Tested: `pdfcraft-organize` (`many_links_to_named_destinations_read_the_name_tree_once`, which fails without the index at 320,400 steps, `a_named_destination_dictionary_entry_beats_a_tree_entry_and_the_first_tree_entry_wins`, `pairs_sharing_one_large_key_do_not_copy_it_per_pair`, which fails when every pair's key is copied at 131,074,001 steps and key bytes, and the existing named-destination tests). Ran: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-automation -p pdfcraft-engine --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match`; `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-automation -p pdfcraft-engine` (automation fails only the known unrelated `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing`); `cargo xtask layers`, `assets`, `parity`, `wasm`. Not run: the app UI. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-19 (M12, form values, never crash, headless): reading a form spends one allowance of 64 MiB (`MAX_VALUE_BYTES`) over the string, name and array text of all its fields' `/V` and `/DV`: each text costs its decoded length plus 24, and each array entry that is not text costs 24, so a shared array of millions of numbers is refused without a walk (an array whose entries alone cost more than what is left is refused in O(1)). A text is checked on its raw length before it is decoded, and a value in the file can be up to about three times as long as what it costs. Cause: `read::values_of` copied a string or array value into every field that names it (by reference or inherited from a parent), so fields sharing one array of tens of thousands of strings held fields x entries strings (9,000,000 for 300 fields x 30,000 entries, gigabytes at larger sizes); only text streams were charged to a budget. A field whose `/V` or `/DV` does not fit reads with that value empty and is flagged `Field::value_omitted` (counted by `Fields::value_omitted` and `pdfcraft_forms::value_omitted`), as a choice field with unread `/Opt` is flagged `options_omitted`; an object named by reference that was refused is remembered, so other fields naming it skip it and later small values still read (a refused direct or inherited value spends what was read of it). Such a field is left alone: `set_value`, shared values and recalculation refuse or skip it, Clear Form and a ResetForm action keep it (`default_value_known` is false, so a script's `default_known` is too; the reset skip also keeps `write_value_impl`'s refusal from aborting the whole reset), every redraw of a text or choice field skips it (`with_field_appearances`, the signed-file display copy, an edit of its tooltip or font through `set_props`, `redraw_field` and `redraw_fields` for an XFA layout, a copy, a redaction), an AF calculation that reads it keeps its total (a JavaScript calculation still reads it as empty text: a known limit), the XFA data layer is given no data for it so the datasets are neither rewritten empty for it nor read into it (and `strip_xfa` does not count it as differing), text, XML, XFDF and FDF exports all leave it out instead of writing empty or Off and `pdfcraft_xfdf::fields_left_out` names the fields left out (`doc_export_data` returns them as `left_out`; the desktop export notice does not mention them yet), opening the file records one repair-log line, and `form_fields` gives it `value_omitted: true`. Its `/V` and `/DV` stay in the file as they were. Known limit: Clear Password Values removes a flagged password field's `/V` as for any other but does not restore it to a `/DV` that was not read, and leaves its old masked appearance. A text stream past its own budget still reads as empty without a flag, as before. Tested: forms `repeated_value_arrays_share_one_text_allowance`, `value_text_allowance_boundaries` (exact fit and one over for a string, a name, an array and entries that are not text), `a_shared_array_of_numbers_is_refused_without_a_walk`, `a_field_whose_value_was_not_read_is_left_alone` (set_value, reset, default_value_known, with_field_appearances), `a_property_edit_and_a_calculation_leave_an_unread_value_alone`, `a_value_inherited_from_a_parent_that_does_not_fit_flags_every_kid`, `a_refused_array_spends_what_was_read_unless_it_is_remembered_by_reference`, `repeated_value_streams_share_one_decode_budget`; xfdf `exports_do_not_write_a_value_that_was_not_read_as_empty` (XML, XFDF and FDF, `fields_left_out`); engine `fields_with_unread_values_are_noted_as_a_repair`, `an_xfa_sync_gives_no_data_for_a_field_whose_value_was_not_read` (the data handed to the XFA layer; the datasets rewrite itself was not run on a real XFA form); automation `form_fields_flags_a_field_whose_value_was_not_read`, `doc_export_data_reports_the_fields_it_left_out`. Each forms, xfdf and engine test fails with its guard removed (the allowance, set_value, default_value_known, redraw, redraw_widgets_of, the AF calculation skip, the refused-array spend, the FDF skip, the XFA mapping); the automation tests were not reverted. Not run: ui-egui tests (its one change is a field literal in a test helper; it was only compiled by clippy), redact tests (it calls the guarded redraw but was not touched). Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-19 (M12, form choice fields, never crash, headless): reading a form spends one allowance of 1,000,000 `/Opt` entries and 16 MiB of option text over all its fields. A combo or list box whose list no longer fits reads with no options and is flagged `Field::options_omitted` (also `Fields::options_omitted`, `pdfcraft_forms::options_omitted`): `set_value`, shared values and `set_props` of its options return an error saying the options were not read, Clear Form, a ResetForm action and a recalculation skip it per field (it keeps its value, the rest of the form still resets and recalculates), a redraw and the display copy (`with_field_appearances`) leave its appearance as the file has it, opening the file records one repair-log line, and `form_fields` gives it `options_omitted: true`. A text field's `/Opt` is not read, and a check box or radio group reads only a list with one entry per widget (the export values `Field::export_of` uses); a refused button list falls back to the on-state names, as with no `/Opt`, and is not counted. A text longer than the bytes left is refused before it is decoded and a refusal spends the rest of the byte allowance and the list's entries, so many fields naming one long list do not each decode it. The skip covers the field's own calculation and a reset: a script writing into the field (`scripting::apply_changes`, `setItems` aside) still gets the error. An export-value edit of a button group is refused when the file's own `/Opt` is not one entry per widget, as before. Its `/Opt`, `/V` and `/I` stay in the file untouched unless a script's `setItems` replaces them. Cause: `read::walk` cloned and parsed the `/Opt` array into option strings once per field, so fields naming one shared indirect array cost fields x options (3,000 x 3,000 read in 4 s of a debug build, gigabytes at larger sizes), and entries naming one long string object copied it per entry. Tested: forms `choice_fields_sharing_one_huge_opt_array_stop_at_the_form_allowance`, `options_naming_one_long_string_stop_at_the_byte_allowance`, `a_choice_field_with_unread_options_is_not_written_or_redrawn`, `a_twin_with_unread_options_is_refused_a_shared_value`, `a_reset_skips_a_choice_field_with_unread_options_and_resets_the_rest`, `a_value_change_still_works_with_a_calculated_choice_field_whose_options_were_not_read`, `only_choice_fields_are_flagged_for_unread_options`, `a_refused_option_list_spends_its_entries`, `a_button_export_edit_is_refused_when_opt_does_not_match_the_widgets` (each fails with its part of the fix removed), engine `choice_fields_with_unread_options_are_noted_as_a_repair`, automation `form_fields_flags_a_choice_field_whose_options_were_not_read`. Ran: cargo fmt --check, clippy and tests on pdfcraft-forms, pdfcraft-engine, pdfcraft-automation, cargo xtask layers, assets, parity, wasm. Not run: ui-egui tests (its test constructor only gained the new field), UI; shared `/V` arrays are read per field the same way and are not limited yet. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-19 (M12, form actions, never crash, headless): a `ResetForm` `/Fields` or `Hide` `/T` array is read only up to 65,536 entries and 4 MiB of field names, and past either the action is not read (the Actions tab lists it as an action of another type, kept as it is; a push button runs nothing) instead of a shortened list. Cause: `action_fields` built each entry's fully qualified name (up to 64 ancestors' partial names joined) with no limit on entries or total size, so a few thousand entries naming widgets under one chain of long partial names built that chain once per entry (gigabytes from a small file); a shortened list would have reset or hidden the wrong fields (a reset that excludes the listed fields would reset more), so it refuses instead. Tested: forms `action_field_lists_over_the_limits_are_not_read` (fails without the fix at its first assert, the Hide button's action is read with its 6.4 MB names). Ran: cargo fmt --check, clippy and tests on pdfcraft-forms, cargo xtask layers, assets, parity, wasm. Not run: tests of other crates, UI. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-19 (M12, named destinations, never crash, headless): the named-destination readers now read an indirect `/Names` or `/Kids` array that several name-tree nodes share once, by the first node to name it, as the attachment tree already did (#87). Cause: render `name_tree_raw` (the `/Names /Dests` index behind bookmarks and links in `doc_info`) rescanned the shared array for every node and charged its keys to the 16 MiB key allowance each time, so a file of N + M objects cost N x M steps and its later distinct names were left unindexed (bookmark and link destinations then had no page); organize `name_tree_lookup` (links copied by extract, split and Combine) cloned and scanned the shared array per node. The same shared-array reading now covers render `name_tree`/`name_tree_find` and organize `name_tree_entries` (#87). `/Names` arrays and `/Kids` arrays are recorded in separate sets, and only once known to be arrays, so a `/Kids` entry naming an object that another node uses as its `/Names` array (or a `/Names` entry naming a node) hides nothing; each array is read at most once as each kind. Estimates unchanged. Tested: render and organize `a_kids_array_that_another_node_uses_as_its_names_array_hides_nothing` (fail with one shared set: the bookmark has no page, the lookup misses) and render `a_names_array_shared_by_many_dests_nodes_spends_the_key_allowance_once` and organize `a_named_destination_lookup_reads_a_shared_names_array_once` fail without the fix (the bookmark has no page; 320401 steps against a bound of 3200) and pass with it. Ran: cargo fmt --check, clippy -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match with --all-targets, cargo test --no-fail-fast, cargo xtask layers, assets, parity and wasm on pdfcraft-render and pdfcraft-organize (results in the pull request). Not run: the app UI.
- 2026-11-19 (M4, bookmarks, never crash, headless): listing bookmarks of an editable document (`bookmark_list`, Bookmarks panel, `bookmark_export`/`bookmark_copy`, Cut and Copy of bookmarks, split by bookmarks: organize `bookmarks`/`bookmark_page`) now spends bookmark titles from one 16 MiB allowance per listing call, and keeps only the first six items of an explicit destination array. Cause: `title_of` decoded each bookmark's `/Title` into a new `String` with no limit and `explicit_dest` cloned the whole `/Dest` array, and both can be one indirect object that every bookmark names; a file of a few thousand objects could make one `bookmark_list` page of 1,000 bookmarks answer with 1,000 copies of a multi-megabyte title (5 MiB x 1,000 = 5 GiB; computed from the sizes, not run at that scale) or copy a long shared array per bookmark. The inspector's outline already charged titles to an allowance; this path did not. Silent change: a title that no longer fits what is left of the allowance lists as empty (a later, shorter title may still list); each call, so each page of `bookmark_list`, has its own allowance. `Bookmark::title_dropped` records it, and `outline_tree` (so `bookmark_export`, `bookmark_copy` and UI Copy/Cut) sets `truncated` when a title was dropped, which makes Cut keep its source and the tools report `truncated: true` (the UI's Copy note still says only "nested too deep", not looked at). Split by bookmarks reads top-level bookmarks, so a dropped title there only changes the output file name. Page labels, links and layers were read first and were already bounded (label tree work, depth, prefix and total-byte limits; shared-node seen sets). Tested: `tests::bookmarks_sharing_one_huge_title_are_listed_within_the_title_allowance` (40 bookmarks sharing one 1 MiB title: 40 MiB listed before; at most 16 MiB, 16 titles per call, and `title_dropped` set on the rest after), `tests::an_outline_with_a_title_past_the_allowance_is_exported_as_truncated` (`outline_tree` sets `truncated`) and `tests::a_long_destination_array_is_copied_only_as_far_as_it_is_read` (a 100,005-item array); the first and last shown failing with the fix stashed (`titles listed 41943040 bytes`, `copied whole: 100005`). Ran on the final tree: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match --all-targets` on pdfcraft-organize and pdfcraft-automation; `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on pdfcraft-organize, pdfcraft-engine and pdfcraft-automation (all pass except `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing`, which fails on the tree without this change); `cargo xtask layers`, `assets`, `parity`. Not run: the 5 GiB case itself, the app UI, fuzzing, the wasm check after this last edit (run before it, on the earlier tree). Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-19 (M12, attachments, name trees, never crash, headless): an indirect `/Names` array of the `/EmbeddedFiles` name tree that several nodes name is now read once, by the first node to name it, as `name_tree_find` already did. Before, a file with N leaf nodes all naming one array of M file specifications listed N x M attachments in `doc_info` (render `name_tree`; 300 x 300 gave 90000) and `attachment_add`, `attachment_edit`, `attachment_delete`, `af_*`, page-template listing and Combine (organize `name_tree_entries`) copied or rewrote all of them; the file is about N + M objects, so a file of a few tens of thousands of each asks for hundreds of millions of entries (computed from the object sizes, not run). Silent change: a tree that lists the same array twice now shows its entries once. Organize `name_tree_entries` also reads a shared indirect `/Kids` array once; render `name_tree` still scans a shared `/Kids` array once per node (its nodes are deduplicated, so the cost is the array's length per node, not multiplied entries). Follow-ups, not done: render `name_tree_raw` (named destinations) and organize `name_tree_lookup` (`/Dests`) read a shared array per node. Organize keeps nodes and arrays in separate seen-sets and records an array only once it resolves to one, so a malformed `/Kids` entry naming a `/Names` array object, or `/Names` naming a node dictionary, does not hide a real entry. Tested: organize unit tests `tests::a_names_array_shared_by_many_nodes_is_listed_once`, `tests::a_kids_entry_naming_the_names_array_does_not_hide_its_entries` and `tests::a_kids_entry_naming_a_node_dictionary_does_not_hide_its_entries` (the last two fail on the previous single seen-set, shown by running them against the code before this change), and end-to-end `shared_name_arrays::attachment_add_keeps_each_entry_of_a_shared_names_array_once`: its `doc_info` 'before' assert fails without the render fix (90000 listed) and its assert on the name `attachment_add` returns fails without the organize fix (it returns "f0000.txt (10)" instead of "new.txt", shown by reverting each fix once). Ran on the final tree: `cargo fmt --check`; `cargo clippy -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-render -p pdfcraft-engine -p pdfcraft-automation --all-targets -- -D warnings -A clippy::collapsible_match` (the allow is for a pre-existing finding at crates/xfdf/src/xfdf.rs:326); `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on those four crates (all pass except `batch_failures::read_only_output_folder_publishes_nothing`, which fails when run as root); `cargo xtask layers`, `assets` and `parity`; `cargo check --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -p pdfcraft-organize -p pdfcraft-render`. - 2026-11-19 (M8, redaction pattern locales, UK, headless, gap 17): `redact_mark` takes a new pattern `nino` (UK National Insurance number, matched by format only: two letters, six digits and a final letter A to D, in either case, with an optional single space or no-break space after the letters, between the digit pairs and before the final letter, in any of those places and all the same character; a first letter of D, F, I, Q, U or V, a second of D, F, I, O, Q, U or V, and the prefixes BG, GB, KN, NK, NT, TN and ZZ are not matched; neither end may touch a letter or digit). - 2026-11-19 (M12, structure namespace duplicates, headless, gap 22): `ns_list` now also reports `duplicate_ns`: each `/NS` value that more than one listed namespace dictionary carries (a namespace is identified by its `/NS`, so two dictionaries with the same `/NS` describe one namespace twice (ISO 32000-2 14.7.4)), with `ns`, `count` and the first 16 object numbers, in order of first appearance, computed over the at most 1,000 namespaces read. Identifiers are compared as the listed text, which is cut at 200 characters, so two longer identifiers that agree in their first 200 characters count as repeats; dictionaries without a string `/NS` are not compared, and the same reference listed twice is still one dictionary. Nothing else is validated (use by structure elements, role map consistency) and nothing is written; `core.pdf20-namespaces` stays partial. - 2026-11-18 (M13, AI redaction suggestions, headless, gap 23): `ai_redaction_suggest` is a new tool that sends the page text (same pages, size, privacy and copy-permission rules as `ai_ask`) to the endpoint the user set up and asks for personal or confidential passages as a JSON list. The text is cut to about 60,000 characters like `ai_ask`'s; `pages_sent` lists the pages the model really received and `truncated` says whether text was left out, and only passages that occur in the text it was given (whitespace-normalised, case-sensitive, while `redact_mark` words match ignoring case) are returned, each with its pages and occurrence count, with `not_in_text` counting the rest. The reply is read as the first JSON array in it that parses and holds a string (brackets of the model's own around it are ignored; at most 200 strings of at most 200 characters, whitespace runs folded, repeats once); a reply with no list is an error. It marks and changes nothing: the person passes accepted passages to `redact_mark` as `words`. A model can miss or over-flag, so it is a starting list, not a check, and there is no UI; `misc.ai-redaction-suggest` is partial. `ai_status` and the module notes now say that summarize, translate and redaction suggestions send text too. Tested: `crates/automation/tests/it/ai.rs::redaction_suggestions_keep_only_passages_that_are_in_the_text_and_mark_nothing`, `redaction_suggestions_only_count_text_the_model_was_given` (a page over the limit; shown failing with the filter reading the full text) and the bad-page error check (end to end against a local stand-in server, not a real model), and `crates/ai/src/tests.rs::passages_are_read_from_a_wrapped_reply_and_junk_is_dropped`, `passages_ignore_brackets_around_the_list_and_fold_newlines_and_tabs` and `document_context_reports_what_was_really_included` (not shown failing with the change reverted). - 2026-11-16 (M2, PDF 2.0 document parts listing, headless, gap 22): `dpart_list` is a new read-only tool that lists the document part tree under the catalog's `/DPartRoot` (ISO 32000-2 14.12) in document order: for each node its object number, the listed parent, depth, `/Start` and `/End` as 1-based pages when they name a page of the document, the number of references in `/DParts` and the `/DPM` entries as short text (at most 32 entries of 200 characters; nested values read as `(array)` or `(dictionary)`); `has_root`, `record_level` (the root's `/RecordLevel`), `skipped` and `truncated` report what was not read (`truncated` also covers pages past 100,000; an absent `/End` reads as `end_page` null, which means not given, the part ending on its start page). Backed by `pdfcraft_organize::list_document_parts` and `Document::document_parts`. Bounds: 5,000 nodes, 64 levels, 5,000 references per node, 100,000 pages mapped to numbers; each node is read once so a cycle or a node listed twice ends (counted in `skipped`); only indirect references are followed to nodes. Nothing is changed or written, the tree is not validated against the specification, and no tool or UI creates or edits parts. - 2026-11-16 (M8, partial redaction of a match, headless, gap 17): `redact_mark` takes `keep_first` and `keep_last` (integers 0 to 64, default 0) with `pattern` or `regex`: that many letters or digits at the start or end of each match stay readable and the mark covers the rest, so a card number can keep its last four digits; refused with a message beside find, words, rect or whole_pages, and for values outside 0 to 64 or not integers. A separator that would begin or end the covered part is left out of the box; a glyph holding several characters (a ligature) is kept readable only when all of it fits within the limit; a match with no more letters or digits than the kept ends is not marked and counted in `left_readable`, and the call fails when that leaves nothing to mark. The work is one pass over each match's glyphs (ends only), nothing is allocated per page beyond a short string per glyph looked at. The kept characters stay in the saved file's text, which is the point; the Search and Redact window has no control for it. Parity `protect.redact-partial-words` is now partial (UI, find and words, and a check against Acrobat not done); the gap 17 "Missing" bullet was reworded and a revision-history row added. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-15 (M12, Edit all JavaScripts, headless): `doc_javascripts` lists every JavaScript in a document in one place (named document scripts, the open action, Will Close/Will Save/Did Save/Will Print/Did Print, field, link, annotation, page and bookmark actions, with their `/Next` chains), each with an id, kind, trigger, where it lives and its code, and `doc_javascript_edit` replaces or removes one by id as one undoable edit that keeps everything else and runs nothing; each edit carries the script's fingerprint, so a stale id cannot overwrite another script, and removing a name repairs the name tree. Bounded at 5000 scripts, 1 MiB per script and 16 MiB of code in all, and safe against cyclic chains, trees and outlines. There is no editor window yet and XFA scripts are not listed. Checked with engine, forms and automation tests (including cyclic, huge, non-text and code-less scripts); the app was not run. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-15 (M10, Print with a comments summary): Print has a new Include a summary of the comments checkbox (off by default, and off again each time the dialog opens), and `doc_print` a `comment_summary` option with `comment_summary_sort` (page, author, date or type). The pages Summarize Comments writes (its Comments only layout, the same generator) for the comments on the printed pages only, so the page range, odd or even and reverse decide which, follow the document's sheets, one summary page per sheet on the same paper, turned the way most of the sheets are (the first sheet when evenly split) and fitted to it, with any of Fit, Actual size, Shrink, Custom or Multiple; the tool's `sheets` counts them and `comment_summary` gives their pages and the comments covered. Printed pages without comments print as before and say that no summary was added (a notice once the print runs or the file is written; `comment_summary.skipped`). Refused up front, before the Will Print actions run and whether or not there are comments: Booklet, Poster, Label, a selected area, Print as image and colour-managed prints (pictures: the summary stays text and is not rasterised), a document that forbids copying, and one that allows only low-resolution printing; in the dialog the box is greyed with the reason and unticked. In a browser the option is unavailable, because the browser print path is handed the document's sheets only (Save as PDF there still works). Not yet: the summary in the dialog's preview, a sort choice in the dialog, summary before the document or placed with it, connector lines. Parity entry `print.print-with-comment-summary` is partial; gap 18 updated. The new strings are English-only until added to craft-i18n. Tested in the engine (landscape, N-up, reversed, refusals), end to end through the tool (page count grows by the summary pages, the comment's text is found on them, ranges, subset, reverse, refusals, no Will Print action on a refusal) and in window tests of the checkbox (summary pages added, the notice, greyed in a booklet and for a copy-restricted document, off each job); no job was sent to a real printer and the browser build was not run. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-15 (M6, Forms Auto-Complete): Preferences ▸ Forms has Auto-Complete: Off (the default) or Basic, and a Remove all remembered entries button. With Basic on, text typed into a plain text field is remembered when the field is left, and typing in another text field offers the remembered entries that begin with what is typed (ignoring case) in a small list under the field: Down and Up move through it, Enter or Tab takes the highlighted one, Escape hides it, a click takes one. Never remembered: password, comb, file-select, multi-line, rich-text, read-only, signature, formatted (date, number, time, special), calculated or validated fields, or text that is empty, over 200 characters or has a control character; at most 200 entries are kept, most recent first, without repeats. The desktop app keeps them in its local settings and a corrupt or oversized saved list gives a shorter list, never an error; the control channel gains `ui.set form-autocomplete=off|basic`. Headless: `form_preferences` gains `auto_complete`, `suggest`, `list_entries`, `forget_entry` and `clear_entries`, and `form_fill` remembers what it fills while Basic is on (a headless session keeps them for the session only, not between runs). Missing: Acrobat's Advanced level, remembering a value loaded from the file rather than typed, and matching beyond a prefix. Parity entry `form.auto-complete` is partial and `misc.prefs-forms` notes it. The new strings (Auto-Complete, Basic, Remove all remembered entries and one hint) are English-only until added to craft-i18n. Tested with unit tests (entries, matching), a tool end-to-end test and kittest tests (off against on, the Forms page, saved settings); the popup's look was not checked in a screenshot. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-14 (M3, Single-key tool accelerators): Preferences has a new opt-in Use single-key accelerators to access tools (off by default, remembered). With it on and nothing holding the keyboard (a text field, dialog, palette or menu), an unmodified Z picks Marquee zoom and G picks Snapshot; V (Select) and H (Hand) already worked and stay on either way. Pressing the active tool's key changes nothing. The control channel reports and sets it (`ui.state` and `ui.set single_key_accelerators`); there is no automation tool, since the headless tools have no pointer tools. Not done: letters for comment and edit tools, and the letter shown in menus and tooltips. Parity entry `view.single-key-accelerators` is partial; gap 16 and the UI parity table updated. The new string is English-only until added to craft-i18n. Tested in kittest (the key on and off, typing into a text field, the preference kept and set over the control channel); each test was shown failing with its change reverted. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-14 (M12, Compare kinds of change): Compare files can be limited to some kinds of change: `replaced`, `inserted`, `deleted` and `visual`, the kinds the comparison actually tells apart (it reads words and pixels, so images, annotations, formatting and background or header/footer changes are not kinds of their own and all count as visual; the dialog, the report and the tool descriptions say so). The Compare Files dialog has a Kinds of change row of checkboxes (Compare is disabled with none ticked); the Compare panel's Show row is the same four checkboxes in place of Text and Visual, and Mark as comments and the report now follow them instead of always covering every change (a report of a filter names the kinds shown, how many changes it hid and the number of visual differences, which are never listed or marked). `doc_compare`, `doc_compare_report` and `doc_compare_mark` take `kinds` (case and spaces ignored; an empty list, a non-string or an unknown name is refused and the error lists replaced, inserted, deleted, visual); `doc_compare` filters its list, counts and visual regions, returns `kinds` and `hidden` (the counts the filter left out) and keeps `identical` about the documents. Engine: `ChangeKinds`, `compare_report_in_kinds`, `mark_differences_in_kinds`. Tested headlessly (engine, automation and kittest dialog tests); not looked at in a screenshot. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-14 (M8/M9, Redact codes and Save dates): `redact_mark` silently dropped `codes` when a literal `overlay` was given without a `code_set`: the codes were never checked and the marks carried only the overlay. It now refuses that call ("codes needs code_set or custom_code_set"), like `codes` with no overlay at all; `overlay` with `code_set` and `codes` was already refused. Save (`save_bytes` and `save_full_bytes`) stamped a new Info `/ModDate` but left the XMP `xmp:ModifyDate` at its earlier value, so a PDF/A file's two modification dates disagreed (ISO 19005 needs them equal). Both now come from the one timestamp of that save: an existing `ModifyDate` of the XMP namespace (found by namespace, so `xap:` counts; element, empty element or attribute form) is rewritten, a PDF/A packet without one gets it once, and an XMP stream that is not plain (a Crypt filter) or a non-PDF/A packet without a ModifyDate is left alone; other Info edits (Title and so on) are still not mirrored into the XMP. Tested headlessly (engine, preflight and automation tests); each new test was shown failing with its fix reverted. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-14 (M14, Preferences: Identity and Forms): Two new Preferences cards, each wired only to behaviour that exists. Identity holds Name (the existing name on new comments, moved here from Comment; it is now also the default author of stamps, measurements and redaction marks headlessly and the text a typed signature or its initials starts from) and Email, which is written as the contact information (`/ContactInfo`) of digital signatures made in the Sign dialog. The headless tools keep their own name and email in the engine session (`Session::set_identity`, set with the `identity` tool, not kept between runs; the desktop app reads its own settings and does not share them with it): comment, stamp, measurement and redaction tools take the name when no `author` is given (PdfCraft when unset), `fill_sign_add` signature and initials take it when `text` is omitted, and `sign_document` takes the email when `contact` is omitted. The new `identity` tool reads and sets both. Forms holds Highlight fields (the same setting as the form bar, moved from Appearance), a Highlight colour for the fillable-field overlay (required fields keep their red border) and Show a field's tooltip, which turns off the `/TU` tooltip on hover. All are remembered (hostile values trimmed, cut or refused), and the control channel gains `ui.set identity-email`, `form-tooltips` and `field-highlight-color`. The new `form_preferences` tool reads and sets Highlight fields (`app.runtimeHighlight`); the colour and tooltips are viewer-only and have no headless effect. Not added because nothing would read them: Acrobat's login name, title and organization (its identity object for scripts, which PdfCraft's script runtime lacks) and Forms auto-complete (nothing remembers field entries). Parity entries `misc.prefs-identity` and `misc.prefs-forms` are now partial; gap 19 updated. The new strings (Identity, Email, Forms, Highlight colour, Default, Show a field's tooltip…, and two hints) are English-only until added to craft-i18n; a missing translation falls back to English. Tested headlessly (automation and kittest tests); the colour and tooltip effects were not looked at in a screenshot. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-13 (M12, Compare Files page ranges): The Compare Files dialog's single Pages field (which only filtered the new file's changes after comparing everything) is now Old file pages and New file pages: one page or one range such as 2-5 for each file, empty for the whole file, with errors that name the file and its page count ("New file: pages must be within 1-4"). The comparison, the Compare panel, Mark as comments and the Report use the same ranges, compare only the words on those pages, and pair the visual differences of the two ranges by position, shading each half on its own page; changes and the report name each file's own pages, and the report names the ranges. `doc_compare_report` and `doc_compare_mark` take `doc_from`/`doc_to`/`other_from`/`other_to` like `doc_compare`. A range is contiguous: the old Pages field also took a list such as `1,3`, which is now refused with a message saying what is allowed. A range in one field only applies to the same pages of the other file (an error naming that file and its page count if it lacks them), and the fields start empty for another pair of files. The new dialog strings are English-only until they are added to craft-i18n. Tested headlessly (engine, automation and kittest dialog tests); not looked at in a screenshot. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-13 (M10, Office documents in Insert pages, Combine files and Create ▸ Multiple files): Word, Excel, PowerPoint, OpenDocument and RTF files now go through the same LibreOffice conversion as File ▸ Open (private temporary folder, macros off, linked content not updated, 120 s and size limits) in Insert pages (the picker, a drop on the page grid, Insert from the clipboard, `page_insert_image`), Combine files (picked, dropped or from a folder; `doc_combine` already took them) and Create ▸ Multiple files (combined or separate). In the app the conversion runs on a worker thread and the flow carries on when it finishes; a batch is all or nothing about LibreOffice being installed (nothing is added, and the message says to install it from libreoffice.org or set PDFCRAFT_SOFFICE), a document that fails to convert is named and left out, and the pickers offer an Office documents extension list. A file's kind is still decided by its bytes, so a PDF named .docx is a PDF. The lookup is injectable (`Session::with_office_locator`, `Automation::with_office_locator`), so the tests run a stand-in `soffice` script on Unix; a real LibreOffice conversion was not run here, and the real-conversion tests still skip where it isn't installed. Still not done: a Create ▸ From Office command, conversion settings, progress and cancel for large batches, the web build (Office files are still refused there, without dropping the PDFs and images dropped with them). Re-estimated: gap 8's remaining first-beta Office item went from 5–10 h to 3–6 h; no other estimate changed.
- 2026-11-13 (M14, Portable app as a default PDF app on Windows): The portable zip registered nothing, so Windows did not list PdfCraft under Settings > Default apps. `pdfcraft.exe --register-file-types` / `--unregister-file-types` and Preferences > Set up PdfCraft as a PDF app… (`help.default_app`, `help.default_app.remove`) now write, or remove, the MSI's Default Programs keys for the current user only (HKCU via the system's reg.exe, no new dependency, no administrator; portable names so an MSI installed for one user is left alone), pointing at the running exe, and the command then opens Settings > Default apps. It never sets the default or touches UserChoice: the user picks PdfCraft for .pdf there. A Stop offering… command and button undo it (choose another .pdf app in Settings first). The registry plan, its guards and the non-Windows error are tested on every OS; the Windows executor was only compiled and linted for Windows, not run, so the parity entry is partial. There is no automation tool yet; the CLI flags are the headless path. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-13 (M7, Find & Replace ragged-right reflow): Replace's reflow now also recognises ragged-right paragraphs whose rows were broken greedily, as word processors do, as wraps when the first word of the next row would not have fitted on the row above (needs two such breaks apart from the widest row's, none that fits, a shared left edge, so centred text, table-of-contents and other leader-dot, gapped or numbered entries, address blocks, poems, lists, code, table columns and short blocks keep their rows). Tested on synthetic Helvetica text only. Not recognised: non-greedy composers (InDesign's paragraph composer, TeX) may keep rows and leave the orphan; justified/ragged mixtures, tables and columns; Edit text's own widening is unchanged. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-13 (M7, Find & Replace reflow): When a longer replacement adds rows, Replace now also tries the paragraph as one flow, rejoining rows recognised as wraps (marked by PdfCraft, or at least two full-measure rows) so no orphan row is left mid-paragraph; ragged-right paragraphs from other producers, short-line blocks, hyphen-ended rows and turned text keep their rows, and CJK/Thai wraps get no inserted space. Replace reports paragraphs that gained rows and may overlap what follows (notice, text_replace grown/warnings, LineEdit rows). Moving other content is still not done. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-04 (M6, PR #5699 group/cache/MK/button review): Expand calculation groups/duplicates with streaming bounded lookup; retain one canonical Field object per exposed identity and prune stale roots. Protect outside shared-MK owners and carry exact button IDs through native clicks and js_run, with regression and persistence coverage.
- 2026-11-04 (M6, PR #5699 early reset/duplicate identities): Enforce the shared 64-call reset budget before argument conversion/scanning, with sticky atomic rejection and Open/event budget sharing. Preserve PDF object identity in Calculate order, live targets and value writeback; regress duplicate names, reversed order, native completion and persistence.
- 2026-11-04 (M6, PR #5699 independent review): Preserve unknown defaults through the JS reset shadow; bound menu/response text before copies. Cover native/console reset persistence, inherited defaults, decode-budget exhaustion and exact Unicode dialog boundaries.
- 2026-11-04 (M6, PR #5699 memory/widget bounds): Count reset widgets and bound escaped UTF-8 text conversion before copies; add negative and Unicode boundary regressions.
- 2026-11-04 (M6, PR #5699 reset estimate): Charge expanded-name scans and field-name copy/comparison bytes; add three negative regressions and byte-exact refusal checks.

> **2026-11-04 (M6, PR #5699 reset budget):** Reject reset amplification before request copies, preview and PDF mutation; cap reset/selector counts and aggregate estimated work. Add native completion, boundary, flood and large-form regressions; preserve ordered under-budget resets.
> **2026-11-04 (M6, PR #5699 dependent reset):** Reuse form reset on an isolated comparison document so unnamed built-in calculated text/combo fields are included; retain normal post-write calculation. Extend persisted-default and typed-response regressions.

> **2026-11-04 (M6, PR #5699 review):** Bound worker capacity and synchronous deadlines, isolate expired/pending realms before edits, keep script prompts above other dialogs, and cover block ASI, control-position regex literals, event routing and complete undo state. Validation is reported in the PR; parity remains partial.
> **2026-11-03 (M6, PR #5699 follow-up):** Accept independent top-level statements separated by automatic semicolon insertion while retaining continuation-chain limits; assert cyclic calculation results and preserve upstream multiline Fill & Sign coverage.
> **2026-11-02 (M6, PR #5699 second review):** Count active expression chains instead of whole-source tokens; align native button events and automation schemas, block document input under script prompts, and validate prompt dismissal and idle repaint scheduling.
> **2026-11-02 (M6, PR #5699 review):** Cache per-edit script geometry/calculation plans, expose live document metadata, and bound persistent worker stacks to 64 MiB; add operation-count, lifecycle and isolated hostile-input/address-space regressions.
> **2026-11-02 (M6, #5211/#269):** Integrate persistent native AcroForm realms, synchronous calculation, page boxes, appearance/geometry/icon writeback and native button prompt continuation. Synthetic workflow and cancellation tests added; validation details accompany the PR. Remaining JavaScript parity stays partial.

PdfCraft's one-page summary: how close it is to Adobe Acrobat Pro's offline feature set, and what
comes next. Adobe's cloud services (Document Cloud, Acrobat Sign requests, shared review, the AI
Assistant) are out of scope; PdfCraft's alternatives are local-first plus opt-in providers.

| Document | Holds |
|---|---|
| [docs/target-app-parity.md](docs/target-app-parity.md) | The authoritative assessment: method, weights, every dimension and area, stage, calibration |
| [docs/gaps.md](docs/gaps.md) | Every known shortfall, ranked: the work list |
| [docs/roadmap.md](docs/roadmap.md) | Milestones M0–M14, current focus, upcoming milestones, risks |
| [docs/pdf-spec-parity.md](docs/pdf-spec-parity.md) | ISO 32000-2 coverage, annotations, actions, signatures, PDF/A/X/UA |
| [docs/file-format-parity.md](docs/file-format-parity.md) | Every format Acrobat reads or writes, create/export conversions, OCR |
| [docs/ui-parity.md](docs/ui-parity.md) | Tools, handles, shortcuts, panels, preferences |
| [docs/hardware-parity.md](docs/hardware-parity.md) | GPU, printers, scanners, smart cards, pen, platforms |
| [docs/localization-parity.md](docs/localization-parity.md) | Per-language status |
| [docs/architecture.md](docs/architecture.md) | How PdfCraft is built |
| `parity/acrobat-features.toml` | The machine-checked checklist (`cargo xtask parity`) |

**Keep it current:** every session, update the milestone row in [docs/roadmap.md](docs/roadmap.md)
and add a line to the [progress log](#progress-log); when a gap closes, remove it from
[docs/gaps.md](docs/gaps.md); re-measure fully when a major arc lands, at least monthly.

## Headline numbers

| | Value | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| **Feature breadth** | **≈ 67%** tier-weighted (57.0% unweighted: 423 shipped + 75 partial of 808; P0 94.2%) | measured, `parity/acrobat-features.toml` |
| Feature breadth, mainstream practitioner | ≈ 70% tier-weighted (63.3% unweighted; P0 95.3%) | measured ([split](docs/target-app-parity.md#mainstream-practitioner-vs-full-acrobat-pro)) |
| **Ready for real work**, full Acrobat Pro | **≈ 50%** (45–53%) | estimated, weighted sum over [dimensions](#by-dimension) |
| **Ready for real work**, mainstream practitioner | **≈ 55%** (50–60%) | estimated ([split](docs/target-app-parity.md#mainstream-practitioner-vs-full-acrobat-pro)) |
| **Ready for real work**, essentials user | **≈ 66%** (60–70%) | estimated ([method](docs/target-app-parity.md#essentials-user)) |
| Remaining to **beta** | ≈ 400–700 Opus 5.5 agent-hours | estimated |
| Remaining to **full parity** | ≈ 780–1,400 Opus 5.5 agent-hours (≈ 300–550 h wall clock with 3–5 agents) | estimated, calibrated on this repo's history |

**Why alpha:** all six of Acrobat Pro's everyday workflows can be completed end to end on macOS
(the [alpha gate](docs/roadmap.md#alpha-gate)), and users praise them (7.6k stars, ≈ 217k downloads
of 0.4–0.5, switching reports such as #179). Two are only partial: editing (no CJK/RTL) and convert
and share (no in-app Office→PDF; Word export loses layout, #773; password-protected files open in
Acrobat since #836, #774). Those, unmeasured quality against Acrobat, the borrowed `hayro` renderer,
and 245 open issues keep it far from beta.

### Readiness by audience

| Audience | Ready | Opus 5.5 agent-hours to ≈ 95% | Work that dominates |
|---|---|---|---|
| Full Acrobat Pro (ready for real work) | ≈ 50% | ≈ 780–1,400 h (≈ 300–550 h wall clock with 3–5 agents; 60–70% parallelizes) | Own renderer and fidelity harness, editing existing content, OCR and Office conversion, Preflight and PDF/A/X/UA, accessibility tagging, signatures for institutions |
| Mainstream practitioner | ≈ 55% | ≈ 500–900 h (≈ 200–400 h wall clock; ≈ 60% parallelizes) | Rendering fidelity, editing existing text (CJK, RTL, subsets), conversion (Word layout, Excel, PowerPoint, Office→PDF), OCR languages, the bug backlog |
| Essentials user | ≈ 66% | ≈ 180–330 h (≈ 90–180 h wall clock; ≈ 50% parallelizes) | Rendering fidelity on everyday files, launch and GPU stability, simple text fixes, Word export layout, Chinese UI glyphs |

Hours are calibrated as in [Effort and calibration](docs/target-app-parity.md#effort-and-calibration) (≈ 1k kept lines per agent-hour for new features, 3–10× slower for hardening and fidelity). The audiences are nested subsets, so essentials ≤ mainstream ≤ full.

## Languages

Twelve key languages: the share of the 2,897 strings in the i18n report's denominator (the interface's strings plus every catalog key), from
`cargo xtask i18n-report` on 2026-11-03. Detail and script notes: [localization-parity.md](docs/localization-parity.md).

| Language | Code | Strings | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| English | `en` | 100% | full |
| Simplified Chinese | `zh-hans` | 88.4% | partial (native LXGW WenKai Lite; no Hans face in the web build, #826) |
| Spanish | `es` | 98.4% | partial (dialogs / tooltips; no help) |
| Hindi | `hi` | 97.5% | partial (Devanagari rendering unqualified) |
| Arabic | `ar` | 97.9% | partial (logical catalog, explicit RTL labels, mirrored stock chrome; native/browser/IME workflows unqualified) |
| French | `fr` | 98.4% | partial |
| Portuguese (Brazil) | `pt-br` | 98.4% | partial |
| Indonesian | `id` | 97.5% | partial |
| Japanese | `ja` | 89.6% | partial |
| German | `de` | 89.7% | partial |
| Korean | `ko` | 97.5% | partial (no Hangul UI face) |
| Vietnamese | `vi` | 98.4% | partial (dialogs / tooltips; no help) |

Also shipped: Traditional Chinese, Russian, Bulgarian, Telugu, Hungarian, Ukrainian, Italian,
Kazakh, Polish, Slovenian, Greek, Turkish, Lithuanian, Finnish, Albanian, Catalan, Romanian,
Norwegian Bokmål, Persian, Central Kurdish, Thai, Lao and Czech: **23 more catalogs**
(35 interface languages including English).

## Progress log
- **2026-11-13 (M7, Edit text names a stand-in font):** Retyping a word in a paragraph whose subset font lacks a letter of it never failed in Edit text (it draws the letters (or the paragraph) in the standard face of its family, as Find and replace now can on request), but it did so silently. The edit now says which letters forced it and which font drew them: a notice after the edit in the interface, and `warnings`, `substituted_fonts` and `missing_letters` in `text_edit`'s result, with Undo restoring the paragraph. `text_edit` gains `substitute_fonts` (paragraphs): false refuses the edit whole, naming the letters, instead of redrawing. A complete face of the same family elsewhere on the page now also supplies the missing letters for a single-run paragraph, and choosing a subset font for a letter it lacks names the letter, the font and the way out. Synthetic regressions at the edit, engine and tool levels. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-12 (M7, Replace in a substitute font):** Find and replace no longer has to skip a paragraph whose subset font lacks a letter of the new text: the Find options menu gains "Use a substitute font if the font lacks letters" (and `text_replace` gains `substitute_fonts`), both off by default, which rewrites the whole paragraph (not only the match) in the substitute font Edit text already falls back to, counts the match as replaced and reports how many paragraphs were rewritten that way and which fonts stepped in, at least (engine `Replacement::substituted` / `substituted_paragraphs` / `substitute_fonts`; the tool's `substitute_font_names`). Without the option the match is still skipped as missing letters, and the notice now says the option exists. Engine, tool and Find-options tests added. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-12 (M10, Insert pages from the clipboard):** Edit ▸ Pages ▸ Insert pages from clipboard (also Organize ▸ Insert ▸ Insert pages from the clipboard, the Pages panel's page tools and the command palette as `page.insert_clipboard`) inserts a picture on the system clipboard, or the PDFs, images and text files copied in a file manager, after the selection in order as one undo step with the new pages selected; what can't be used is named with its reason and left out, at most 100 files and 512 MB together, and a clipboard with nothing usable says what is allowed. Engine `Session::plan_insert` builds the single batch edit (every renamed form field is noted); `page_insert_image` inserts PDFs, pictures and text files headlessly. Tests cover the engine, the tool and the window. Desktop only. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-12 (M12, Search all PDFs in a folder):** Edit > Advanced search gains a Search in: This document / Folder scope. Choose folder (and Include sub-folders), then Search or Enter reads the `.pdf` files of the folder on a worker thread with the new engine `folder_search` (each page's text, searched with the find bar's matcher, so Case-sensitive and Whole words agree), lists the matches by file with page and context, and a double-click opens the file at that page; Stop cancels between pages. The `text_find_folder` tool does the same headlessly (`folder`, `query`, `case_sensitive`, `whole_words`, `recursive`, `max_files` up to 2000, `limit` up to 5000): the folder must be inside `--root`, links are never followed, a corrupt, password-protected, oversized or linked file is listed in `skipped` with its reason and never fails the call, and `files_capped`, `results_capped` and `complete` say when a cap cut the answer. Counts of failed and cut pages (also in files with no match), a 4 GiB read cap, unlistable sub-folders reported apart from skipped files, typed skip reasons worded in the interface language, a search that survives closing the find bar, and a Stop state with an incomplete hint were added after review. Tests cover two-file matches with pages and snippets, options, no match, a corrupt and a password-protected file, caps, root confinement and argument checks, cancellation, and the window end to end. Text layer only (no OCR), no index, one file after another. `view.search-folder` is now shipped. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-12 (M14, Preferences: Units and paper, comment tools):** A new Units and paper card and two new settings in the Comment card, each wired to behaviour that already existed. Units and paper holds the unit page sizes and margins are shown in (moved here from Documents and view) and a new choice of US Letter or A4 for Create ▸ Blank page, which was fixed to the system region's paper and is now remembered (`blank_paper`; a missing, mistyped or corrupt value keeps the current paper). The Comment card holds Keep the selected comment tool selected after use (the same persisted setting as the toolbar pin) and Reset comment tool colours and sizes, which forgets the colours, opacities, widths and font sizes saved with Make Current Properties Default. The opt-in control channel gains `ui.set units=in|mm|cm|pt`, `blank-paper=letter|a4` and `reset-comment-defaults=true`, and `ui.state` reports `units` and `blank_paper`. Parity entries `misc.prefs-units-guides` and `misc.prefs-commenting` are now partial; gap 19 and ui-parity updated. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-12 (M14, Move a tab to a new window):** Dragging a document tab more than 32 points out of the window, or Move to new window in a tab's right-click menu, opens its document in a second PdfCraft process (`--new-window --page N`, which does not bring last session back) at the page shown and closes the tab here. The document needs a saved file that is still there, no unsaved changes and no other tab of it; otherwise a notification says why and the tab stays, as it does when the launch fails. Not added to Reopen Closed Tab. Pure window management, so no automation tool. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-12 (M8, Custom redaction codes):** `redact_mark` takes `custom_code_set` (`id`, `name`, `codes`) beside `code_set` and `codes`; the set is checked (id of `a-z0-9-` that is not a built-in id, 1 to 256 distinct codes the overlay can draw, without control or invisible characters or edge spaces, bounded size) with errors naming the field, and its picked codes become the overlay text in the set's order. Tests cover the validation limits and the tool end to end (mark, apply, text). `protect.redaction-codes` stays partial: the Edit Codes editor, choosing a custom set in the dialog, saving sets and import/export are still missing. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-12 (M13, batch Save as PDF/A):** `doc_batch` gains a `pdfa` step (last in the list, optional `level` 2b or 3b, default 2b) that runs the same conversion as `pdfa_convert` on each file after the other steps, saves the result and reports `bytes_before`, `bytes_after`, `remaining_issues` (a count of what verification still finds, such as fonts that are not embedded), `compliant` (true when that count is 0) and the conversion's warnings. A file with remaining issues is still written and counted completed (`pdfa_verify` on the output gives the details); a file that cannot be converted, such as an encrypted one without the owner's rights, is a per-file error and the batch continues. An unknown level is refused before anything is written. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-11 (M4, Remove white margins):** `page_remove_white_margins` (engine `Session::remove_white_margins`) crops each page, or the pages chosen, to the bounds of what it paints: a raster of the page at up to 144 dpi (never past 2000 pixels a side), where a pixel is content unless it is transparent or every colour channel is 250 or more, widened by an optional `padding` in points (default 0) and kept inside the old crop box, rounded outward to 0.01 pt so it never cuts content (judged as the file sets it: layers at the file's defaults, comments shown); `/Rotate` and `/UserUnit` are followed. The new boxes go in through the ordinary crop edit (`SetPageBox`) as one undoable step. A blank page is left as it is and reported in `blank`, a page that already fills its crop box in `unchanged`. Headless only: no menu command or dialog yet, as the render runs on the calling thread and the app needs a background job first. `organize.remove-white-margins` partial. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-13 (M4, Remove white margins in the app):** Pages ▸ Remove white margins… (palette and menu, `page.remove_white_margins`) opens a small dialog: the pages (all, the current page or a range, checked like the other page-range dialogs) and the padding to keep around the content in the unit chosen in Preferences (default 0, at most 1000 pt). The measuring runs on a background worker, one page raster at a time, with a progress card, Cancel between pages and an unfrozen window; the engine's job is split into `Session::white_margins_job`, `WhiteMarginsJob::run` and `Session::apply_white_margins` (the headless `remove_white_margins` is those three in a row). The result goes in through the ordinary crop edit as one undoable step and a toast says how many pages were cropped, were blank and left alone, or were already tight; it is refused, changing nothing, if the document was edited or another one brought to the front while the worker ran. `organize.remove-white-margins` stays partial (no odd/even or per-side options; not yet in the Organize panel list). Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-11 (M12, Compare page ranges):** `doc_compare` takes an optional page range for each document (`other_from`/`other_to` for the older, `doc_from`/`doc_to` for the newer; a missing end is the first or last page) and compares only the words on those pages (engine `Session::compare_in`, `PageRange`). Changes keep each document's own page numbers; a range below 1, reversed or past the last page is refused (an invalid argument naming it), not clamped; with `visual` the two ranges' pages are paired by position. The Compare Files dialog is not wired to it yet (its existing Pages field still filters the new file's changes after the comparison), and `doc_compare_report` and `doc_compare_mark` take no ranges. `misc.compare-page-ranges` partial. Overall estimate unchanged.
- **2026-11-11 (M7, Remove Bates numbering):** Bates numbers PdfCraft adds are now drawn in a stream of their own (`/Artifact` `/Subtype /BatesN`, `/PCMark /Bates`), so Edit ▸ Bates numbering ▸ Remove Bates numbering (`edit.bates_remove`, `doc_bates_remove`) takes them off every page and leaves ordinary headers, footers and page content alone (a page shrink added with only Bates numbers goes too); undoable, and saved and reopened files keep working. On a one-line box with one Bates token only the token goes; other boxes with a token go whole. Update header & footer replaces Bates numbers when its new text has a Bates token and otherwise leaves them alone; Remove header & footer still takes them too. Bates numbers added before this change or by other tools aren't recognised. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-16 (M5, #3741):** Ctrl+M opens Comments on Windows/Linux and repeated activation keeps it open. macOS leaves Command+M unbound for Minimize. Dispatch, menus, palette, shortcut help and control/headless metadata resolve the platform-specific registry binding. Mapping and real-input regressions are retained through the rebase; estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-03 (M3, #3669):** A drag across a table selects its rows, as Preview does, instead of reading order's detour down each column; a cell padded with spaces reads with the next cell of its row. Synthetic render and UI drag regressions; estimates unchanged.

- **2026-11-09 (M10, #5130):** Windows Printer default sends native two-sided bindings only when automatic duplex support is confirmed, preserving manual driver defaults (#5130/#5772); physical output remains unqualified. Both Windows submission paths preserve DLL guards and current spooler fixes. Review follow-up validates both duplex scopes through craft-print 9fe50c94 and corrects CI formatting. Estimates unchanged.

- **2026-10-27 (M10, #5005):** Vector printing retains each annotation's optional-content membership when drawing its appearance.
  Shared appearances and existing layer state remain independent; estimates unchanged.

- **2026-10-29 (M10, #5029):** Vector printing also honours the implicit NoRotate behavior of existing Text appearances when the flag is clear.
  Print eligibility, source dictionaries and shared appearance streams remain unchanged.

- **2026-10-27 (M10, #5029):** Vector printing retains the orientation and upper-left anchor of existing NoRotate annotation appearances on rotated source pages.
  Crop origins, UserUnit and later sheet transforms remain effective; estimates unchanged.

- **2026-10-27 (M10, annotation print precision):** Vector printing preserves small placement coefficients for existing form, comment and stamp appearances with large bounding boxes or matrices.
  Synthetic render and save/reopen regressions cover the change; estimates unchanged.

- **2026-10-31 (M6, PR #4166/#4088 integration):** Integrated main f137a289 (0.16.1), preserving upstream progress entries and automatic-field Print suppression with its console diagnostic. ShowPanel/SaveAs filtering, explicit button/console printing, field effects and document-error provenance remain intact, together with all three engine regressions and the headless automation regression. Manifests, lockfile and CI match incoming main. Windows x64 / Rust 1.95 validation: 761 engine/automation tests passed, zero failed and four existing ignored; formatting, scoped all-target Clippy with -D warnings, layers/assets/parity and the engine/automation WebAssembly check passed. A fresh CLI build and the 13-step synthetic form exercise passed; the inspected render shows Qty=4 and Total=8, and explicit button/console Print requests are retained. Main's 100 headless parity notices, five renderer WebAssembly warnings and three optional egui patch notices remain. Estimates unchanged.

- **2026-10-29 (M6, PR #4166/#4088 integration):** Retained automatic-field Print suppression, ShowPanel/SaveAs filtering, explicit button/console printing and document-error provenance while integrating upstream 0.15. All three engine regressions and the headless regression remain in their current test modules, with matching parity evidence. Dependency manifests retain main's integrated craft-libs revision, which includes #366. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-10-27 (M10, annotation print precision):** Vector printing preserves small placement coefficients for existing form, comment and stamp appearances with large bounding boxes or matrices.
  Synthetic render and save/reopen regressions cover the change; estimates unchanged.

- **2026-10-13 (M8, redact by regex):** `redact_mark` takes `regex` (caller expression, linear-time engine, length/size/nesting caps, empty-matching refused) with optional `validate` (luhn, mod97, iban). Headless only; issue #1886.

- **2026-11-08 (M5, comment exchange):** Comments keep their formatting and appearance through XFDF and FDF. XFDF export and import carry rich text, default style, border width, style and dash pattern, rotation, justification, fringe, line leaders and captions, the caret symbol and redaction overlay text as XFDF 3.0 (ISO 19444-1) names them, and a stamp's appearance as `<appearance>` (the DICT/STREAM/DATA encoding, bounded); copy and paste carries every comment's appearance. FDF export keeps a stamp's /AP with its forms and images, as XFDF does, and FDF import keeps any /AP the file carries (ISO 32000-2 §12.7.8.3.4). The intent goes out as both `intent` and `IT`. An unreadable XFDF appearance is left out, said in the import report and the repair log, and the comment is drawn by PdfCraft; an FDF past the import budget is refused whole. A redaction mark is always drawn by PdfCraft.

- **2026-11-07 (M2, #6449):** A non-embedded Times New Roman set as a TrueType CID font (`TimesNewRoman,Bold`, `TimesNewRomanPSMT` and the other faces) draws the glyphs its codes name, as the non-embedded Arial does: its first 258 glyphs follow the same standard Macintosh order, which the embedded Times New Roman subsets of a public French form confirm in 941 of 942 `/ToUnicode` entries (vendored hayro-interpret patch 88). That form drew "Salarié(e) du régime général" as "Salari²*g+f w't²i lo g'ì²p²tcn" and "changement" as "ej angement". Times and Times New Roman MT Std are not read this way.

- **2026-11-05 (M2, #5889):** A non-embedded Arial set as a TrueType CID font (`CIDFontType2`, `Identity-H`, as Word and PDFMaker write it) draws the glyphs its codes name. Those codes are glyph indexes in the author's Arial, whose first 258 glyphs follow the standard Macintosh order, so the standard substitute now draws each glyph by name (vendored hayro-interpret patch 76). Before, it took each code as one of its own glyph indexes, and "Low Power" drew as "@ck 'Dck Yf". Arial Narrow, Black and Unicode MS, and `CIDFontType0` fonts, are not read this way. Copying or searching those runs still needs a `/ToUnicode`.

- **2026-11-07 (M7, #2964 / #3185):** Supported Form text can be prepared for direct editing; groups left grouped (unsafe compositing, text nested past eight levels) are reported. Legacy Thai keeps the original font programs, Regular/Bold and text settings, also across same-face subsets; the narrow THSarabunPSK map repair is checked glyph by glyph against the font program; cross-family replacement is explicit. Builds on #3067 and #2811. Unsafe groups and mixed contextual styles remain partial; estimates unchanged.

- **2026-10-28 (M10, #4119 partial):** Scan & OCR ▸ Enhance scanned file cleans scanned pages in place: background removal (level 0–100, as the camera clean-up), edge shadow removal (book gutters, copier borders) and despeckle. Each scan image is decoded, cleaned and written back into its own object at the same size and colour space (JPEG stays JPEG, others lossless, 1-bit stays 1-bit), so the OCR text layer, comments and links stay; one undo step, shared images once, unsupported images (CMYK, indexed, JPEG 2000, JBIG2, CCITT, broken data) reported. `page_enhance_scan` drives it headlessly. Not yet an option of Recognize text. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-07 (M10, `ocr.ocr-web`, port onto #977):** Web OCR rebuilt on main's engines and preprocessing (#977). `OcrJob::run` is now `OcrJob::steps` a page at a time: the page renders, crops to the region and cleans each page, a Web Worker runs ocrs on the result, and `OcrShot::place` maps the words back through the region and preprocessing. Every strategy reads like this when only one engine is present, and the web build has only ocrs. The downloaded models are installed into the engine cache (`ocr::install`), so the Verify screen and the engine-status checks see them too. The batch keeps main's skipped-files bucket, low-confidence notes and cancel. The translations moved to craft-i18n (storytold/craft-i18n#141). The Verify screen starts the model download itself and runs once the models land; before, on a fresh page it failed with the desktop's "set PDFCRAFT_MODELS" message. Known limits: the cleaning steps, and the Verify screen's Run OCR and re-reads, run on the page. Estimates unchanged.

- **2026-11-03 (M11, shared JBIG2 dictionary):** With Monochrome Images set to JBIG2, Optimize has up to 16 JBIG2 images share one symbol dictionary (`/JBIG2Globals`), the letters found on more than one page stored once, when that is smaller (`pdfcraft_filters::encode_jbig2_shared`). Eight pages of 10 pt text rendered at 300 dpi take 41% less in Helvetica and 42% in Times than coded alone, 10% in Courier; groups of 16 save 45%, of 32 only 47%. Only images redaction can store again without the dictionary share one (image masks and DeviceGray named directly, at most 64 Mi pixels), and redacting one stores the rest of its group so, which the group bounds to 15.

- **2026-11-03 (M5, #5850 review):** Resolve indirect IT when recognizing typewriter contents and Fill & Sign marks. Comment listing and property lookup retain their existing direct-Subtype behavior. A real double-click Edit composer preserves the typewriter’s text as written; ordinary comment edits keep trimming. Regressions cover direct Subtype with indirect intent, save/reopen, raw posted contents and same-index comments on different pages. The existing live-movement test now commits its setup with an outside click rather than Enter. Estimates unchanged.

- **2026-11-03 (M5, #5850):** Fill & Sign Enter inserts a line break in a growing editor; clicking outside commits and Escape cancels. Nonempty text keeps leading, internal and trailing line breaks. The headless fill_sign_add path sizes its typewriter box for the same wrapped lines so saved appearances show every row. Production-order keyboard and engine save/reopen/render regressions exercise the UI and headless callers. Estimates unchanged.

- **2026-11-03 (community, M7, Thai word breaking):** Thai rows end between words, found with ICU4X's dictionary line segmenter (`icu_segmenter` 2.3, compiled dictionary data, no LSTM model; Unicode-3.0), instead of only at spaces when rewrapping and at any cluster in Add text. A row never starts with a following vowel, tone or other mark, Mai Yamok or Paiyannoi, nor ends with a leading vowel; a word wider than the row still breaks between clusters, and the spaces where an Add text row ends are dropped as in Latin text. Rewrapping existing text works out the breaks once per run of non-space characters. Rewriting a paragraph into Thai still needs a source font with the Thai glyphs (there is no Thai fallback face for it yet). The compiled dictionaries (Thai, Lao, Khmer, Burmese) add about 1.8 MB to a stripped release binary. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-11-03 (M7, #5835):** In image crop mode, a right-click on the image, its crop box or its handles keeps the crop and opens no menu, so the menu no longer switches as the pointer moves. A right-click elsewhere clears the selection and opens the page menu. UI tests cover all four page rotations, overlapping images, a 45° image, and a check that the Done/Cancel/Remove Crop pills at a page edge still keep the crop.

- **2026-11-03 (M14 docs, #5805):** `docs/localization-parity.md` and the Languages table here count every catalog with `cargo xtask i18n-report`: the share of the 2,897 strings in its denominator, on `14ae839a`. The catalog-union measure it replaces had grown to 2,895 keys and left four catalogs at "100%" that were 44 keys short. Hindi, Indonesian and Korean are listed with their catalogs (97.5% each) instead of 0, and Thai, Lao and Central Kurdish join the table. The ≈ 68% localization score is not re-scored.

- **2026-11-02 (M10, #5743):** macOS print jobs name their title and owner (`com.apple.print.JobInfo.PMJobName`, `PMJobOwner`), as the system print dialog does, because a Kyocera filter crashes on a job without them. Checked by a test and through `doc_print` against a stand-in `lp`; not yet on a physical printer.

- **2026-11-02 (M3, #5708):** Pages shorter than the window sit in its middle, one page or row at a time and when scrolling continuously with every page in view.
- **2026-11-02 (M3, #5716):** A snapshot is drawn at 300 ppi, or at the zoom if that is finer, so one taken zoomed out stays readable; an area too large for one 4096-pixel tile comes out as fine as a tile allows (about 147 ppi for 2000 pt). A choice of resolution and a selection that stays active remain.
- **2026-11-02 (M14, #5737, #5460):** Preferences can turn off the update check at start. It stays on by default, and Help ▸ Check for updates still asks.

- **2026-11-01 (M7, #5657):** Header/footer, watermark and background additions and replacements stage the complete call before committing. Synthetic later-page failures preserve exact incremental bytes, object count, dirty state and allocation cursor; success controls retain other marks and shared/inherited resources. Estimates unchanged.

- **2026-11-01 (M14, #5572):** Preferences ▸ Documents and view sets how many recent files to keep, from 0 to 50 with 12 by default. At 0 Home has no Recent section and File ▸ Open Recent is disabled. The list is cut when Preferences closes, so the number can pass through 0 on its way to another.

- **2026-11-01 (M5/M14, customizable hotbar, freehand highlight, and moveable navigation rail):** Added Freehand-Highlight (`/Subtype /Ink`, `/IT /FreehandHighlight`, Multiply blending, 100 pt max line width) inside the standard Highlight flyout and headless `comment_add`; added a customizable floating or side-docked hotbar with a full-side chrome strip mode, settings gear icon anchored at the bar end, and 24–64 px sizing; and added Left/Right docking for the navigation rail (`rail_side`). Focused unit and integration tests pass across `annot`, `automation`, and `ui-egui`. Estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-01: PR #2155 further review preserves inherited Form text state and soft-mask chronology during Arrange, composes crop-group moves in the proper scope, and uses affine/press-relative image resize plus a shared rotation pivot. No-op script buttons preserve hidden source text; paste coordinates, visible selection ownership, and Snapshot/Export permissions have dedicated regressions. Candidate only; estimates unchanged.

- 2026-11-01: PR #2155 latest follow-up handles explicit image-paste events without release-time guesses, refreshes the font-size field when a selection becomes mixed, and specifies mixed-script normalization with exact Undo restoration. Candidate only; estimates unchanged.

- 2026-11-01: PR #2155 review follow-up preserves editor crops through group moves and keeps arranged artwork outside added-item streams; unsafe nested-tag extraction is refused. Image handles and flips follow page orientation, added images accept general transforms, and clipboard/text/Home/font-discovery edge cases have focused regressions. Candidate only; estimates unchanged.
- 2026-11-01: PR #2155 maintainer edits remove redundant image/text helpers and add regressions for crop ownership, arrange scope/state fidelity, clipboard precedence, crop-drag bounds, and font scanning. Transform and Unicode-script tools complete the headless paths.
- **2026-10-31 (M3/M4, #5330):** The document quick toolbar offers Crop pages directly through the existing page.crop command, with the command’s assembly-permission check and an active-tool highlight. The existing crop, undo and headless page_set_box paths are unchanged. No toolbar customization or new shortcut. Estimates unchanged.

- **2026-10-30 (M6):** Button scripts can read and change the normal push-button caption with `buttonGetCaption` and `buttonSetCaption`. The caption is drawn and retained through undo/redo and save/reopen; unsupported face indices are refused. Estimates unchanged.
- **2026-10-18 (M3, #1354, local candidate):** Integrated --two-paged with current main, retaining upstream modal/picker ownership and single-tab reuse. Native launch/opening/password regressions, negative checks against the old wrapper, visual inspection, formatting, strict scoped Clippy, layers, assets, parity and scoped WASM compilation pass. Three existing socket tests are unavailable in this workspace; one existing network test remains ignored. No push or GitHub merge; estimates unchanged.
- **2026-10-31 (M2, PR #4172 review):** Added same-machine pixel oracles for GBK Song, Hei, Kai and FangSong, including all four subset-prefixed Founder aliases. Reference PDFs use explicit Unicode mappings and independently selected installed faces; font data and rasters remain in memory. All eight cases ran; wrong-glyph and wrong-family production mutations each failed the new assertion and were restored. Validation: 422 renderer unit tests, renderer all-target Clippy with -D warnings, formatting, layers/assets/parity and renderer WASM checks passed; existing optional-patch/WASM warnings and parity notes remain. Estimates unchanged.

- **2026-10-31 (M2, PR #4172):** Integrated main fa5f9f8c, retaining both roadmap histories and raster regression groups. Restored display_config and thumbnail_config imports after the upstream explicit-import migration. Validation: 422 renderer unit tests, engine/UI all-target checks, scoped all-target Clippy with -D warnings, formatting, layers, assets, parity and renderer/engine WASM checks passed. Existing optional-patch/WASM warnings and parity notes remain; no full engine/UI runtime test rerun. Display-only font policy and estimates unchanged.
